# Treats Rule-Mining Campaign Log

Append-only laboratory record for the campaign ending
2026-08-13 07:00 Asia/Seoul.

## Campaign Contract

- Started: 2026-08-12 09:46:01 Asia/Seoul.
- Deadline: 2026-08-13 07:00:00 Asia/Seoul.
- Continue until the deadline; a rule count is not a completion criterion.
- Spend no more than 25% of active work on threshold-only tuning within
  existing rule families.
- Prioritize distinct mechanisms: pathways, gene interactions, anatomy,
  phenotypes, ontology structure, provenance, and negative evidence.
- Keep source-exclusive TTD target pairs blind. Search artifacts may expose
  training-hit and DRH-validation-hit booleans only; blind and unlabeled pairs
  must remain indistinguishable.
- When network access fails, continue with local Neo4j, source files, caches,
  code, and results. Record the interruption only if it blocks a family.
- For each hypothesis record the executable query, metrics, examples,
  ablations, failure mode, and graph limitation.

## Initial Hypothesis Queue

### H09-PATHWAY-01: Disease-specific pathway convergence

Hypothesis: a drug is more likely to treat a disease when it decreases a gene
that participates in a compact process supported independently by the disease
or its phenotypes, especially when a second causal disease gene participates
in the same process.

Planned ablations:

- Direct disease-process support only.
- Shared process with a second causal gene only.
- Both independent disease-process and second-driver support.
- Process degree and provenance constraints.

Known risk: previous shared-process rules were corroborative but not selective;
the new test must require independent disease/process support rather than only
GOA co-membership.

### H09-INTERACTION-01: Curated molecular propagation

Hypothesis: a drug can treat a disease by decreasing a target that is connected
to a distinct causal driver through a curated physical or signed regulatory
edge, when target and driver also share independent pathway and tissue context.

Planned ablations:

- Physical interaction versus signed positive regulation.
- One versus multiple interaction sources or publications.
- Shared compact process.
- Shared specific anatomy.
- Driver disease-relative evidence rank.

Known risk: prior SIGNOR expansion produced low precision and drug-specific
fanout.

### H09-ANATOMY-01: Multi-branch tissue concordance

Hypothesis: target inhibition is more predictive when the target is expressed
in multiple disease-relevant, non-ubiquitous anatomies and at least one disease
phenotype localizes to the same anatomy.

Planned ablations:

- One versus multiple shared anatomies.
- Disease-location and gene-expression degree constraints.
- Phenotype-localized anatomy branch.
- Independent disease-anatomy publication support.

### H09-PHENOTYPE-01: Cross-representation phenotype support

Hypothesis: phenotype evidence can become useful without an external EFO-HPO
mapping by joining gene and disease phenotypes through graph-native ontology,
shared anatomy, shared process, or common ancestor structure.

Planned ablations:

- Exact phenotype.
- Bounded ontology ancestor.
- Shared anatomy.
- Shared genetically associated process.

Known risk: disease phenotypes are HPO while gene-phenotype associations are
predominantly EFO.

### H09-ONTOLOGY-01: Mechanism-preserving disease-family transfer

Hypothesis: a known non-blind indication can transfer to an unlabeled disease
when both diseases share the same inhibited causal target and a specific
ontology ancestor, rather than merely lying within three subclass steps.

Planned ablations:

- Lowest common ancestor specificity.
- Shared phenotype and anatomy under the ancestor.
- Same causal target versus interacting causal module.
- Exclude generic disease ancestors and broad poisoning nodes.

### H09-PROVENANCE-01: Independent evidence architecture

Hypothesis: source independence and provenance topology are more predictive
than raw evidence counts, particularly when binding, perturbation,
gene-disease, anatomy, and translational assertions come from non-overlapping
primary resources.

Planned ablations:

- Number of source domains.
- Source entropy and single-source concentration.
- SemMed-only versus non-SemMed corroboration.
- Independent molecular, genetic, anatomical, and clinical channels.

### H09-NEGATIVE-01: Contradiction and adverse-evidence gating

Hypothesis: explicit contraindications, adverse phenotype overlap, disease-like
effects, and mechanism-direction conflicts can remove false positives from
otherwise useful rules without sacrificing validation recall.

Planned ablations:

- Exact contraindication or causes/exacerbates edge.
- Shared drug-side-effect / disease-phenotype node.
- Shared adverse anatomy.
- Drug decreases a gene whose reduced activity is associated with the disease.
- Negative evidence weighted as a gate versus a score.

## Probe Failure: 2026-08-12 10:02 Asia/Seoul

The first all-family coverage run completed the direct-context, physical
propagation, signed propagation, multi-target convergence, and phenotype
inventory queries in memory, then stalled in `phenotype_bridge_coverage`.

Failed executable shape:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(disease:Disease)
MATCH (disease)-[:HAS_PHENOTYPE]->(disease_phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
MATCH (gene)-[:GENE_ASSOCIATED_WITH_CONDITION]->
      (gene_phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
...
```

Failure: the query materialized the disease-phenotype by gene-phenotype
cross-product before testing bridges. It remained active for about two minutes
and was interrupted rather than consume the campaign window. No result was
interpreted.

Correction: deduplicate the direct drug-gene-disease paths first and use
pair-level `EXISTS` subqueries for exact, ontology, anatomy, and process
bridges.

The separate ontology probe failed immediately with a Cypher syntax error at a
`CASE WHEN EXISTS {...} OR EXISTS {...}` expression. Correction: parenthesize
the disjunction inside `CASE WHEN (...)`.

Runner limitation: the initial runner wrote output only after every probe
completed, so the successful in-memory results were lost when the phenotype
probe was interrupted. Correction: persist the report after each named probe
and record per-probe Neo4j failures explicitly before moving on.

## Coverage Pass 1: 2026-08-12 10:01 Asia/Seoul

Executable artifact:
`src/miner2049er/campaign_probes.py`; machine-readable output:
`results/campaign-probes.json`.

All counts below are graph coverage diagnostics, not rule metrics. The base
shape is a drug that directly binds and decreases a gene with
`MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE` to a MONDO disease. No TTD label was inspected.

### Direct mechanistic context

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(disease:Disease)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND disease.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
...
```

Metrics: 1,998 distinct drug-gene-disease rows and 1,790 drug-disease
candidates. Of those rows, 295 had a direct disease-process bridge, 1,408 had
a shared causal process, 282 had both, 997 had at least two Bgee anatomy
overlaps, and 183 had phenotype-supported anatomy. No row had the existing
phenotype-process feature.

Interpretation: pathway and anatomy context are common enough for nested
mechanistic rules. The zero phenotype-process count is a graph-feature
limitation and should not be treated as biological negative evidence.

### Physical interaction propagation

Executable shape:

```cypher
(drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target)
(drug)-[:AFFECTS {direction: decreased}]->(target)
(target)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]-(driver)
(driver)-[:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(disease)
```

Metrics: 26,786 target-driver paths, 10,478 drug-disease candidates, 501 drugs,
and 370 diseases. All retained interaction rows had SIGNOR provenance; 8,165
rows shared a process with at most 100 gene members and 14,128 had a
disease-localized Bgee anatomy with disease-location degree at most 50.

Interpretation: propagation is not coverage-limited. Its raw search space is
about six times the direct candidate space, so independent context is required
to suppress false positives.

### Signed interaction propagation

Executable shape:

```cypher
(drug)-[:AFFECTS {direction: decreased}]->(target)
(target)-[:REGULATES {direction: upregulated}]->(driver)
(driver)-[:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(disease)
```

Metrics: 15,039 paths, 6,534 drug-disease candidates, 385 drugs, and 304
diseases. All rows had SIGNOR provenance, 8,434 involved a top-three disease
driver, and 3,635 shared a compact process.

Interpretation: directionality alone is not selective. This family must be
tested as a contextual module rule rather than as the previously weak bare
three-hop rule.

### Multi-target convergence

Executable shape:

```cypher
(drug)-[:AFFECTS {direction: decreased}]->(gene1)
(gene1)-[:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(disease)
(drug)-[:AFFECTS {direction: decreased}]->(gene2)
(gene2)-[:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(disease)
```

Metrics: 257 gene-pair rows representing 173 drug-disease candidates, 71 drugs,
and 29 diseases. There were 141 rows where both genes shared a process with at
most 100 members and 43 where the genes directly interacted.

Interpretation: this is a genuinely narrower non-linear family. It supports
testing multi-target causal modules separately from single-target rank
thresholds.

### Phenotype representation inventory

Disease `HAS_PHENOTYPE` edges: 258,699 edges from 10,704 diseases to 11,461
objects, with HP and MONDO object prefixes.

Gene `GENE_ASSOCIATED_WITH_CONDITION` phenotype edges: 160,818 edges from
16,001 genes to only 112 objects, with EFO, HP, NCIT, and UMLS prefixes.

Gene condition-to-disease edges: 36,577 edges from 12,970 genes to only 79
disease objects, with MONDO, EFO, and UMLS prefixes.

Graph limitation: the gene phenotype branch has many assertions but extremely
low object diversity. Any phenotype rule must report whether apparent support
comes from a handful of broad phenotype hubs.

### Negative evidence

Among the 1,998 base rows, 128 had a direct adverse edge from the drug to the
disease, 161 shared a drug side-effect/disease phenotype, 162 had an opposite
drug-to-gene increase assertion, and 474 involved an ontology-hub disease with
descendant degree above 500.

Interpretation: explicit and structural negative gates have enough coverage
for ablation. They must be evaluated as exclusions from otherwise viable
families, not as stand-alone predictors.

### Failure: ontology transfer aggregation

The ontology-transfer probe still failed before execution:

```text
Invalid input 'WHEN': expected an expression, ')' or ','
```

The disjunction was parenthesized, but Neo4j still rejected aggregation
directly around the `EXISTS` expression. Correction: compute each `EXISTS`
flag in a `WITH` clause and aggregate only the resulting booleans.

## Probe Failure: 2026-08-12 10:03 Asia/Seoul

The row-level ontology-transfer rewrite parsed through the original failure but
then stopped at:

```cypher
coalesce(ancestor._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) BETWEEN 2 AND 200
```

Neo4j reported `Invalid input 'BETWEEN'`. No ontology metrics were produced.
Correction: use explicit `>= 2` and `<= 200` predicates. This is a query
compatibility failure, not evidence against the ontology hypothesis.

## Phenotype Bridge Result: 2026-08-12 10:04 Asia/Seoul

The corrected pair-level query completed in 78.79 seconds:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(disease:Disease)
...
WITH DISTINCT subject, gene, disease
RETURN count(*) AS base_path_rows,
       sum(CASE WHEN EXISTS {
         MATCH (disease)-[:HAS_PHENOTYPE]->(phenotype)
               <-[:GENE_ASSOCIATED_WITH_CONDITION]-(gene)
       } THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS exact_rows,
       ...
```

Metrics: 1,998 base paths and 1,790 drug-disease pairs; 0 exact phenotype
bridges, 2 bounded ontology bridges, 0 shared-anatomy bridges, and 0
shared-process bridges.

Conclusion: cross-representation phenotype support is falsified as a primary
candidate generator on this snapshot. The only nonzero branch covers 0.10% of
base paths and is too sparse to justify threshold search. A phenotype branch
will still be evaluated once as a rare corroborator, but further effort moves
to mechanisms with usable coverage.

Graph limitation: disease phenotypes have broad HPO diversity, while gene
condition assertions collapse onto only 112 phenotype objects and do not join
meaningfully through the available graph-native anatomy or process topology.

## Ontology Probe Cost Failure: 2026-08-12 10:08 Asia/Seoul

After both syntax corrections, the broad ontology-transfer query ran for more
than three minutes without returning and was manually interrupted. It joined
every non-blind treated anchor to every candidate disease sharing an inhibited
causal target, then tested multiple variable-length ontology paths.

No metrics were interpreted. The hypothesis remains open, but the executable
form is not campaign-viable.

Correction: start from the much smaller non-blind treatment anchor set, use
one-hop sibling or bounded direct-lineage patterns as indexed joins, and avoid
testing several variable-length alternatives inside aggregate `EXISTS`
subqueries.

Invocation:

.venv/bin/python -m miner2049er.rule_evaluator ...

The command exited successfully without evaluating anything because
`rule_evaluator.py` exposes a console entry point but has no `__main__` guard.

`results/runs/20260812T011522Z/summary.json`.

The evaluator used training and source-exclusive Drug Repurposing Hub labels.
TTD-only labels remained collapsed with unresolved candidates and were not
inspected.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision | Runtime |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R094 | disease-anchored causal pathway module | 97 | 10 | 0 | 10.31% | 0.00% | 7.69s |
| R095 | compact dual-target causal module | 129 | 16 | 2 | 13.95% | 1.77% | 1.59s |
| R096 | interacting dual-target causal module | 41 | 11 | 1 | 29.27% | 3.33% | 0.51s |
| R097 | physical propagation plus pathway and tissue | 1,399 | 64 | 3 | 4.79% | 0.22% | 157.03s |
| R098 | signed propagation plus pathway and tissue | 817 | 50 | 3 | 6.49% | 0.39% | 24.75s |
| R099 | phenotype-localized tissue pathway | 3 | 1 | 0 | 33.33% | 0.00% | 1.22s |
| R100 | independent molecular/genetic provenance | 201 | 40 | 1 | 20.40% | 0.62% | 0.55s |
| R101 | direct pathway and tissue concordance | 105 | 26 | 0 | 24.76% | 0.00% | 0.11s |
| R102 | R101 plus contradiction gates | 97 | 25 | 0 | 25.77% | 0.00% | 0.17s |
| R103 | specific sibling indication transfer | 75 | 33 | 0 | 44.00% | 0.00% | 2.38s |
| R104 | bounded direct-lineage indication transfer | 198 | 59 | 1 | 30.30% | 0.72% | 7.36s |
| R105 | ontology phenotype corroboration | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% | 7.72s |

Interpretation by family:

- Multi-target convergence is the strongest genuinely new treatment-free
  mechanism. Direct interaction raises visible precision from 13.95% to 29.27%
  while reducing 129 candidates to 41. The compact-process branch retains two
  DRH hits, so the two branches are complementary rather than a simple strict
  nesting.
- Adding signed direction to interaction propagation improves visible and DRH
  precision relative to unsigned propagation while returning fewer candidates,
  but both remain broad and expensive. They need structural specificity, not
  another bare interaction threshold.
- Independent provenance reaches 20.40% visible precision with 201 candidates,
  showing that evidence architecture has useful signal without treatment
  edges.
- Negative evidence removes eight candidates and one training hit from R101.
  Visible precision rises from 24.76% to 25.77%, but recall falls and neither
  version hits DRH. The gate is useful but not sufficient to rescue this family.
- Immediate sibling transfer is precise on visible labels but has no DRH hit.
  Bounded lineage gains one DRH hit and substantially more recall. Ontology
  transfer remains plausible but is vulnerable to broad parent and ancestor
  nodes.
- Phenotype-localized anatomy is too sparse, and direct phenotype ontology
  corroboration is entirely unlabeled. These branches are not candidates for
  further parameter search.

### Pareto recomputation

The global visible surface remained 32 rules and the global DRH surface
remained 7 rules; treatment-oriented evidence still dominates both.

The treatment-free visible surface remained 11 entries but changed
substantially. New entries were R096, R097, R098, R100, R101, and R102. They
displaced old R015, R017, R031, R037, R052, and R054. Thus six new mechanisms
dominated six prior treatment-free surface entries even though the surface
cardinality did not change.

The treatment-free DRH surface grew from 6 to 7 entries because R095 added a
new precision/recall tradeoff without displacing an old entry.

### Visible validation examples

- R095: Eslicarbazepine-epilepsy and Neratinib-breast cancer.
- R096: Neratinib-breast cancer.
- R098: Docetaxel-breast cancer, Neratinib-breast cancer, and
  Larotrectinib-myeloproliferative neoplasm.
- R100: Neratinib-breast cancer.
- R104: Neratinib-breast cancer.

Limitation: four of the five inspected families share the
Neratinib-breast-cancer DRH hit. The apparent cross-family validation is
therefore not independent and must not be counted as five biological
successes.

### Unlabeled-example audit

Potentially plausible examples include Quercetin-Alzheimer disease,
Lorlatinib-neuroblastoma, Entrectinib-neuroblastoma,
Ertugliflozin-familial renal glucosuria, and
Carbamazepine-Dravet syndrome. These remain predictions, not validated
discoveries.

The audit also exposed systematic ontology noise: predictions to
`MONDO:0000001` disease, `MONDO:0002254` syndrome, `MONDO:0004992` cancer, and
`MONDO:0005070` neoplasm. The next iteration will add a structural disease
specificity requirement and analyze the exact mechanism paths. This is an
ontology-quality correction motivated by examples, not a sweep over arbitrary
numeric cutoffs.

## Mechanism Path Audit: 2026-08-12 10:19 Asia/Seoul

### Disease specificity

Read-only Cypher:

```cypher
MATCH (d:Disease)
WHERE d.id IN [
  'MONDO:0000001', 'MONDO:0002254', 'MONDO:0004992', 'MONDO:0005070',
  'MONDO:0007254', 'MONDO:0004975', 'MONDO:0020076', 'MONDO:0100135'
]
RETURN d.id, d.name,
       d._miner_disease_descendant_degree,
       d._miner_disease_superclass_degree
```

The noisy nodes had 3,873 to 37,824 descendants: disease 37,824; syndrome
3,873; cancer 13,345; neoplasm 14,301. In contrast, breast cancer had 342,
myeloproliferative neoplasm 162, Alzheimer disease 21, and Dravet syndrome 0
materialized direct descendants. Requiring at most 500 descendants and at
least one parent cleanly separates these examples.

### Dual-target validation paths

The R095 path extraction returned:

- Eslicarbazepine-epilepsy through SCN1A/SCN2A/SCN8A. Several target pairs
  shared `GO:0086002` cardiac muscle cell action potential involved in
  contraction; SCN1A/SCN8A also shared membrane depolarization during action
  potential.
- Neratinib-breast cancer through EGFR and ERBB2 in `GO:0038134`
  ERBB2-EGFR signaling pathway.

The epilepsy example demonstrates that a compact shared process is not
necessarily disease-specific. This motivated a disease-process ablation, but
the cardiac process itself is also a warning that process labels require
qualitative inspection.

### Signed propagation validation paths

The R098 detail query returned:

- Docetaxel and Neratinib: EGFR upregulates ERBB2 via SIGNOR; both genes share
  ERBB2-EGFR signaling and are Bgee-expressed in mammary duct and mammary-gland
  epithelium; ERBB2 is the top-ranked breast-cancer driver.
- Larotrectinib: ABL1 upregulates JAK2 via SIGNOR; both share actin filament
  polymerization and platelet-derived growth factor receptor signaling, with
  Bgee expression in bone-marrow cells and granulocytes; JAK2 is the top-ranked
  myeloproliferative-neoplasm driver.

These are coherent multi-hop mechanisms. They justify retaining the family
even though its candidate set remains noisy.

### Provenance validation path

Neratinib-breast cancer was supported through ERBB2 with six binding sources
(SIGNOR, GtoPdb, DrugCentral, DGIdb, ChEMBL, BindingDB), one non-SemMed
gene-disease source, four direct relation types, 13 causal publications, NPMI
0.500, and disease rank 1. An EGFR path had seven binding sources but failed
the final provenance rule because its gene-disease evidence had no non-SemMed
source and only two relation types.

This confirms that R100 selects the ERBB2 path rather than merely counting all
Neratinib target edges.

### Ontology-transfer validation path

R104 predicted Neratinib-breast cancer from a training treatment edge to
`MONDO:0004992` cancer supplied by Multiomics Drug Approvals. Breast cancer is
a descendant of cancer within two steps, and both EGFR and ERBB2 support the
anchor and candidate.

The mechanism is coherent but the anchor is extremely broad. Candidate
specificity should be required; anchor specificity would remove this
validation example and is deferred as a later ablation.

## Evaluation Batch 2: 2026-08-12 10:25 Asia/Seoul

Verbatim executable queries and hypotheses are in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`,
rules `TREAT-I09-R106` through `TREAT-I09-R116`. Run artifact:
`results/runs/20260812T012439Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism/refinement | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision | Runtime |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R106 | disease-anchored compact dual targets | 7 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% | 0.46s |
| R107 | ontology-specific interacting dual targets | 18 | 1 | 1 | 11.11% | 5.88% | 0.32s |
| R108 | ontology-specific signed tissue propagation | 372 | 18 | 3 | 5.65% | 0.85% | 2.84s |
| R109 | R108 with top-three disease driver | 264 | 11 | 3 | 5.30% | 1.19% | 1.33s |
| R110 | ontology-specific independent provenance | 186 | 39 | 1 | 21.51% | 0.68% | 0.40s |
| R111 | ontology-specific direct-lineage transfer | 131 | 52 | 1 | 40.46% | 1.27% | 7.14s |
| R112 | disease-anchored interacting dual targets | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% | 1.85s |
| R113 | anatomy-localized dual-target module | 30 | 5 | 2 | 23.33% | 8.00% | 3.99s |
| R114 | R108 plus contradiction gates | 317 | 10 | 3 | 4.10% | 0.98% | 161.10s |
| R115 | provenance plus process and anatomy | 3 | 1 | 0 | 33.33% | 0.00% | 0.10s |
| R116 | disease-anchored signed propagation | 9 | 1 | 0 | 11.11% | 0.00% | 0.65s |

Findings:

- R113 is the strongest new validation tradeoff. Tissue colocalization reduces
  R095 from 129 to 30 candidates while retaining both DRH hits, raising DRH
  precision from 1.77% to 8.00%.
- R107 retains the interacting dual-target DRH hit but removes 23 of 41
  candidates. Its visible precision falls because most removed broad-class
  outputs were labeled training pairs, illustrating that ontology hubs inflate
  apparent visible precision.
- R109 retains all three signed-propagation DRH hits while removing 108
  candidates from R108. The driver-rank constraint improves DRH precision but
  slightly reduces visible precision.
- R110 adds one treatment-free visible Pareto entry and improves visible
  precision over R100 from 20.40% to 21.51%.
- R111 improves ontology-transfer visible precision from 30.30% to 40.46% and
  DRH precision from 0.72% to 1.27% while retaining its validation hit.
- Disease-associated process edges are not useful as hard gates in these
  families. R106, R112, and R116 retain 7, 1, and 9 candidates respectively,
  with only one training hit across all three and no DRH hits.
- Negative gates remove 55 signed-propagation candidates and eight training
  hits while preserving all three DRH hits. DRH precision rises modestly, but
  visible precision falls and runtime increases from 2.84 seconds to 161
  seconds. The current query shape is too costly for routine use.
- Cross-channel provenance plus both process and anatomy is overconstrained:
  only three candidates and no DRH hit.

### Pareto recomputation

Global surfaces remained unchanged at 32 visible entries and 7 DRH entries.

The treatment-free visible surface grew from 11 to 12 entries because R110
added a new provenance/disease-specificity tradeoff.

The treatment-free DRH surface is now 6 entries. R113 dominates the prior R095
multi-target point and also removes the older R052 anatomy point from that
surface. Surface cardinality fell, but the surviving frontier improved.

## R113 Example Audit: 2026-08-12 10:29 Asia/Seoul

R113 retained the same two DRH examples as R095:
Eslicarbazepine-epilepsy and Neratinib-breast cancer.

The optimized detail query anchored the two pairs before expanding paths:

```cypher
UNWIND [
  ['CHEBI:174358', 'MONDO:0005027'],
  ['CHEBI:61397', 'MONDO:0007254']
] AS pair
MATCH (subject:Drug {id: pair[0]}), (disease:Disease {id: pair[1]})
...
```

Neratinib was supported by EGFR/ERBB2, ERBB2-EGFR signaling, mammary duct, and
mammary-gland epithelium.

Eslicarbazepine was supported by SCN1A/SCN2A/SCN8A expressed in cingulate
cortex, hypothalamus, neocortex, occipital lobe, parietal lobe, and frontal
lobe. The shared process branch still included the misleading cardiac
action-potential label, but the independent brain-anatomy branch makes the
overall non-linear rule coherent.

Unlabeled R113 examples included Erlotinib-, Dacomitinib-, Afatinib-,
Osimertinib-, Tucatinib-, and other kinase-inhibitor predictions for breast
cancer; Quercetin- and Methylthioninium-Alzheimer disease;
Ketoconazole- and Posaconazole-congenital adrenal hyperplasia; and
Cannabidiol-, Riluzole-, and Lidocaine-epilepsy.

Lidocaine-epilepsy has a graph contraindication and is therefore an explicit
false-positive warning for the ungated rule. Methylthioninium-poisoning is
another semantic warning: the disease-specificity degree filter does not
remove every broad or non-therapeutic disease concept.

The original unanchored path-detail query ran for more than 90 seconds and was
interrupted. The `UNWIND`-anchored rewrite completed in under two seconds.
Diagnostic query planning is therefore a documented performance limitation.

## New Derived Feature: Chemical-class specificity

Coverage probes found 68,196 outgoing Drug `SUBCLASS_OF` edges and 61,466,176
immediate sibling paths, representing 3,990,006 drug pairs across 1,208
classes. Unqualified shared-class transfer is therefore unusably broad.

Materialized query:

```cypher
MATCH (drug:Drug)-[:SUBCLASS_OF]->(chemical_class)
WITH chemical_class, count(DISTINCT drug) AS degree
SET chemical_class._miner_drug_member_degree = degree
RETURN count(chemical_class), min(degree),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.5),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.9),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.99), max(degree)
```

The feature covered 1,972 classes: minimum 1, median 2, p90 30, p99 1,165, and
maximum 1,998 drugs. The reproducible feature definition and clear/index
handling were added to `src/miner2049er/rule_features.py`.

## Evaluation Batch 3: 2026-08-12 10:32 Asia/Seoul

This batch tested a genuinely different family: transferring an indication
between drugs through a shared directional target mechanism. Verbatim queries
are R117-R122 in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`. Run artifact:
`results/runs/20260812T013221Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Peer-transfer mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision | Runtime |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R117 | one shared inhibited causal target | 458 | 82 | 4 | 18.78% | 1.06% | 1.58s |
| R118 | R117 plus specific chemical class | 89 | 16 | 2 | 20.22% | 2.74% | 4.17s |
| R119 | two shared inhibited causal targets | 28 | 13 | 1 | 50.00% | 6.67% | 1.60s |
| R120 | distinct interacting causal targets | 7 | 2 | 0 | 28.57% | 0.00% | 24.80s |
| R121 | R120 plus shared disease anatomy | 7 | 2 | 0 | 28.57% | 0.00% | 25.13s |
| R122 | R117 plus contradiction gates | 351 | 65 | 2 | 19.09% | 0.70% | 4.51s |

Findings:

- Shared identical targets provide usable signal but do not dominate the
  existing treatment-oriented global surfaces.
- Chemical-class specificity removes 369 candidates while retaining two of
  four DRH hits. It improves DRH precision but loses half the validation
  recall.
- Requiring two shared targets is the best peer-transfer point: 50% visible
  precision and 6.67% DRH precision with 14 visible labels among 28 candidates.
- Transfer across distinct interacting targets produces only seven candidates
  and no DRH hit. Adding tissue localization changes nothing, so anatomy is
  redundant within that tiny branch.
- Contradiction gates remove 107 candidates, 17 training hits, and two DRH
  hits. They slightly improve visible precision but materially worsen DRH
  performance. Negative evidence is not uniformly reliable as a hard gate.

Pareto recomputation: no global or treatment-free surface changed. The
peer-transfer family remains a useful secondary library family, especially
R119, but does not currently dominate an existing point.

## Directionality and Variant-Evidence Audit: 2026-08-12 10:35 Asia/Seoul

Hypothesis: gene-to-disease edges may carry directional qualifiers that
distinguish disease-driving increases from decreases and therefore resolve the
central ambiguity in target-inhibition rules.

Executable bounded audit:

```cypher
MATCH (gene:Gene)-[relation:AFFECTS|CAUSES|CONTRIBUTES_TO|REGULATES]->(object)
WHERE object:Disease OR object:PhenotypicFeature
RETURN type(relation), relation.qualified_predicate,
       relation.object_aspect_qualifier,
       relation.object_direction_qualifier, count(*)
ORDER BY count(*) DESC
```

The bounded gene-to-disease inventory contained 2,389 `CAUSES`, 1,752
`AFFECTS`, and 173 `CONTRIBUTES_TO` edges. Gene-to-phenotype contained 589
`AFFECTS`, 273 `CAUSES`, and 49 `CONTRIBUTES_TO` edges. None carried usable
object-direction qualifiers. The two disease-to-gene `AFFECTS` edges were also
unqualified.

Four unbounded qualifier scans across all relationship types ran for more than
90 seconds in parallel and were interrupted. This is both a query-planning
failure and a graph limitation: relationship-type-specific scans are required
for practical diagnostics.

`SequenceVariant` node count was zero, and no variant or mutation node labels
were present. Variant-mediated gain-of-function/loss-of-function rules are
therefore unavailable in this graph build.

Positive and negative gene-disease correlation edges were extremely sparse
(five positive and three negative disease edges, seven positive and two
negative phenotype edges), concentrated in ICEES, and did not overlap the
direct drug-target mechanisms. They are not viable directional evidence.

Conclusion: disease-driving direction is absent. This is a first-order graph
gap rather than a threshold problem.

## New Derived Feature: Phenotype Specificity

Materialized query:

```cypher
MATCH (disease:Disease)-[:HAS_PHENOTYPE]->(phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
WITH phenotype, count(DISTINCT disease) AS degree
SET phenotype._miner_disease_member_degree = degree
RETURN count(phenotype), min(degree),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.5),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.9),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.99), max(degree)
```

The feature covered 9,473 phenotypes: minimum 1, median 3, p90 34, p99 260,
and maximum 2,237 diseases. The feature definition and clear/index handling
were added to `src/miner2049er/rule_features.py`.

## Evaluation Batch 4: Phenotype Transfer

Verbatim executable rules are R123-R126 in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`. Run
artifact: `results/runs/20260812T014016Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R123 | treated anchor shares specific phenotype | 27 | 8 | 0 | 29.63% | 0.00% |
| R124 | at least two shared phenotypes | 12 | 6 | 0 | 50.00% | 0.00% |
| R125 | phenotype plus shared anatomy | 2 | 2 | 0 | 100.00% | 0.00% |
| R126 | shared causal target plus phenotype | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% |

Coverage probes found 47 specific shared-phenotype anchor paths representing
29 candidate pairs; only 14 paths had multiple phenotypes. Adding a shared
causal target left one pair and adding shared anatomy left two.

R125 entered the global visible Pareto surface, increasing its cardinality
from 32 to 33, but it has no unresolved candidates and therefore cannot make a
new prediction. The validation surface remained at seven entries. Phenotype
transfer is corroborative and training-heavy, not a useful generator in this
graph.

## Evaluation Batch 5: Direct Process, Activity, and Pathway Perturbation

Verbatim executable rules are R127-R130 in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`. Run
artifact: `results/runs/20260812T014426Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision | Contraindicated |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R127 | decrease compact disease process | 7,967 | 311 | 7 | 3.99% | 0.091% | 326 |
| R128 | decrease disease-associated molecular activity | 1,468 | 19 | 0 | 1.29% | 0.00% | not retained |
| R129 | decrease disease-associated pathway | 616 | 25 | 1 | 4.22% | 0.169% | not retained |
| R130 | process decrease plus inhibited causal target | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% | 1 |

Inventory queries found 19,087 drug-to-biological-process `AFFECTS` edges with
decreased direction, 1,201 decreased molecular-activity edges, and 89
decreased pathway edges. The corresponding disease-association joins yielded
14,810 direct-process pairs, 1,468 molecular-activity pairs, and 616 pathway
pairs before the compact-process constraints.

The direct process rule is high coverage but noisy and produces 326 explicit
contraindications. Requiring a directly inhibited causal gene in the same
process collapses coverage to three pairs and does not recover a visible
treatment. Direct process direction cannot substitute for missing
disease-process direction.

Pareto recomputation after batches 4 and 5: global visible surface 33, global
validation surface 7, treatment-free visible surface 12, and treatment-free
validation surface 6. Only R125 changed a surface, and it has no prediction
utility.

## Hypothesis Family: Tissue Exposure

Hypothesis: a target mechanism is more plausible when drug localization,
target expression, and disease location coincide in the same anatomy.

Inventory:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:LOCATED_IN]->(anatomy:AnatomicalEntity)
MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (gene)-[:EXPRESSED_IN]->(anatomy)
MATCH (object)-[:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->(anatomy)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN count(*) AS paths,
       count(DISTINCT [subject.id, object.id]) AS pairs
```

The unranked join contained 1,788 paths and 297 pairs. The top-three
disease-gene rank restriction retained 471 paths and 155 pairs. Ten pairs had
at least two distinct targets, seven of which used multiple anatomies.

All 22,544 drug `LOCATED_IN` edges are SemMedDB-derived. They may reflect
literature mention rather than measured exposure, so the base rule receives a
medium mechanism score despite its non-linear structure.

Multi-target examples included Amiodarone-arrhythmia through SCN5A/KCNH2 in
heart, Cannabidiol/Phenytoin-epilepsy through SCN2A/SCN8A in brain, and
Imatinib-CML through ABL1/BCR in blood and bone marrow. Lidocaine-epilepsy is a
known counterexample and must remain in the failure audit.

Executable rules R131-R132 were appended to `rules/campaign-i09.toml`.

## Hypothesis Family: Active Metabolite

Hypothesis: a parent drug may treat a disease through a distinct active
metabolite that directly inhibits a causal disease gene.

Inventory:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:HAS_METABOLITE]->(metabolite:ChemicalEntity)
MATCH (metabolite)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (metabolite)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE subject <> metabolite
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN count(*) AS paths,
       count(DISTINCT [subject.id, object.id]) AS pairs
```

`HAS_METABOLITE` covers 164 parent drugs and 798 small-molecule metabolites.
The mechanism join yielded 44 paths and 43 pairs across only three distinct
parents after excluding self-loops. Without `subject <> metabolite`, misleading
Fenofibrate-to-Fenofibrate and Haloperidol-to-Haloperidol paths appeared.

Requiring the parent to independently inhibit the same gene left three
Oxcarbazepine pairs through Eslicarbazepine: epilepsy through SCN8A, and Dravet
syndrome and familial hemiplegic migraine through SCN1A. Transfer through an
interacting but distinct parent target had zero coverage.

Executable rules R133-R134 were appended to `rules/campaign-i09.toml`.

The original four-way parallel tissue/metabolite probe returned output larger
than the conversation context and was truncated. Compact one-query-at-a-time
aggregates recovered the results. This was a tooling output-volume failure,
not a graph or network blocker.

Verbatim executable rules are R131-R134 in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`. Run
artifact: `results/runs/20260812T015127Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision | Contraindicated |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|

| R132 | R131 with two targets | 10 | 3 | 0 | 30.00% | 0.00% | 1 |
| R133 | distinct active metabolite | 43 | 17 | 0 | 39.53% | 0.00% | 2 |
| R134 | parent and metabolite same target | 3 | 1 | 0 | 33.33% | 0.00% | 0 |

R131 is contraindication-heavy and does not validate. R132 is coherent but too
small and still has no DRH hit. R133 is the strongest visible point and enters

has no DRH hit. R134 leaves two unresolved/blind pairs and one training pair.

Global surface recomputation from `results/rule-library.json`: 33 visible, 7
validation, 11 treatment-free visible, and 7 treatment-free validation. These
counts correct the earlier stale 12/6 treatment-free count; the rule library
recomputes from the latest completed result for every catalog rule.

## New Derived Features: Side-effect Specificity and Profile Size

Materialized queries:

```cypher
MATCH (drug:Drug)-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]->(phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
WITH phenotype, count(DISTINCT drug) AS degree
SET phenotype._miner_side_effect_drug_degree = degree
RETURN count(phenotype), min(degree),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.5),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.9),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.99), max(degree)
```

```cypher
MATCH (drug:Drug)-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]->(phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
WITH drug, count(DISTINCT phenotype) AS degree
SET drug._miner_side_effect_profile_degree = degree
RETURN count(drug), min(degree),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.5),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.9),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.99), max(degree)
```

Side-effect coverage is 49,928 edges across 1,012 drugs and 1,350 phenotypes.
Phenotype drug degree has minimum 1, median 4, p90 96, p99 505, maximum 868.
Drug profile degree has minimum 1, median 36, p90 111, p99 216, maximum 363.
Both features, clear handling, and range indexes were added to
`src/miner2049er/rule_features.py`.

## Hypothesis Family: Side-effect Pharmacological Fingerprints

Hypothesis: uncommon side effects that cover a large fraction of two drugs'
bounded adverse-event profiles can fingerprint shared pharmacology and support
indication transfer from a non-blind treated peer.

Naive executable probe:

```cypher
MATCH (peer:Drug)-[treatment:TREATS|AMELIORATES_CONDITION]->(object:Disease)
WHERE any(source IN coalesce(treatment.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
          WHERE NOT source IN
            ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd'])
MATCH (peer)-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]->(phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
      <-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]-(subject:Drug)
WHERE subject <> peer
  AND phenotype._miner_side_effect_drug_degree <= 96
WITH subject, peer, object, count(DISTINCT phenotype) AS shared
RETURN count(*), count(DISTINCT [subject.id, object.id]),
       percentileDisc(shared, 0.5), percentileDisc(shared, 0.9), max(shared)
```

Even after excluding the broadest 10% of phenotypes, the naive join generated
2,804,982 peer-indication rows and 856,193 distinct drug-disease candidates.
It is unusable and was not promoted.

Profile-level executable definition:

```cypher
MATCH (peer:Drug)-[treatment:TREATS|AMELIORATES_CONDITION]->(object:Disease)
WHERE any(source IN coalesce(treatment.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
          WHERE NOT source IN
            ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd'])
  AND peer._miner_side_effect_profile_degree >= 3
  AND peer._miner_side_effect_profile_degree <= 111
MATCH (peer)-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]->(phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
      <-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]-(subject:Drug)
WHERE subject <> peer
  AND subject._miner_side_effect_profile_degree >= 3
  AND subject._miner_side_effect_profile_degree <= 111
  AND phenotype._miner_side_effect_drug_degree <= 96
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
WITH subject, peer, object, count(DISTINCT phenotype) AS shared,
     CASE WHEN subject._miner_side_effect_profile_degree <
               peer._miner_side_effect_profile_degree
          THEN subject._miner_side_effect_profile_degree
          ELSE peer._miner_side_effect_profile_degree END AS smaller
WHERE shared >= 3 AND 2 * shared >= smaller
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

This structural redefinition reduced coverage to 1,154 peer-indication rows
and 722 candidate pairs. Shared counts ranged from 3 to 10 with median 5.

A first attempt used SQL-style `BETWEEN`, which Neo4j rejected; the valid query
uses explicit lower and upper comparisons.

A shared directly inhibited causal disease target had zero coverage, proving
that this family is distinct from target-mediated peer transfer. Excluding
exact candidate-side-effect/disease-phenotype overlap retained 689 pairs.
Requiring a shared immediate chemical class of at most 30 drugs retained 69
pairs.

The chemical-class corroboration query initially exceeded Neo4j's 1.4 GiB
transaction memory limit while three large probes ran concurrently. Rerunning
it alone completed in 10.4 seconds. This is a concurrency/resource blocker,
not a failed biological hypothesis.

Executable rules R135-R137 were appended to `rules/campaign-i09.toml`.

## Evaluation Batch 7: Side-effect Pharmacological Fingerprints

Verbatim executable rules are R135-R137 in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`. Run
artifact: `results/runs/20260812T020033Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision | Contraindicated |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R135 | profile indication transfer | 722 | 76 | 4 | 11.08% | 0.62% | 6 |
| R136 | R135 without disease mimicry | 689 | 65 | 4 | 10.01% | 0.64% | 6 |
| R137 | R135 plus chemical class | 69 | 36 | 1 | 53.62% | 3.03% | 1 |

No global surface changed. The negative phenotype-overlap gate retained all
four DRH hits but removed eleven training hits, so visible precision worsened
while DRH precision improved only marginally. Chemical class produces a strong
visible precision gain but loses three DRH hits.

The four R135/R136 DRH examples were Amcinonide-dermatitis, clobetasone
butyrate-psoriasis, Medrysone-conjunctivitis, and Emedastine-conjunctivitis.
R137 retained only Amcinonide-dermatitis.

Path audit:

- Amcinonide shared four uncommon side effects with treated Fluticasone and
  several fluorinated-steroid classes.
- Clobetasone butyrate shared five corticosteroid adverse effects with
  Dexamethasone, Hydrocortisone, Methylprednisolone, Prednisone, and
  Triamcinolone.
- Medrysone shared five or six ocular adverse effects with Loteprednol and
  Prednisolone.
- Emedastine shared conjunctival hyperemia, ocular pruritus, and eye irritation
  with treated Besifloxacin.

The held-out examples are coherent class or administration-context analogs,
but the rule remains indirect and reporting-sensitive.

## Hypothesis Family: Prodrug and Active Moiety

Hypothesis: a prodrug can inherit either an indication or a directional
causal-target mechanism from its active moiety.

Relation audit:

```cypher
MATCH (active:Drug)-[conversion:DERIVES_FROM]->(subject:Drug)
WHERE subject.chembl_prodrug = 'True'
RETURN subject.id, subject.name, active.id, active.name,
       conversion.primary_knowledge_sources, conversion.publications
ORDER BY subject.name, active.name
```

The graph contains 142 drugs with `chembl_prodrug = 'True'`, but only 13
plausible active-to-prodrug `DERIVES_FROM` pairs. Examples include
fluorouracil-to-capecitabine/tegafur, dopamine-to-levodopa,
prednisolone-to-prednisone, acyclovir-to-valaciclovir, mycophenolic
acid-to-mycophenolate mofetil, and mercaptopurine-to-azathioprine. Every
conversion edge is from SemMedDB.

`HAS_PART` was rejected for this purpose: only 117 Drug-to-ChemicalEntity edges
exist, and examples are dominated by generic meronymy such as metabolite,
amino-acid, and nucleic-acid components.

Active-to-prodrug indication transfer:

```cypher
MATCH (active:Drug)-[:DERIVES_FROM]->(subject:Drug)
WHERE subject.chembl_prodrug = 'True'
MATCH (active)-[treatment:TREATS|AMELIORATES_CONDITION]->(object:Disease)
WHERE any(source IN coalesce(treatment.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
          WHERE NOT source IN
            ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd'])
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

With ontology specificity constraints this yielded 242 pairs across ten
prodrugs and nine active drugs. The reverse derivative direction yielded 30
pairs but included incoherent testosterone-to-pregnenolone/progesterone
transfers and was rejected.

Treatment-free active-moiety target transfer yielded 40 pairs across three
prodrugs and three active drugs: prednisolone-to-prednisone, dopamine-to-
levodopa, and mycophenolic-acid-to-mycophenolate-mofetil. Requiring the active
drug also to treat the same disease left 15 prednisone pairs.

Executable rules R138-R140 were appended to `rules/campaign-i09.toml`.

## Evaluation Batch 8: Prodrug and Active Moiety

Verbatim executable rules are R138-R140 in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`. Run
artifact: `results/runs/20260812T020440Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision | Contraindicated |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R138 | active indication to prodrug | 242 | 127 | 1 | 52.89% | 0.87% | 4 |
| R139 | active target mechanism to prodrug | 40 | 17 | 0 | 42.50% | 0.00% | 2 |
| R140 | treatment and target convergence | 15 | 14 | 0 | 93.33% | 0.00% | 0 |

R138 entered the global visible surface and reduced its cardinality from 33 to
32 by dominating an older point. R139 entered the treatment-free visible
surface. Validation surfaces remained unchanged.

The sole R138 DRH hit was prednisone-congenital adrenal hyperplasia. R140's
only unresolved non-blind candidate was prednisone-pulmonary edema. That
candidate is a warning that very high visible precision in a small,
treatment-conditioned cohort can still produce a biologically dubious novel
prediction.

## Hypothesis Family: Path-disjoint Provenance in a Dual-target Module

Hypothesis: source independence should be represented as evidence topology
across a nonlinear module, not only as a source-count threshold on one edge.

Base query:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene1:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect1:AFFECTS]->(gene1)
MATCH (gene1)-[evidence1:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene2:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect2:AFFECTS]->(gene2)
MATCH (gene2)-[evidence2:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
MATCH (gene1)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)<-[:INVOLVED_IN]-(gene2)
WHERE gene1.id < gene2.id
  AND effect1.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect1.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect1.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND effect2.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect2.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect2.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND process._miner_gene_member_degree <= 100
  AND evidence1._miner_non_semmed_primary_source_count >= 1
  AND evidence2._miner_non_semmed_primary_source_count >= 1
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

The base non-SemMed dual-target topology yielded 22 pairs. Requiring both
manual-agent and automated-agent binding evidence somewhere across the module
retained 13. Requiring both agent types independently for each target retained
six.

The strict six-pair subset was coherent: Erdafitinib, Futibatinib,
Infigratinib, Nintedanib, Pemigatinib, and Ponatinib inhibited FGFR2/FGFR3 in
compact bone-development processes for craniosynostosis. The broader subset
also included Methylthioninium-Alzheimer/dementia through APP/MAPT and
Lidocaine-epilepsy through sodium-channel pairs, preserving known warning
examples.

Executable rules R141-R143 were appended to `rules/campaign-i09.toml`.

## Evaluation Batch 9: Path-disjoint Provenance

Verbatim executable rules are R141-R143 in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`. Run
artifact: `results/runs/20260812T021200Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision | Contraindicated |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R141 | non-SemMed genetics for both targets | 22 | 4 | 1 | 22.73% | 5.56% | 1 |
| R142 | manual and automated agents across module | 13 | 1 | 0 | 7.69% | 0.00% | 1 |
| R143 | both agents for each target | 6 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% | 0 |

No surface changed. R141's sole DRH hit was Eslicarbazepine-epilepsy. Agent-type
heterogeneity was not a monotonic precision signal: it removed the validation
hit and most training hits. R143's six FGFR-inhibitor craniosynostosis pairs
remain mechanistically coherent but entirely unlabeled predictions.

## Blocked Family: Preclinical Translation

Inventory query:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[trial:IN_PRECLINICAL_TRIALS_FOR]->(object:Disease)
RETURN trial.primary_knowledge_sources, count(*),
       count(DISTINCT subject), count(DISTINCT object)
```

Seventeen of nineteen preclinical edges were TTD-sourced and the other two
were Drug Repurposing Hub-sourced. Using either would expose a blind or visible
validation partition as a feature. No training-safe preclinical rule exists,
so the family was abandoned without inspecting pair identities.

## Blocked Family: Cell-type Mechanisms

Coverage inventory:

- Bgee supplies 217,441 `Gene EXPRESSED_IN Cell` edges across 39,716 genes but
  only 41 cell types.
- Drug-to-cell: 4,353 `LOCATED_IN`, 2,280 `AFFECTS`, and 753 `DISRUPTS`, all
  SemMedDB.
- Disease-to-cell: 1,667 `LOCATED_IN` and 243 `DISRUPTS`, all SemMedDB.
- Phenotype-to-cell: 79 `LOCATED_IN`, all SemMedDB.

Executable base probe:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:LOCATED_IN]->(cell:Cell)
MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (gene)-[expression:EXPRESSED_IN]->(cell)
MATCH (object)-[:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->(cell)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND 'infores:bgee' IN coalesce(expression.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

The base left only Imatinib-acute-lymphoblastic-leukemia and
Imatinib-BCR-ABL1-positive-CML in bone-marrow cells. Replacing disease
localization with `DISRUPTS`, requiring drug `AFFECTS` the same cell, or
localizing a disease phenotype to the cell all produced zero pairs. This
family adds no discovery coverage and was not promoted to the catalog.

Transporter, uptake, secretion, and catalytic relationship types are absent;
`HAS_METABOLITE` is the only metabolism-specific relation available.

## Hypothesis Family: Comorbidity-constrained Molecular Transfer

Hypothesis: indication transfer between clinically coexisting diseases may be
credible when both diseases share the drug's inhibited causal target.

Base query:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[treatment:TREATS|AMELIORATES_CONDITION]->(anchor:Disease)
WHERE any(source IN coalesce(treatment.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
          WHERE NOT source IN
            ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd'])
MATCH (anchor)-[:COEXISTS_WITH]-(object:Disease)
MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(anchor)
MATCH (gene)-[:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
WHERE object <> anchor
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

All 42,751 disease-to-disease coexistence edges are SemMedDB-derived. The base
rule yielded 261 candidates across 56 drugs and 84 diseases. Requiring two
shared inhibited causal genes left ten candidates; requiring a specific shared
phenotype yielded zero.

All ten dual-target candidates were EGFR-family inhibitors transferred from a
broad cancer anchor to breast cancer through EGFR/ERBB2. The family therefore
overlaps prior ontology-transfer behavior rather than contributing genuinely
new biology.

Executable rules R144-R145 were appended to `rules/campaign-i09.toml` for
complete evaluation, but the family is marked low priority.

## Evaluation Batch 10: Comorbidity-constrained Transfer

Verbatim executable rules are R144-R145 in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`. Run
artifact: `results/runs/20260812T022411Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision | Contraindicated |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R144 | coexistence plus one shared target | 261 | 65 | 1 | 25.29% | 0.51% | 9 |
| R145 | coexistence plus two shared targets | 10 | 1 | 1 | 20.00% | 11.11% | 0 |

No surface changed. R145's sole DRH hit was Neratinib-breast cancer. The high
validation precision reflects a tiny EGFR/ERBB2 cancer-transfer cohort rather
than a broad comorbidity mechanism.

## Blocked Family: Opposite Disease and Drug Process Direction

Hypothesis: a drug that decreases a process increased by disease, or increases
a process decreased by disease, would provide an explicit corrective rule.

Bounded inventory:

```cypher
MATCH (disease:Disease)-[relation:AFFECTS]->(process)
WHERE process:BiologicalProcess
   OR process:MolecularActivity
   OR process:Pathway
RETURN labels(process), relation.qualified_predicate,
       relation.object_aspect_qualifier,
       relation.object_direction_qualifier,
       relation.primary_knowledge_sources, count(*)
```

The graph contains 4,473 disease-to-biological-process and 115
disease-to-molecular-activity `AFFECTS` edges, all SemMedDB-derived and all
without qualified predicate, aspect, or direction. An all-relation inventory
ran for more than 90 seconds and was interrupted. The bounded evidence is
sufficient to show that corrective process direction is unavailable.

## New Derived Feature: Cellular-component Compactness

Materialized query:

```cypher
MATCH (gene:Gene)-[:PART_OF]->(component:CellularComponent)
WITH component, count(DISTINCT gene) AS degree
SET component._miner_component_gene_member_degree = degree
RETURN count(component), min(degree),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.5),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.9),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.99), max(degree)
```

The feature covers 1,118 components: minimum 1, median 11, p90 54, p99 323,
maximum 2,607 genes. The reproducible feature, cleanup handling, and range
index were added to `src/miner2049er/rule_features.py`.

The first p90 probes were launched concurrently with materialization and
returned zero because the property was not yet present. Rerunning the unchanged
queries after materialization recovered the results. This was an execution
dependency error, not a biological failure.

## Hypothesis Family: Cellular-component Causal Modules

Hypothesis: a compact GO cellular component can represent a molecular assembly
in which inhibition of one or two causal genes produces a disease-relevant
mechanism beyond pairwise interaction.

Base query:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene1:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene1)
MATCH (gene1)-[:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (gene1)-[:PART_OF]->(component:CellularComponent)
      <-[:PART_OF]-(gene2:Gene)
MATCH (gene2)-[:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
WHERE gene2 <> gene1
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND component._miner_component_gene_member_degree <= 54
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

The single-inhibition/second-driver variant yielded nine pairs; requiring
direct binding and decrease of both genes yielded six. Requiring a direct
GeneticsKP disease-component association had zero overlap.

Every surviving pair involved neonatal diabetes mellitus, KCNJ11/ABCC8, and
the 12-gene inward-rectifying-potassium-channel component. Single-target
predictions covered Chlorpropamide, Diazoxide, Gliclazide, Glipizide,
Gliquidone, Glyburide, Repaglinide, Tolazamide, and Tolbutamide. Six drugs
directly inhibited both genes.

Executable rules R146-R147 were appended to `rules/campaign-i09.toml`.

## Evaluation Batch 11: Cellular-component Modules

Verbatim executable rules are R146-R147 in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`. Run
artifact: `results/runs/20260812T023022Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R146 | one inhibited member plus second causal member | 9 | 1 | 0 | 11.11% | 0.00% |
| R147 | both causal component members inhibited | 6 | 1 | 0 | 16.67% | 0.00% |

Neither rule changed a surface. The family is retained because its unlabeled
predictions form a coherent, high-mechanism neonatal-diabetes cohort, not
because it validated in DRH.

## Blocked Family: Cross-species Ortholog Phenotypes

Panther supplies 150,252 orthology paths, including 24,907 explicit
human-to-mouse paths across 18,051 human and 18,860 mouse genes.

Executable probe:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(human_gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(human_gene)
MATCH (human_gene)-[:ORTHOLOGOUS_TO]-(mouse_gene:Gene)
MATCH (mouse_gene)-[:GENE_ASSOCIATED_WITH_CONDITION]->
      (phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
MATCH (object:Disease)-[:HAS_PHENOTYPE]->(phenotype)
WHERE 'NCBITaxon:9606' IN coalesce(human_gene.in_taxon, [])
  AND 'NCBITaxon:10090' IN coalesce(mouse_gene.in_taxon, [])
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

The exact phenotype join, a causal mouse-gene phenotype join, and a mouse-gene
disease-association join all yielded zero. GeneticsKP's 160,048
gene-phenotype edges attach almost entirely to human-taxoned genes and only 112
phenotype nodes. The limitation is missing species-resolved phenotype evidence,
not missing orthologs.

## Hypothesis Family: Signed GO Causal Propagation

GOA supplies 584 gene-to-process positive-effect assertions and 206
negative-effect assertions across the signed upstream predicates. These differ
from the earlier SIGNOR gene-to-gene rules by propagating through GO processes.

Positive propagation probe:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[:ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_POSITIVE_EFFECT|
                ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_OR_WITHIN_POSITIVE_EFFECT]->
      (process:BiologicalProcess)
MATCH (driver:Gene)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)
MATCH (driver)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE target <> driver
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND process._miner_gene_member_degree <= 100
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

This yielded ten pairs. Tissue colocalization retained nine, while a direct
disease-process genetic association had zero overlap. The positive cohort was
dominated by HDAC2 inhibitors propagated to UBE3A through progesterone-receptor
signaling in Angelman syndrome; Sunitinib-PRKAA1/2-PNPLA2-Dorfman-Chanarin
disease supplied the other mechanism.

The negative-effect counterpart yielded six top-driver pairs: DPP9 inhibitors
Alogliptin, Saxagliptin, and Vildagliptin releasing pyroptosis linked to
familial Mediterranean fever or severe congenital neutropenia. This is a
plausible counter-mechanism and is evaluated diagnostically, not intended as a
treatment rule.

Executable rules R148-R150 were appended to `rules/campaign-i09.toml`.

## Evaluation Batch 12: Signed GO Propagation

Verbatim executable rules are R148-R150 in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`. Run
artifact: `results/runs/20260812T024023Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R148 | positive-effect process propagation | 10 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% |
| R149 | R148 plus tissue localization | 9 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% |
| R150 | negative-effect release counter-mechanism | 6 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% |

No surface changed. R148/R149 remain high-mechanism unlabeled prediction
cohorts. R150 was changed to library status `rejected` after evaluation because
its signed path predicts process release rather than treatment. The absence of
explicit graph contraindications does not make its sign therapeutically
positive.

## New Derived Feature: Gene-family Compactness

Panther supplies 55,096 `Gene-MEMBER_OF-GeneFamily` edges. A degree inventory
found 7,439 families with minimum 1, median 3, p90 14, p99 61, and maximum 531
distinct gene members.

Materialized query:

```cypher
MATCH (gene:Gene)-[:MEMBER_OF]->(family:GeneFamily)
WITH family, count(DISTINCT gene) AS degree
SET family._miner_gene_family_member_degree = degree
RETURN count(family), min(degree),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.5),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.9),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.99), max(degree)
```

The reproducible feature, cleanup handling, and range index were added to
`src/miner2049er/rule_features.py`.

## Hypothesis Family: Causal Paralogs in Compact Gene Families

Hypothesis: a drug target may substitute for a distinct causal disease driver
when both genes belong to a compact Panther family. Shared compact processes,
affected-tissue expression, or direct target-driver interaction were tested as
independent context constraints.

Base executable query:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[:MEMBER_OF]->(family:GeneFamily)
      <-[:MEMBER_OF]-(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE target <> driver
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND family._miner_gene_family_member_degree <= 14
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

This returned 632 paths, 159 distinct drug-disease pairs, and 18 families. The
unrestricted family query had 38,430 paths, 2,873 pairs, and 88 families.

Process-convergent executable addition:

```cypher
MATCH (target)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process:BiologicalProcess)
      <-[:INVOLVED_IN]-(driver)
WHERE process._miner_gene_member_degree <= 100
```

Together with the complete base query, this returned 2,963 paths, 115 pairs,
13 families, and 62 processes. The unrestricted shared-process form had
16,011 paths and 1,394 pairs.

Anatomy-convergent executable addition:

```cypher
MATCH (target)-[target_expression:EXPRESSED_IN]->
      (anatomy:AnatomicalEntity)
      <-[driver_expression:EXPRESSED_IN]-(driver)
MATCH (object)-[:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->(anatomy)
WHERE 'infores:bgee' IN
      coalesce(target_expression.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:bgee' IN
      coalesce(driver_expression.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
```

Together with the complete base query, this returned 734 paths, 67 pairs, six
families, and 13 anatomies.

Interaction-convergent executable addition:

```cypher
MATCH (target)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]-(driver)
```

Together with the complete base query, this returned 150 paths and 12 pairs,
all from one family.

The first raw example audits for the process and anatomy variants exceeded the
available tool output context and were truncated. This was an audit-output
failure, not a query failure. Grouping by drug, disease, target, driver, and
family and collecting at most five contexts made the examples inspectable.

Representative process cohorts:

- ACE inhibitors targeting ACE mapped to ACE2-driven COVID-19 through four
  renin-angiotensin and vascular processes.
- JAK1/JAK3/TYK2 inhibitors mapped to JAK2-driven acquired polycythemia vera
  through compact JAK-STAT processes.
- Hydroxychloroquine or imiquimod targeting TLR7/8 mapped to TLR9-driven
  excess-LMW-DNA disease through endolysosomal Toll-like-receptor signaling.
- Curcumin targeting EP300 mapped to CREBBP-driven Rubinstein-Taybi syndrome.
- Tazemetostat targeting EZH1 mapped to EZH2-driven Weaver syndrome.

Representative anatomy cohorts were dominated by JAK-family targets and
JAK2-driven acquired polycythemia vera in bone marrow. The ACE/ACE2 COVID-19
cohort localized through testis, which demonstrates that shared expression and
disease anatomy can be technically true but therapeutically uninformative.

All 12 direct-interaction pairs were inhibitors of JAK1 mapped through JAK3 to
severe combined immunodeficiency: Abrocitinib, Baricitinib, Fedratinib,
Ibrutinib, Momelotinib, Pacritinib, Ponatinib, Ritlecitinib, Ruxolitinib,
Sunitinib, Tofacitinib, and Upadacitinib. This is countermechanistic: suppressing
a related JAK does not repair JAK3 deficiency and may worsen immune signaling.
The interaction variant is therefore a negative diagnostic unless evaluation
provides strong contrary evidence.

Verbatim executable rules R151-R154 were added to
`rules/campaign-i09.toml`. The family remains mechanistically ambiguous because
paralog similarity does not encode whether the causal driver is excessive,
deficient, or compensated by the target.

## Evaluation Batch 13: Compact Gene-family Substitution

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T042031Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Context | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision | Contraindicated |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R151 | compact family | 159 | 7 | 0 | 4.40% | 0.00% | 2 |
| R152 | family plus compact process | 115 | 7 | 0 | 6.09% | 0.00% | 1 |
| R153 | family plus affected anatomy | 67 | 6 | 0 | 8.96% | 0.00% | 0 |
| R154 | family plus direct interaction | 12 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% | 0 |

None entered the global or treatment-free visible or validation Pareto surfaces.
The surfaces remained 32 global visible, seven global validation, 11
treatment-free visible, and seven treatment-free validation rules.

The process and anatomy ablations remove 27.7% and 57.9% of base candidates but
do not preserve additional labels or recover a DRH hit. R154's absence of
contraindication labels is not positive evidence: all 12 outputs are the
countermechanistic JAK1/JAK3 severe-immunodeficiency cohort described above.
R151-R154 were therefore marked `rejected`. No family-degree threshold tuning
was pursued.

## Untouched Predicate Inventory

Metadata and endpoint probes were used to identify mechanisms not represented
in the existing catalog:

- `HAS_SUBSTRATE`: 2,095 edges, of which 2,091 are ChEMBL; direction is
  gene/protein to chemical or drug and no edge carries activity qualifiers.
- `ACTIVE_IN`: 81,227 GOA gene-to-cellular-component edges.
- `COLOCALIZES_WITH`: 2,023 GOA gene-to-cellular-component edges.
- `PRECEDES`: 14,602 edges; 14,555 are SemMedDB and 47 are GO-CAM.
- `HAS_INPUT`: 30,832 edges; 30,830 are SemMedDB and two are GO-CAM.
- `PRODUCES`: 16,198 edges, all SemMedDB.
- `IS_INPUT_OF`: 917 GO-CAM edges.
- `HAS_CHEMICAL_ROLE`: only 24 Drug Repurposing Hub edges.

The first combined endpoint-inventory query failed because expressions after
`UNION ALL` were not explicitly aliased:

```text
Expression in CALL { RETURN ... } must be aliased
```

The same inventory was rerun as bounded per-predicate queries and succeeded.
The chemical-role family was not considered further because it is both tiny and
validation-source-derived. SemMedDB `HAS_INPUT`, `PRODUCES`, and `PRECEDES`
were not promoted because their endpoint categories are heterogeneous and the
edges carry no direction or mechanistic qualifiers.

A broad wildcard GO-CAM gene-gene qualifier inventory scanned for more than
eight minutes and was interrupted. Restricting the relationship union to
`REGULATES|CAUSES|ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF|ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_NEGATIVE_EFFECT|PRECEDES|
IS_INPUT_OF` completed in nine seconds.

GO-CAM gene-gene topology includes 2,147 `REGULATES`, 860 `IS_INPUT_OF`, 602
`CAUSES`, 156 `ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF`, 34 `PRECEDES`, and 29
`ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_NEGATIVE_EFFECT` edges. All properties
`qualified_predicate`, `object_aspect_qualifier`, and
`object_direction_qualifier` are null on these edges.

## New Derived Feature: Active-component Compactness

Materialized query:

```cypher
MATCH (gene:Gene)-[:ACTIVE_IN]->(component:CellularComponent)
WITH component, count(DISTINCT gene) AS degree
SET component._miner_component_active_gene_degree = degree
RETURN count(component), min(degree),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.5),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.9),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.99), max(degree)
```

The feature covers 550 components: minimum 1, median 16, p90 185, p99 3,121,
and maximum 10,752 active genes. Its reproducible build, cleanup handling, and
range index were added to `src/miner2049er/rule_features.py`.

## Hypothesis Family: GOA Active-component Causal Modules

Hypothesis: `ACTIVE_IN` can localize the molecular activities of two causal
disease genes more specifically than generic `PART_OF` membership.

Base propagated probe:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[:ACTIVE_IN]->(component:CellularComponent)
      <-[:ACTIVE_IN]-(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE target <> driver
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND component._miner_component_active_gene_degree <= 185
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

Without compactness the motif produced 1,256,558 paths and 155,951 pairs.
The p90 component constraint reduced it to 9,134 paths and 1,912 pairs. This
variant remained too weak because the target need not itself be disease
relevant.

Requiring target and driver both to have derived causal disease evidence left
292 paths and 49 pairs. Requiring direct binding and decrease of both causal
genes left 1,329 paths but only 17 pairs, all in one component. Verbatim
executable rules R155-R156 are in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`.

The 17 dual-inhibition pairs were EGFR/ERBB2 inhibitors predicted for breast
cancer through the basal plasma membrane. The 49-pair form added
AURKA/BUB1/BUB1B aneuploidy, DMD/DES cardiomyopathy, and several broad
`poisoning` outputs. This family is coherent but heavily concentrated in one
well-known receptor module.

## Rejected Family: Compact Direct Drug Participation in PathBank

Hypothesis: a drug that is a direct participant in a compact pathway containing
a top-ranked causal driver may modulate the disease through pathway chemistry.

Executable probe:

```cypher
MATCH (pathway:Pathway)-[:HAS_PARTICIPANT]->(subject:Drug)
MATCH (pathway)-[:HAS_PARTICIPANT]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND COUNT { (pathway)-[:HAS_PARTICIPANT]->(:Gene) } <= 7
  AND COUNT { (pathway)-[:HAS_PARTICIPANT]->(:Drug) } <= 1
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

PathBank contains 30,192 pathways with gene participants (median and p90 both
seven genes) and 30,197 pathways with drug participants (median and p90 both
one drug). The compact query produced 11,519 path rows but collapsed to only
ten drug-disease pairs. Outputs were dominated by water, ATP, thousands of
near-duplicate lipid pathways, and drug-metabolism paths. Phenytoin-Brugada
syndrome is plausibly adverse rather than therapeutic, and Velmanase
alfa-angioedema arose through ACE-inhibitor metabolism paths unrelated to the
candidate drug. The family was rejected without catalog promotion or threshold
sweeps.

## Rejected Diagnostic: Causal-enzyme Substrate Exposure

Hypothesis: a drug that is both a ChEMBL substrate and an inhibitor of a causal
enzyme might modulate the corresponding disease.

Executable query is R159 in `rules/campaign-i09.toml`. It produced three pairs:
Doxorubicin-gout, Imatinib-gout, and Mitoxantrone-gout, all through ABCG2.
These are more naturally interpreted as transporter-mediated exposure or
toxicity signals than as gout treatments. R159 is therefore cataloged with
status `rejected` as graph-native negative evidence.

## Hypothesis Family: GO-CAM Causal Propagation

Direct executable query:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[step:CAUSES]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE target <> driver
  AND 'infores:go-cam' IN coalesce(step.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

This produced 601 paths and 215 pairs. Representative modules include
MDM2/HDAC1/SIRT1 to TP53 in cancers, GSK3B to EZH2 in Weaver syndrome, SIK2 to
EP300 in Rubinstein-Taybi syndrome, and HDAC6 to SQSTM1 in bone Paget disease.

Two-step executable query:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[step1:CAUSES]->(middle:Gene)
      -[step2:CAUSES]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE target <> middle AND middle <> driver AND target <> driver
  AND 'infores:go-cam' IN coalesce(step1.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:go-cam' IN coalesce(step2.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

This five-edge body produced 24 paths and 12 pairs. It contains
CDK1/DYRK2/MAPK1 to SUFU to GLI1/GLI2 for skin basal-cell carcinoma and AKT1
to GSK3B to EZH2 for Weaver syndrome.

Exact edge audit showed that every inspected causal step is manually asserted,
publication-backed `RO:0002630`, represented as `biolink:causes`. The edges
are knowledge assertions from a manual agent, but `RO:0002630` is unsigned.
MDM2-TP53 and SUFU-GLI illustrate why causal upstream cannot be assumed to mean
positive activation. An explicit GO-CAM negative-effect target-to-driver probe
had zero candidate pairs. R157-R158 are therefore evaluated as unsigned causal
hypotheses with medium rather than high mechanism scores.

## Evaluation Batch 14: Active Components, GO-CAM, and Substrate Diagnostic

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T054200Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R155 | two causal genes active in compact component | 49 | 1 | 2 | 6.12% | 4.17% |
| R156 | both causal active genes inhibited | 17 | 1 | 1 | 11.76% | 6.25% |
| R157 | direct unsigned GO-CAM causal propagation | 215 | 1 | 0 | 0.47% | 0.00% |
| R158 | two-step unsigned GO-CAM propagation | 12 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% |
| R159 | enzyme-substrate exposure diagnostic | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% |

No global or treatment-free surface changed: 32 global visible, seven global
validation, 11 treatment-free visible, and seven treatment-free validation.

R155's DRH hits were Docetaxel-breast-cancer and
Neratinib-breast-cancer; its training hit was Lapatinib-breast-cancer. R156
retained Neratinib and Lapatinib. The nonblind unlabeled R156 cohort contains
