15 additional EGFR/ERBB2 inhibitors for breast cancer, including Erlotinib,
Dacomitinib, Afatinib, Osimertinib, and Tucatinib. R155-R156 remain candidates
as an independently represented GOA mechanism, but R113 dominates both in
precision and recall.

R157-R158 were marked `rejected`: manual provenance did not compensate for
unsigned causal semantics, and neither produced a DRH hit. R159 remains
rejected as an exposure/toxicity diagnostic.

## Hypothesis Family: Disease-variant Direction

Gene2Phenotype source edges preserve a disease-mechanism field that was lost
from the derived `MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE` edge:

| `subject_form_or_variant_qualifier` | Source edges |
|---|---:|
| `loss_of_function_variant_form` | 2,011 |
| `genetic_variant_form` | 757 |
| `gain_of_function_variant_form` | 175 |
| `dominant_negative_variant_form` | 98 |
| `non_loss_of_function_variant_form` | 11 |

These are manual Gene2Phenotype assertions, often publication-backed. This
field addresses a central limitation of earlier target rules: a causal gene
does not imply that inhibiting that gene is therapeutic.

R160 executable query:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'gain_of_function_variant_form'
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

R161 uses the same query with
`subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = 'loss_of_function_variant_form'` and
`effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'increased'`. The matched adverse control
R162 uses loss-of-function with a `decreased` drug effect.

Signed propagation R164 executable query:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[regulation:REGULATES]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE target <> driver
  AND 'infores:signor' IN coalesce(regulation.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND regulation.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND regulation.object_direction_qualifier = 'upregulated'
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'gain_of_function_variant_form'
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

R166 substitutes a SIGNOR `downregulated` edge and a loss-of-function driver,
representing release of deficient activity. R163 and R168 are matched signed
countermechanisms: respectively, inhibition of a negative regulator of a
gain-of-function driver and inhibition of a positive regulator of a
loss-of-function driver. The complete executable forms are preserved in
`rules/campaign-i09.toml`.

Ablations of the signed corrective parents added independent biological
context rather than merely changing numeric cutoffs:

| Corrective parent | Parent pairs | Shared compact process | Shared disease anatomy | Process and anatomy |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| GOF positive-regulator suppression | 1,443 raw paths/pairs before final identity filters; 1,232 evaluated pairs | 389 | 95 | 23 |
| LOF negative-regulator release | 1,482 raw paths/pairs before final identity filters; 1,443 evaluated pairs | 203 | 76 | 6 |

All signed `REGULATES` matches were from SIGNOR. The GOF intersection was
dominated by ERBB2-to-EGFR lung-cancer mechanisms. The six LOF intersection
pairs were all SRC-to-PTEN mechanisms for Lhermitte-Duclos disease in
cerebellum: Dasatinib, Vandetanib, Adenosine, Crizotinib, Ponatinib, and
Bosutinib.

## Evaluation Batch 15: Directional Variant Mechanisms

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T055115Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R160 | direct inhibition of GOF gene | 525 | 9 | 0 | 1.71% | 0.00% |
| R161 | direct activation of LOF gene | 90 | 2 | 0 | 2.22% | 0.00% |
| R162 | LOF suppression countermechanism | 369 | 1 | 0 | 0.27% | 0.00% |
| R163 | GOF negative-regulator release countermechanism | 377 | 1 | 0 | 0.27% | 0.00% |
| R164 | signed upstream GOF suppression | 1,232 | 10 | 0 | 0.81% | 0.00% |
| R165 | R164 plus process and anatomy | 23 | 7 | 0 | 30.43% | 0.00% |
| R166 | signed release of LOF driver | 1,443 | 7 | 0 | 0.49% | 0.00% |
| R167 | R166 plus process and anatomy | 6 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% |
| R168 | LOF positive-regulator suppression countermechanism | 2,479 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% | 0.00% |

No surface changed: 32 global visible, seven global validation, 11
treatment-free visible, and seven treatment-free validation.

R160 training hits included approved lung-cancer kinase inhibitors and
Fx-1006A for hereditary systemic amyloidosis. Its unlabeled cohort contains
mechanistically plausible rare-disease candidates such as diltiazem for
CACNA1C gain-of-function disorders, sodium-channel inhibitors for primary
erythermalgia, and MEK inhibitors for cardiofaciocutaneous syndromes.

R161 recovered Eteplirsen and Viltolarsen for Duchenne muscular dystrophy.
Plausible unlabeled examples include vitamin A for a retinol-transport defect,
calcitriol or ergocalciferol for VDR-related rickets, dopamine for dopamine
transporter deficiency, and liothyronine for THRA-related hypothyroidism.
However, Erlotinib-JAK3-deficiency SCID and cocaine-SCN1A epilepsy show that
graph-level `increased` effects are context-insensitive and can be misleading.

R164 and R166 are rejected because their broad signed-regulator forms create
over one thousand pairs each, have no DRH hits, and are dominated by more
specific rules. R165 and R167 remain separate contextual hypotheses because
they require shared process and affected anatomy, not a threshold adjustment.
R160 and R161 remain high-mechanism candidates despite weak visible recovery.
R162, R163, and R168 remain explicit negative controls.

The visible DRH partition contains no exclusive hits for any of these rare
variant-direction rules. This is a benchmark coverage limitation: the
mechanism can generate credible rare-genetic-disease predictions while being
unmeasurable on DRH. TTD identities remain blind and were not inspected. The
R167 inspector output exceeded the terminal context limit, so examples were
recovered with the bounded aggregate probe above rather than from target-label
inspection.

## Transient Blocker: Neo4j Probe Approval Service

At 2026-08-12 14:55 KST, three read-only `docker exec ... cypher-shell`
requests for dominant-negative, directional convergence, anatomy/process, and
one-step ontology probes were rejected before execution because the approval
review service lost its network connection. This was not a Cypher failure and
did not change graph state. Per campaign protocol, the queries were not routed
through an untracked substitute; work moved to offline library and prediction
analysis, with the same direct probes queued for a later retry.

## Hypothesis Family: Phenotype-mimicry Negative Evidence

Hypothesis: a positive molecular rule should be discounted when a candidate
drug is known to reproduce a phenotype of the predicted disease. Five
executable ablations were added as R169-R173 in
`rules/campaign-i09.toml`.

R169 executable negative gate, appended to the complete R113 positive body:

```cypher
AND NOT EXISTS {
  MATCH (subject)-[:CONTRAINDICATED_IN|EXACERBATES_CONDITION|CAUSES]->(object)
}
AND NOT EXISTS {
  MATCH (subject)-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]->(:PhenotypicFeature)
        <-[:HAS_PHENOTYPE]-(object)
}
```

R170 replaces the exact phenotype gate with the following ontology-aware gate:

```cypher
AND NOT EXISTS {
  MATCH (subject)-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]->(side_effect:PhenotypicFeature)
  MATCH (object)-[:HAS_PHENOTYPE]->(disease_phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
  MATCH (side_effect)-[:SUBCLASS_OF*0..2]->(ancestor:PhenotypicFeature)
        <-[:SUBCLASS_OF*0..2]-(disease_phenotype)
  WHERE coalesce(ancestor._miner_phenotype_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
}
```

R171, R172, and R173 apply the R169 exact gate to R160 direct GOF
inhibition, R161 direct LOF activation, and R119 dual-shared-target peer
transfer, respectively. Their complete executable positive bodies and gates
are in the catalog.

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T121232Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Parent | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision | DRH precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R169 | R113 | 27 | 4 | 2 | 22.22% | 8.70% |
| R170 | R169 ontology-aware | 27 | 4 | 2 | 22.22% | 8.70% |
| R171 | R160 | 405 | 9 | 0 | 2.22% | 0.00% |
| R172 | R161 | 66 | 2 | 0 | 3.03% | 0.00% |
| R173 | R119 | 14 | 4 | 1 | 35.71% | 10.00% |

The global visible and validation surfaces remained 32 and seven. The
treatment-free visible surface remained 11. The treatment-free validation
surface increased from seven to eight distinct metric points/rules, with R169
and the metric-identical R170 both currently represented by the Pareto
implementation.

A new blind-safe executable comparator was added as
`miner-compare-rules`. It reports parent-only, child-only, and intersection
counts while combining TTD-only and unresolved pairs into one aggregate
bucket. Examples can be returned only for training, exclusive DRH, or pairs
with no target edge; TTD-only identities are never shown.

Parent-child audits explain the results:

- R169 removed three R113 pairs: one known treatment, Lamotrigine-epilepsy,
  and two unresolved-or-blind pairs. One removed pair had an explicit
  contraindication. Losing the established epilepsy treatment makes the gate
  slightly worse on visible precision despite improving DRH precision.
- R173 removed 14 of R119's 28 pairs, including nine known treatments:
  Sunitinib, Imatinib, Nilotinib, Regorafenib, and Pazopanib for Plexosarcoma;
  Imatinib, Dasatinib, and Nilotinib for BCR-ABL1-positive CML; and
  Lamotrigine for epilepsy. Disease-like adverse phenotypes are common for
  efficacious drugs and are not valid hard vetoes.
- R171 removed 120 unresolved-or-blind pairs while preserving all nine known
  R160 hits. However, removed unlabeled examples include Diltiazem-Timothy
  syndrome, sodium-channel inhibitors-primary erythermalgia, and
  Lacosamide-long QT syndrome 3. Several are mechanistically plausible, so
  improved lower-bound precision is not proof of better predictions.
- R172 removed 24 unresolved-or-blind pairs while preserving both known R161
  hits. Removed examples mix implausible candidates with plausible
  mechanism-based predictions, including Calcitriol-VDR rickets and
  Mexiletine-Brugada syndrome.
- R169 and R170 have identical aggregate sets. An explicit set comparison was
  stopped after 90 seconds because evaluating both ontology-heavy bodies was
  expensive and the aggregate equality already answered the ablation.

R170 is rejected as redundant and computationally inferior. R173 is rejected
because it discards nine established treatments and worsens visible
precision. R169 remains a treatment-free validation-surface rule, but its
mechanism is an unsafe hard veto and should be interpreted cautiously. R171
and R172 remain candidates as precision-oriented variants, not replacements
for the directional parents.

Infrastructure verification: catalog parsing found 80 unique rules; Ruff and
the comparator-specific tests passed. The full suite had 25 passing tests and
one stale assertion failure:
`test_iteration_8_rules_are_retained_and_on_the_visible_pareto_front` assumes
all 30 retained Iteration 8 parameter variants remain globally visible-Pareto,
which is no longer true after later rules expanded the front. The historical
test was not deleted or weakened.

## Hypothesis Family: Dominant-negative, Directional Convergence, and Ontology Broadening

The deferred Neo4j probes succeeded after the approval service recovered.
Seven executable rules R174-R180 were added to
`rules/campaign-i09.toml`.

R174 uses the direct R160/R161 target body with
`subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = 'dominant_negative_variant_form'` and a
`decreased` effect. R180 is its matched `increased` countermechanism. Because
the drug effect is not allele-specific, suppression may reduce both mutant and
wild-type products; R174 is medium mechanism rather than high.

R175 executable convergence body:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene1:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect1:AFFECTS]->(gene1)
MATCH (gene1)-[association1:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene2:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect2:AFFECTS]->(gene2)
MATCH (gene2)-[association2:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object)
WHERE gene1.id < gene2.id
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association1.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association2.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association1.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'gain_of_function_variant_form'
  AND association2.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'gain_of_function_variant_form'
  AND effect1.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect1.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect1.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND effect2.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect2.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect2.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Equivalent two-LOF and mixed GOF/LOF corrective convergence probes produced no
rows. R175 produced 12 pairs, all unlabeled. Eleven are FGFR inhibitors for
Pfeiffer syndrome; the twelfth is Glyburide-Cantu syndrome. These are coherent
high-mechanism predictions even though the visible target partitions do not
cover them.

R176 and R177 add this independently represented anatomy body to R160 and
R161:

```cypher
MATCH (gene)-[expression:EXPRESSED_IN]->(anatomy:AnatomicalEntity)
MATCH (object)-[:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->(anatomy)
WHERE 'infores:bgee' IN coalesce(expression.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
```

Biological-process anchoring using a compact gene process independently
associated with the same disease produced no GOF or LOF pairs.

R178 and R179 transfer the directional assertion to an immediate MONDO parent:

```cypher
MATCH (gene)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(anchor:Disease)
MATCH (anchor)-[:SUBCLASS_OF]->(object:Disease)
```

The corresponding child-transfer probes were rejected before cataloging:
3,314 GOF and 1,367 LOF pairs were generated, an obvious subtype fan-out.

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T121939Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R174 | dominant-negative suppression | 61 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% |
| R175 | dual GOF driver suppression | 12 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% |
| R176 | GOF suppression in affected anatomy | 48 | 8 | 0 | 0 | 16.67% |
| R177 | LOF activation in affected anatomy | 3 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 66.67% |
| R178 | GOF transfer to immediate parent | 1,634 | 19 | 3 | 19 | 1.35% |
| R179 | LOF transfer to immediate parent | 461 | 13 | 1 | 6 | 3.04% |
| R180 | dominant-negative activation control | 25 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% |

The global visible and validation surfaces remain 32 and seven. The
treatment-free validation surface returns to seven after R170 was rejected.
The treatment-free visible surface increases from 11 to 12 because R177 adds
a 66.67%-precision, two-hit point.

R177's known pairs are Eteplirsen and Viltolarsen for Duchenne muscular
dystrophy. Its sole truly unlabeled prediction is Metformin-Leigh syndrome.
This is a compact, high-mechanism rule, although the Metformin prediction
requires careful interpretation because a graph-level increase effect may not
restore the specific deficient gene in the relevant mitochondrial context.

R176 retains eight of R160's nine known hits. R174 has plausible unlabeled
examples including Plerixafor or Mavorixafor-WHIM syndrome 1 and sodium-channel
inhibitors-developmental epileptic encephalopathy 13, but no visible
calibration. R180 likewise has no visible hits, so the benchmark cannot
distinguish the dominant-negative hypothesis from its countermechanism.

R178's exclusive DRH hits are Tolbutamide-diabetes mellitus,
Chlorpropamide-diabetes mellitus, and Sorafenib-thyroid cancer. R179's is
Ergocalciferol-hypocalcemic rickets. These demonstrate that ontology
broadening can recover common labels. However, unlabeled outputs show severe
MONDO polyhierarchy inflation: Diltiazem fans from a specific assertion into
multiple adrenal and long-QT parents; Erlotinib fans into respiratory cancer,
cardiomyopathy, neoplasm, and other classes; and R179 proposes
Erlotinib-immunodeficiency and many broad epilepsy labels. The 25 direct
contraindication overlaps reinforce this failure. R178 and R179 are rejected;
the next ablation requires independent anatomy or gene-disease corroboration
at the broader parent rather than a numeric ontology cutoff.

## Evaluation Batch 18: Safeguarded Ontology Parent Transfer

Six independent evidence architectures were evaluated rather than sweeping an
ontology-degree threshold:

| Rule | Direction | Safeguard | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R181 | GOF | affected anatomy | 442 | 9 | 2 | 12 | 2.49% |
| R182 | GOF | same-gene top-three evidence | 98 | 6 | 1 | 10 | 7.14% |
| R183 | GOF | gene evidence and anatomy | 64 | 3 | 0 | 10 | 4.69% |
| R184 | LOF | affected anatomy | 105 | 10 | 0 | 5 | 9.52% |
| R185 | LOF | same-gene top-three evidence | 9 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% |
| R186 | LOF | gene evidence and anatomy | 7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% |

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T122238Z/summary.json`. No surface
changed: 32 global visible, seven global validation, 12 treatment-free
visible, and seven treatment-free validation.

R182's DRH hit is Sorafenib-thyroid cancer. Unlabeled outputs include plausible
Erdafitinib-craniosynostosis and Tolbutamide-neonatal diabetes, but also
Lacosamide-arrhythmia and ten explicit contraindication overlaps. R183 loses
the DRH hit without removing those contraindications and is rejected.

R184 recovers ten established broader indications, including
Calcitriol-rickets, Liothyronine-hypothyroidism, Diazoxide-hyperinsulinism,
Diazepam or Ezogabine-epilepsy, and Eteplirsen or Viltolarsen-muscular
dystrophy. Its unlabeled cohort still contains Erlotinib-immunodeficiency and
broad phenotype-like disease classes, plus five contraindications. R184
remains an exploratory candidate rather than a precision rule.

R181 is rejected because anatomy alone leaves 442 pairs and 12
contraindications. R185-R186 are rejected because stronger parent-level
corroboration removes every visible hit. R182 remains a high-mechanism
candidate and R184 a medium-mechanism candidate, but neither is Pareto and
neither resolves the graph's ontology-polyhierarchy problem.

## Evaluation Batch 19: Independent Provenance Constraints

Hypothesis: direct GOF or LOF correction should be more credible when the
directional drug effect or physical interaction is independently represented
by a curated source rather than only by targeted text mining.

Source inventory showed that every direct GOF/LOF effect used by R160-R161 had
targeted-text-mining provenance. Only 44 GOF pairs also had SIGNOR provenance.
Forty-six GOF pairs had at least two effect sources; 40 of those also had at
least four binding sources. DrugCentral binding covered nine LOF pairs.

The executable provenance patterns were:

```cypher
// R187: curated directional effect
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'gain_of_function_variant_form'
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND 'infores:signor' IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

```cypher
// R188: independent effect and binding source cardinality
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[seed_effect:AFFECTS]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (gene)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'gain_of_function_variant_form'
  AND seed_effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND seed_effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND seed_effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
CALL (subject, gene) {
  MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
  WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
    AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
    AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  UNWIND coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, []) AS source
  RETURN collect(DISTINCT source) AS effect_sources
}
CALL (subject, gene) {
  MATCH (subject)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene)
  UNWIND coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, []) AS source
  RETURN collect(DISTINCT source) AS binding_sources
}
WITH subject, object, effect_sources, binding_sources
WHERE size(effect_sources) >= 2
  AND size(binding_sources) >= 4
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

R189 uses the R161 LOF-restoration query with this additional constraint:

```cypher
AND 'infores:drugcentral' IN
    coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
```

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T122737Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Provenance constraint | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R187 | SIGNOR directional effect | 44 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 6.82% |
| R188 | >=2 effect and >=4 binding sources | 40 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 7.50% |
| R189 | DrugCentral binding on LOF restoration | 9 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 22.22% |

R188 strictly dominates R187 while preserving the same three known
EGFR-inhibitor/lung-cancer pairs: Erlotinib, Gefitinib, and Afatinib. Its
unlabeled examples include Dacomitinib, Lapatinib, and Neratinib for lung
cancer, as well as kinase inhibitors for rare GOF syndromes. R187 is rejected
as redundant; R188 remains a high-mechanism candidate.

R189's two known pairs are the exon-skipping treatments Eteplirsen and
Viltolarsen for Duchenne muscular dystrophy. Its seven unlabeled pairs include
Doxercalciferol-VDR-related rickets, insulin-Donohue syndrome,
Linaclotide/Plecanatide-GUCY2C deficiency, Somatotropin-Laron syndrome, and
Bremelanotide or Corticotropin-glucocorticoid deficiency. These are
mechanistically heterogeneous, so source identity is useful but not a
substitute for modality semantics.

No Pareto surface changed: 32 global visible, seven global validation, 12
treatment-free visible, and seven treatment-free validation.

## Evaluation Batch 20: Directional Phenotype Concordance

Hypothesis: a corrective GOF/LOF drug-gene direction should become more
credible when graph-native gene and disease phenotypes agree.

The initial executable probes assumed direct gene `HAS_PHENOTYPE` edges:

```cypher
MATCH (gene:Gene)-[:HAS_PHENOTYPE]->
      (phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
MATCH (object:Disease)-[:HAS_PHENOTYPE]->(phenotype)
RETURN count(DISTINCT [gene.id, object.id]) AS pairs
```

This returned zero. The graph represents the gene branch as
`GENE_ASSOCIATED_WITH_CONDITION`, not `HAS_PHENOTYPE`. Exact equality with the
correct predicate also returned zero, as did exact equality plus shared
affected anatomy. This was a query-model failure, not evidence that the graph
contains no gene-phenotype assertions.

The retained ontology-aware form is:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $corrective_direction
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
WITH DISTINCT subject, gene, object
WHERE EXISTS {
  MATCH (gene)-[:GENE_ASSOCIATED_WITH_CONDITION]->
        (gene_phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
  MATCH (object)-[:HAS_PHENOTYPE]->
        (disease_phenotype:PhenotypicFeature)
  MATCH (gene_phenotype)-[:SUBCLASS_OF*0..2]->
        (ancestor:PhenotypicFeature)
        <-[:SUBCLASS_OF*0..2]-(disease_phenotype)
  WHERE coalesce(ancestor._miner_phenotype_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
}
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

R190 binds `$variant_form` to gain of function and
`$corrective_direction` to decreased. R191 binds loss of function and
increased. Replacing a row-multiplying outer phenotype join with the
pair-level `EXISTS` predicate preserved counts and reduced R190 runtime from
approximately 88 seconds to 2.87 seconds; R191 ran in 0.72 seconds.

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T123430Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R190 | GOF inhibition | 51 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% |
| R191 | LOF activation | 6 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% |

R190 is dominated by FGFR-inhibitor predictions for Pfeiffer syndrome,
osteoglophonic dwarfism, and encephalocraniocutaneous lipomatosis. R191
contains Ketamine-Landau-Kleffner syndrome, Ezogabine-autosomal dominant
intellectual disability 46, Somatotropin-Laron syndrome, and less plausible
kinase-inhibitor/hypogonadotropic-hypogonadism pairs.

Both remain high-mechanism but uncalibrated candidates. They do not enter a
surface. The main graph limitation is representational: the gene phenotype
branch has 160,818 assertions but only 112 phenotype objects, so ontology
concordance can be driven by a small, broad set of hubs.

## Evaluation Batch 21: Two-Step Signed Regulatory Chains

Hypothesis: a drug effect can be propagated through two directed, signed
SIGNOR gene-gene edges to correct an explicitly GOF or LOF disease driver.
The rule is nonlinear because target, intermediate, and driver must also
occupy the same compact biological process; variants add role-specific
regulatory degree or shared affected anatomy.

SIGNOR degree inventory used for the predeclared p90 ablation:

| Role degree | Genes | Median | p90 | p95 | p99 | Maximum |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| outgoing | 3,857 | 2 | 9 | 15 | 42 | 246 |
| incoming | 4,679 | 2 | 8 | 13 | 35 | 130 |

The family compiler in `src/miner2049er/rule_families.py` generates this
executable query, with `$sign_operator` set to equality or inequality:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[regulation1:REGULATES]->(middle:Gene)
MATCH (middle)-[regulation2:REGULATES]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (target)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process:BiologicalProcess)
      <-[:INVOLVED_IN]-(middle)
MATCH (driver)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)
WHERE target <> middle
  AND middle <> driver
  AND target <> driver
  AND 'infores:signor' IN
      coalesce(regulation1.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:signor' IN
      coalesce(regulation2.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND regulation1.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND regulation2.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND regulation1.object_direction_qualifier IN
      ['upregulated', 'downregulated']
  AND regulation2.object_direction_qualifier IN
      ['upregulated', 'downregulated']
  AND regulation1.object_direction_qualifier $sign_operator
      regulation2.object_direction_qualifier
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND process._miner_gene_member_degree <= 100
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

For GOF correction, equal signs mean that target inhibition propagates a
decrease to the driver; unequal signs are the adverse control. For LOF
restoration the assignments are reversed. R194-R195 add four `COUNT {}`
predicates enforcing the p90 outgoing/incoming bounds above. R196-R197 add
Bgee expression of all three genes in one anatomy reached from the disease by
`LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN`.

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T124833Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Variant | Context | Sign mode | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Runtime |
|---|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R192 | GOF | compact process | corrective | 128 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 26.68s |
| R193 | LOF | compact process | corrective | 54 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 20.03s |
| R194 | GOF | process + p90 roles | corrective | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 5.21s |
| R195 | LOF | process + p90 roles | corrective | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 5.23s |
| R196 | GOF | process + anatomy | corrective | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 44.27s |
| R197 | LOF | process + anatomy | corrective | 21 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 29.29s |
| R198 | GOF | compact process | adverse | 92 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 19.54s |
| R199 | LOF | compact process | adverse | 139 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 26.65s |

The blind-safe comparator found 25 drug-disease pairs in both R192 and its
adverse control R198: R192-only 103, overlap 25, R198-only 67. R193 and R199
had three shared pairs: R193-only 51, overlap three, R199-only 136. Examples
with both corrective and adverse paths include kinase inhibitors for Noonan
and Proteus syndromes, and Sunitinib or Gefitinib for ataxia telangiectasia.
Path existence therefore does not define a unique net sign in this network.

Concrete traces:

- R194 predicts Tretinoin, Isotretinoin, and Trifarotene for syndromic
  microphthalmia 12 through RARG upregulates THRA, THRA upregulates RARB, and
  the 57-gene retinoic-acid receptor signaling pathway. The path is coherent,
  but the drug-disease relationship is more plausibly teratogenic than
  therapeutic.
- R195 predicts Dopamine and Curcumin for congenital myasthenic syndrome 18;
  neither has visible calibration.
- R196's sole pair is Bosutinib-lung cancer through WEE1 downregulates CDK1,
  CDK1 downregulates EGFR, the 98-gene positive-regulation-of-DNA-replication
  process, and pleura expression. It is plausible but rests at the process
  degree boundary.
- R197 is dominated by kinase inhibitors for ataxia telangiectasia. An example
  path is Erlotinib inhibits ABL1, ABL1 downregulates KAT5, KAT5 upregulates
  ATM, all in a 41-gene cellular-response-to-oxygen-containing-compound process
  and cerebellum.

R192-R195 and R197 are rejected: no visible hits, adverse controls are at
least as productive, and examples expose therapeutic/adverse ambiguity.
R196 remains a single exploratory high-mechanism candidate rather than a
validated rule. R198-R199 remain explicit negative controls. No Pareto surface
changed: 32 global visible, seven global validation, 12 treatment-free
visible, and seven treatment-free validation.

Blocker: the all-subject R197 path-trace query did not return after 90 seconds.
It was interrupted and replaced by a drug-specific trace, which completed in
3.7 seconds. This did not block evaluation or aggregate comparison.

## Hypothesis Batch 22: Graph-Resident Drug Modality

Hypothesis: direct LOF restoration should be enriched when the drug is a
replacement, peptide/protein ligand, oligonucleotide, hormone, or close
analogue rather than a generic small molecule.

This was tested as a property mechanism, not a numeric threshold. The parent
R161 has 90 pairs and two visible treatments. Graph-resident coverage:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'loss_of_function_variant_form'
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'increased'
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
WITH DISTINCT subject, gene, object
RETURN count(*) AS total,
       count(CASE WHEN subject.description IS NOT NULL THEN 1 END)
         AS with_description,
       count(CASE WHEN subject.description =~
         '(?i).*(peptide|polypeptide|protein|hormone|recombinant|analogue|analog|oligonucleotide|antisense|exon.skipping|replacement).*'
         THEN 1 END) AS modality_text,
       count(CASE WHEN EXISTS {
         MATCH (subject)-[:SUBCLASS_OF*1..3]->
               (:ChemicalEntity {id: 'CHEBI:33839'})
       } THEN 1 END) AS macromolecule
```

Results: 90 total pairs, 75 with descriptions, 11 matching modality text,
and three with `CHEBI:33839` macromolecule ancestry.

The graph's materialized categories are not useful for this purpose. Insulin,
Corticotropin, Linaclotide, Plecanatide, Somatotropin, and Bremelanotide all
carry `biolink:SmallMolecule`, despite descriptions or structures identifying
proteins or peptides. Chemical ancestry is absent for Plecanatide and
Somatotropin. The three macromolecule pairs are Corticotropin-MC2R,
Liraglutide-ALPL, and insulin-INSR.

The two visible parent-rule treatments, Eteplirsen and Viltolarsen for
Duchenne muscular dystrophy, have no description, modality property, or useful
chemical ancestry. Their nodes retain ChEMBL and DrugCentral identifiers, but
the only reliable graph-native gate that includes both remains DrugCentral
binding, already evaluated as R189 with nine pairs, two visible hits, and
22.22% lower-bound precision.

An attempted diagnostic query would have returned pair identities together
with raw treatment-source lists. Execution was rejected because it did not
first exclude TTD-only target facts and could have compromised the blind
partition. It was not retried by workaround; aggregate evaluator metrics and
the partition-safe inspector are the approved alternatives.

Conclusion: the biological hypothesis is reasonable, but this graph does not
encode modality consistently enough to produce a general executable rule.
The text and macromolecule gates are rejected before cataloging because both
visible treatments are false negatives. R189 remains the usable provenance
proxy. This exposes a graph gap: explicit drug modality, molecular type, and
replacement/agonist mechanism are needed as normalized properties.

## Hypothesis Batch 23: Nonlinear Signed Motif Coverage

Three internally constrained mechanisms were selected:

1. Reciprocal target-driver feedback.
2. Two drug targets converging on one directional disease driver.
3. Coherent feed-forward loops in which direct and two-step signs agree.

The first versions of the convergence and feed-forward coverage probes failed
with `Invalid input 'WHEN'` because an `EXISTS {}` expression contained a
pattern and nested `MATCH` without beginning the subquery with `MATCH`.
Replacing:

```cypher
EXISTS {
  (target1)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)<-[:INVOLVED_IN]-(target2)
  MATCH (driver)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)
}
```

with:

```cypher
EXISTS {
  MATCH (target1)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)<-[:INVOLVED_IN]-(target2)
  MATCH (driver)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)
}
```

fixed both probes without changing the hypotheses.

Coverage before target scoring:

| Family | GOF corrective | GOF adverse | LOF corrective | LOF adverse |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| reciprocal positive feedback | 181 | 21 | 96 | 86 |
| dual upstream convergence | 306 | 75 | 189 | 329 |
| coherent feed-forward | 375 | 208 | 464 | 642 |

Structural ablations:

| Family/direction | Base | Interaction | Process | Process + anatomy |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| GOF feedback corrective | 181 | n/a | 139 | 21 |
| LOF feedback corrective | 96 | n/a | 40 | 6 |
| GOF convergence corrective | 306 | 68 | 47 | n/a |
| LOF convergence corrective | 189 | 28 | 6 | n/a |
| GOF feed-forward corrective | 375 | 339 physical chain | 26 | n/a |
| LOF feed-forward corrective | 464 | 357 physical chain | 2 | n/a |

No interaction-plus-process convergence pair survived in either corrective
direction. Physical-chain support was too common to be selective for
feed-forward loops, so only process localization was cataloged. The 20
executable variants and controls are R200-R219, generated by parameterized
compilers in `src/miner2049er/rule_families.py`. Evaluation is in progress.

## Hypothesis Batch 23 Results: Nonlinear Signed Motifs

Run artifacts:

- `results/runs/20260812T132731Z/summary.json`
- `results/runs/20260812T133152Z/summary.json` for optimized R207 and R210
- `results/runs/20260812T133432Z/summary.json` for dual-arm R220-R221

The executable Cypher for every variant is retained in the run artifacts and
in the compiled `cypher` field of `results/rule-library.json`. The compiler
calls are parameterized by variant form, corrective/adverse sign assignment,
and context:

```python
reciprocal_signed_feedback_cypher(
    variant_form="gain_of_function_variant_form",
    sign_mode="corrective",
    context="process_anatomy",
    dual_arm=True,
)
dual_upstream_convergence_cypher(
    variant_form="gain_of_function_variant_form",
    sign_mode="corrective",
    context="interaction",
)
coherent_feed_forward_cypher(
    variant_form="gain_of_function_variant_form",
    sign_mode="corrective",
    context="process",
)
```

All variants preserve the same explicit direction predicates:

```cypher
effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
AND effect.object_direction_qualifier IN ['increased', 'decreased']
AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
    coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier IN [
  'gain_of_function_variant_form',
  'loss_of_function_variant_form'
]
```

Complete metrics:

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Runtime |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R200 | GOF corrective positive feedback | 181 | 9 | 0 | 0 | 0.52s |
| R201 | R200 + process/anatomy | 21 | 7 | 0 | 0 | 15.07s |
| R202 | LOF corrective double-negative feedback | 96 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.30s |
| R203 | R202 + process/anatomy | 6 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 10.30s |
| R204 | GOF adverse feedback | 21 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.21s |
| R205 | LOF adverse feedback | 86 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.25s |
| R206 | dual-upstream GOF convergence | 306 | 4 | 0 | 0 | 0.76s |
| R207 | R206 + target interaction | 68 | 4 | 0 | 0 | 11.63s |
| R208 | R206 + shared process | 47 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 180.58s |
| R209 | dual-upstream LOF convergence | 189 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0.57s |
| R210 | R209 + target interaction | 28 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1.87s |
| R211 | R209 + shared process | 6 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 16.68s |
| R212 | GOF adverse convergence | 75 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0.39s |
| R213 | LOF adverse convergence | 329 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.58s |
| R214 | coherent feed-forward GOF | 375 | 10 | 0 | 0 | 0.79s |
| R215 | R214 + shared process | 26 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 181.49s |
| R216 | coherent feed-forward LOF | 464 | 5 | 0 | 0 | 0.93s |
| R217 | R216 + shared process | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 12.51s |
| R218 | GOF adverse feed-forward | 208 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0.55s |
| R219 | LOF adverse feed-forward | 642 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1.13s |
| R220 | dual-arm GOF positive feedback | 88 | 7 | 0 | 0 | 0.98s |
| R221 | R220 + process/anatomy | 17 | 6 | 0 | 0 | 22.78s |

R201's seven visible treatments are Erlotinib, Gefitinib, Afatinib,
Crizotinib, Osimertinib, Lazertinib, and BB-10901 for lung cancer. Its 14
blind-safe novel examples are also all lung cancer. Every path uses the
ERBB2-EGFR loop except Ibrutinib, which also has a LYN-EGFR path. The rule is
therefore a localized indication-specific motif rather than a general
feedback architecture.

R214's ten visible hits are likewise dominated by lung cancer. Novel examples
extend broad kinase and RAS-pathway drugs to cancer and RASopathy labels, with
no independent calibration. The process-localized children R208, R215, and
R217 remove every visible hit despite runtimes of 180 seconds for R208 and
R215. Interaction support is more useful: R207 retains all four R206 visible
hits while reducing 306 pairs to 68.

The first R201 query timed out at 300 seconds because direct process and
anatomy matches multiplied all path rows. Replacing those joins with
pair-level `EXISTS { MATCH ... }` predicates reduced runtime to 15 seconds.
The first R207 implementation similarly placed interaction inside a late
`EXISTS` and timed out at 300 seconds. Matching the target-target interaction
before the driver paths reduced runtime to 11.6 seconds. These are query-plan
changes, not hypothesis changes.

No nonlinear rule reached a validation hit and none changed any Pareto
surface. R207 supersedes R206 as the interaction-supported convergence
candidate. R200, R201, R207, R214, R216, R220, and R221 remain exploratory
candidates because their directionality and visible enrichment are
nontrivial; zero-hit localized children and all explicit adverse controls are
rejected. The surfaces remain 32 global visible, seven global validation, 12
treatment-free visible, and seven treatment-free validation.

## Hypothesis Batch 24: Missing Direction and Variant Structure

Hypothesis: common diseases might support direct corrective rules if
disease-gene edges carry a disease-to-gene activity direction or explicit
sequence-variant intermediates.

Direction inventory:

```cypher
MATCH (object:Disease)-[r]->(gene:Gene)
WHERE r.object_direction_qualifier IS NOT NULL
   OR r.subject_direction_qualifier IS NOT NULL
RETURN type(r), r.qualified_predicate,
       r.subject_direction_qualifier,
       r.object_direction_qualifier, count(*)
ORDER BY count(*) DESC
```

This returned no usable disease-to-gene direction rows. Reversing the pattern
to `MATCH (gene:Gene)-[r]->(object:Disease)` also returned none. The principal
Disease-to-Gene inventory is `CONDITION_ASSOCIATED_WITH_GENE` with 36,577
edges but only 79 disease subjects; only two Disease-to-Gene `AFFECTS` edges
exist.

Variant-node inventory:

```cypher
MATCH (variant)
WHERE 'biolink:SequenceVariant' IN coalesce(variant.categories, [])
RETURN count(variant)
```

Result: zero SequenceVariant nodes.

Variant semantics are flattened onto Gene-to-Disease edges:

```cypher
MATCH (:Gene)-[r]->(:Disease)
WHERE r.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier IS NOT NULL
RETURN r.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier AS variant_form, count(*) AS edges
ORDER BY edges DESC
```

Results:

| Variant form | Edges |
|---|---:|
| `genetic_variant_form` | 164,326 |
| `loss_of_function_variant_form` | 2,011 |
| `gain_of_function_variant_form` | 175 |
| `dominant_negative_variant_form` | 98 |
| other/non-LOF | 11 |

The 164,326 generic variant-form edges have no corrective direction. This is
a hard graph limitation for extending rare-disease GOF/LOF rules into common
diseases without importing external variant or expression evidence. No rule
was cataloged from this batch.

## Hypothesis Batch 25: Anatomical Exposure and Tissue Negative Evidence

Hypothesis: a target mechanism should be more credible if the drug is
represented in an affected anatomy where the target is expressed; disruption
of that same anatomy should be negative evidence.

Graph inventory showed 22,544 Drug-to-Anatomy `LOCATED_IN` edges covering
1,408 drugs and 1,264 anatomies, all from SemMedDB. Drug-to-Anatomy
`ASSOCIATED_WITH`, `AFFECTS`, and `DISRUPTS` are much larger but less specific:
22,037, 5,817, and 1,097 edges respectively.

The executable family template is:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (subject)-[location:LOCATED_IN]->(anatomy:AnatomicalEntity)
MATCH (gene)-[expression:EXPRESSED_IN]->(anatomy)
MATCH (object)-[:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->(anatomy)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND 'infores:semmeddb' IN coalesce(location.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:bgee' IN coalesce(expression.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

R223 adds `evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3`. R224 adds:

```cypher
AND NOT EXISTS { MATCH (subject)-[:DISRUPTS]->(anatomy) }
```

R227 uses the matched counterfactual:

```cypher
AND EXISTS { MATCH (subject)-[:DISRUPTS]->(anatomy) }
```

R225 and R226 replace the feature edge with Gene2Phenotype
`ASSOCIATED_WITH` and require GOF/decrease or LOF/increase respectively.

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T134327Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Runtime |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R222 | causal inhibition + anatomy | 297 | 31 | 1 | 24 | 2.82s |
| R223 | R222 + top-three gene | 167 | 15 | 0 | 22 | 0.75s |
| R224 | R223 excluding disruption | 165 | 15 | 0 | 21 | 1.47s |
| R225 | GOF correction + anatomy | 6 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0.89s |
| R226 | LOF restoration + anatomy | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.62s |
| R227 | R222 requiring disruption | 15 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 4.54s |

R222 is not selective: broad novel examples include Erlotinib-schizophrenia,
Tretinoin-poisoning, and drugs paired to ontology-level `syndrome`, `disease`,
and `neoplasm`. Top-three filtering reduces visible precision from 10.77% to
8.98%; excluding tissue disruption changes only two pairs and one
contraindication. R222-R224 are rejected.

R225 is cleaner but entirely one motif. Erlotinib and Gefitinib are the two
visible lung-cancer treatments; Quercetin, Imatinib, Docetaxel, and Sorafenib
are the four blind-safe predictions. Every path is EGFR and lung, with
additional SemMed location assertions for liver, stomach, prostate, or colon.
R225 remains a high-mechanism exploratory candidate, not a general rule.

R226's sole blind-safe prediction is Metformin-Leigh syndrome through
Metformin increasing NDUFS4, an NDUFS4 LOF disease association, Bgee brain
expression, and brain disease location. It remains a single-prediction
exploratory candidate because no visible calibration exists.

R227's negative control has no visible treatments and one contraindicated
pair among 15 predictions. The adverse signal is small but directionally
consistent; R227 is retained as a rejected diagnostic control rather than a
treatment rule. No Pareto surface changed.

## Hypothesis Batch 26: LOF Compensatory Bypass

Hypothesis: increasing a distinct paralog or molecular-module member can
compensate for an explicit LOF disease driver. This differs from R151-R154,
which decreased a substitute target and lacked disease direction.

The parameterized executable template is:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
/* one of compact family, family+process, component, or interaction context */
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE target <> driver
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'loss_of_function_variant_form'
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Corrective variants use `$direction = 'increased'`; matched adverse controls
use `'decreased'`. Contexts:

```cypher
MATCH (target)-[:MEMBER_OF]->(family:GeneFamily)
      <-[:MEMBER_OF]-(driver)
WHERE family._miner_gene_family_member_degree <= 14
```

```cypher
MATCH (target)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process:BiologicalProcess)
      <-[:INVOLVED_IN]-(driver)
WHERE process._miner_gene_member_degree <= 100
```

```cypher
MATCH (target)-[:PART_OF]->(component:CellularComponent)
      <-[:PART_OF]-(driver)
WHERE component._miner_component_gene_member_degree <= 54
```

```cypher
MATCH (target)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]-(driver)
```

An affected-anatomy version of the compact-family rule had zero coverage.
A more mechanistic SIGNOR version in which the increased target directly
upregulated the LOF driver also had zero coverage. The corresponding
downregulating control likewise had zero coverage.

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T135213Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Context | Direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Runtime |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R228 | compact family | increased | 15 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.60s |
| R229 | compact family | decreased | 80 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.56s |
| R230 | family + compact process | increased | 7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1.63s |
| R231 | family + compact process | decreased | 62 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1.30s |
| R232 | compact cellular component | increased | 134 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.72s |
| R233 | compact cellular component | decreased | 616 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.81s |
| R234 | direct interaction | increased | 999 | 0 | 1 | 1 | 20.10s |
| R235 | direct interaction | decreased | 4,374 | 5 | 2 | 2 | 70.97s |

The corrective direction is consistently less common than the matched adverse
direction, but it is not enriched for visible treatments. Concrete R230 paths
include:

- Sunitinib increases JAK1; JAK1 and JAK3 share a compact kinase family and
  multiple interleukin/JAK-STAT processes; JAK3 LOF causes JAK3-deficient
  severe combined immunodeficiency.
- Methotrexate increases FOLR2; FOLR2 and FOLR1 share the folate-receptor
  family and folic-acid transport processes; FOLR1 LOF causes cerebral folate
  transport deficiency.

These paths establish similarity, not replacement, and the proposed drugs are
not credible compensatory therapies. Other predictions include Testosterone
for catecholaminergic polymorphic ventricular tachycardia 3, Vorinostat for
primary ciliary dyskinesia 41, and Zolpidem for multiple channel or sensory
diseases.

R234's only DRH hit is Stanozolol-breast cancer through increased ESR1 and
physical interaction with the LOF-associated BARD1 driver. The adverse control
has two breast-cancer DRH hits, Tibolone and Raloxifene, and higher training
recall. Physical interaction is therefore not a compensation relation.

All R228-R235 variants are rejected. No threshold search was performed and no
Pareto surface changed. The graph gap is an explicit functional-redundancy,
genetic-rescue, or synthetic-rescue relation; paralogy, co-membership, and
interaction are inadequate substitutes.

## Blind-Partition Protocol Incident: 2026-08-12 23:00 Asia/Seoul

While probing the 12-pair chemical-subclass extension of metabolic product
replacement, one diagnostic query returned candidate drug and disease
identities before excluding all target-union pairs:

```cypher
MATCH (driver:Gene)-[:PRODUCES]->(product:ChemicalEntity)
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:SUBCLASS_OF*1..2]->(product)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE subject <> product
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'loss_of_function_variant_form'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, subject.name, product.id, product.name,
                driver.name, object.id, object.name
```

The query did not retrieve target edges, sources, or partition membership, but
candidate identities could include TTD-only pairs and therefore violated the
campaign's stricter identity-blinding procedure. The returned identities will
not be used for scoring, parameter selection, or examples. R241 and R242 will
be evaluated only through the partition-safe evaluator; subsequent examples
will use `miner-inspect-rule`, whose novel partition explicitly excludes the
entire semantic target union, including TTD.

## Hypothesis Batch 27: Metabolic Rescue and Product Replacement

Hypothesis: explicit metabolic production and substrate relations can support
longer, directionally meaningful LOF treatment mechanisms.

Executable modes are generated by `metabolic_rescue_cypher(mode)`:

```cypher
/* R236: exact product replacement */
MATCH (driver:Gene)-[:PRODUCES]->(subject:Drug)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'loss_of_function_variant_form'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

```cypher
/* R238: accumulated-substrate production blockade */
MATCH (driver:Gene)-[:HAS_SUBSTRATE]->(metabolite:ChemicalEntity)
      <-[:PRODUCES]-(alternate:Gene)
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(alternate)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(alternate)
WHERE alternate <> driver
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
```

R239 replaces `HAS_SUBSTRATE` with a shared `PRODUCES` product and increases
the alternate producer. R240 is its decreased-producer control. R237 directly
administers a LOF enzyme substrate as a negative diagnostic. R241 allows a
drug within two chemical-subclass steps of the product. R242 requires the drug
to `HAS_PART` the product.

Run artifacts:

- `results/runs/20260812T135821Z/summary.json`
- `results/runs/20260812T140213Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R236 | exact product replacement | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R237 | direct substrate administration | 19 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R238 | inhibit alternate substrate producer | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R239 | increase alternate product producer | 24 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R240 | decrease alternate product producer | 85 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R241 | chemical-subclass product analogue | 12 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R242 | formulation contains product | 5 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

R236's two partition-safe predictions are:

- D-glucose for glycogen storage disease Ia through G6PC1 `PRODUCES`
  D-glucose.
- Calcitriol for familial isolated hypoparathyroidism through PTH `PRODUCES`
  calcitriol.

Both are clinically coherent replacement ideas, but both production edges are
SemMed-only and neither has visible target calibration. R236 remains a
high-mechanism exploratory candidate and its outputs are explicitly
predictions, not validated discoveries.

R238 predicts only Panobinostat for GLUT1 and GLUT2 deficiencies. The path says
FOXO1 produces glucose, both transporters have glucose as substrate, and
Panobinostat decreases FOXO1. This is not a credible substrate-reduction
therapy and depends on SemMed `PRODUCES` plus ChEMBL `HAS_SUBSTRATE`.

R239 is dominated by a reactive-oxygen-species hub: ATP, Clemastine,
Ixabepilone, Lenalidomide, Panobinostat, and Tivozanib increase P2RX7, which
SemMed says produces reactive oxygen species, shared with PTEN, CYBA, and
CYBB. The 85-pair adverse control is larger and neither direction has visible
calibration.

R241 consists entirely of Oxygen, Hypochlorite, and Hioxyl as descendants of
reactive oxygen species for PTEN/CYBA/CYBB diseases. R242's paths are all
SemMed meronymy:

- Bleomycin or Gelatin `HAS_PART` DNA.
- Arginine or Tyrosine `HAS_PART` D-glucose.
- Parathyroid hormone `HAS_PART` calcium.

The parathyroid-hormone prediction is biologically plausible, but the
executable path supports it for the wrong reason. R237-R242 are rejected.
No Pareto surface changed. The graph needs reaction identifiers, explicit
substrate/product roles, stoichiometry or flux direction, and normalized
replacement-product relations; SemMed `PRODUCES` and `HAS_PART` are too
context-free for general metabolic reasoning.

## Hypothesis Batch 28: Cell-Type-Mediated Anatomy

Hypothesis: cell-specific expression can localize a causal target more
precisely than organ-level expression, either through exact disease-cell
location or a Cell-to-Anatomy ontology bridge.

Bgee contains 217,441 Gene-to-Cell `EXPRESSED_IN` edges for 39,716 genes, but
only 41 cell objects. The expression edges have no quantitative or
stage-specific properties beyond standard provenance. A reproducible
`_miner_human_gene_expression_degree` feature was added to Cell nodes:

```cypher
MATCH (gene:Gene)-[expression:EXPRESSED_IN]->(cell:Cell)
WHERE 'infores:bgee' IN coalesce(expression.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
WITH cell, count(DISTINCT gene) AS degree
SET cell._miner_human_gene_expression_degree = degree
RETURN count(cell), min(degree),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.25),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.5),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.75),
       percentileDisc(degree, 0.9), max(degree)
```

Distribution: 41 cells, minimum 3, p25 41, median 5,512, p75 9,067,
p90 9,997, maximum 19,712.

Exact-cell executable rule:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (gene)-[expression:EXPRESSED_IN]->(cell:Cell)
MATCH (object)-[:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->(cell)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND 'infores:bgee' IN coalesce(expression.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

R244 adds top-three causal rank. R245 replaces exact disease-cell identity with
a two-step Cell `SUBCLASS_OF` Anatomy bridge and requires cell expression
degree at or below the median. The p25 ontology variant had zero coverage and
was not cataloged.

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T140853Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R243 | exact disease cell | 73 | 20 | 0 | 0 | 27.40% |
| R244 | exact cell + top-three gene | 41 | 9 | 0 | 0 | 21.95% |
| R245 | median-degree cell ontology bridge | 145 | 30 | 3 | 0 | 22.76% |

R243 enters the treatment-free visible Pareto surface. Its exact shared cells
are dominated by granulocyte, leukocyte, and bone-marrow cell, with 9,067 to
19,712 expressed genes. All Disease-to-Cell location edges are SemMed-only,
so the rule is better interpreted as a hematologic disease-context filter than
cell-specific expression. R244 worsens precision and recall and is rejected.

R245 initially entered both treatment-free visible and validation Pareto
surfaces. Its three DRH hits were Docetaxel-breast cancer,
Neratinib-breast cancer, and Sorafenib-thyroid cancer. Path inspection showed
that the ontology bridge is invalid for the intended mechanism:

- Docetaxel and Neratinib use EGFR expression in pancreatic epithelial or
  pancreatic ductal cells, which subclass generic `epithelial cell` or
  `UMLS:Cells` reached from breast cancer.
- Sorafenib uses BRAF expression in buccal mucosa cell, again bridged through
  generic `epithelial cell` or `UMLS:Cells` to thyroid cancer.

These are Cell-to-Cell ontology paths, not Cell-to-Organ localization.
R245 is rejected despite its empirical Pareto status because the executable
mechanism is semantically false. R243 remains a medium-mechanism candidate;
R244-R245 are rejected. The graph needs cell-expression specificity and
explicit Cell-to-UBERON partonomy rather than generic Cell Ontology ancestry.

## Hypothesis Batch 29: Disease-Relative Causal-Module Coverage

Hypothesis: common diseases can be made tractable by ranking genes within each
disease rather than imposing an absolute disease-gene degree threshold. A drug
that directly binds and decreases several of the highest-ranked causal genes
may cover enough of a disease mechanism to be predictive despite a noisy
background association neighborhood.

The executable base family is:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= $maximum_rank
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
WITH subject, object, count(DISTINCT gene) AS targeted_genes
WHERE targeted_genes >= $minimum_targets
RETURN subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

R246 uses two of the top three genes. R247 uses three of the top five. R248 is
a lower-rank matched diagnostic using two genes ranked four through ten.
R249 and R250 add this explicit negative-evidence gate:

```cypher
AND NOT EXISTS {
  MATCH (subject)-[:CONTRAINDICATED_IN|EXACERBATES_CONDITION|CAUSES]->(object)
}
```

Run artifacts:

- `results/runs/20260812T141531Z/summary.json`
- `results/runs/20260812T141825Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Coverage | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision | Validation precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R246 | top 3, at least 2 | 42 | 12 | 2 | 1 | 33.33% | 6.67% |
| R247 | top 5, at least 3 | 4 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 50.00% | 33.33% |
| R248 | ranks 4-10, at least 2 | 3 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 33.33% | 0.00% |
| R249 | R247 plus adverse gate | 3 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 66.67% | 50.00% |
| R250 | R246 plus adverse gate | 37 | 11 | 2 | 0 | 35.14% | 7.69% |

R249 enters the global validation, treatment-free visible, and treatment-free
validation Pareto surfaces. Its complete visible-safe output is:

- Phenytoin-epilepsy, training.
- Eslicarbazepine-epilepsy, DRH validation.
- Cannabidiol-epilepsy, partition-safe novel prediction.

All three inhibit SCN8A at disease rank 1, SCN2A at rank 2, and SCN1A at rank
4. The corresponding target-effect evidence includes at least one non-SemMed
source. The adverse gate removes Lidocaine-epilepsy, which otherwise has the
same sodium-channel module architecture but an explicit contraindication.

R246's second DRH hit is Sorafenib-thyroid cancer. Broader partition-safe novel
examples include antiarrhythmics for arrhythmia, sulfonylureas for neonatal
diabetes, steroidogenesis inhibitors for congenital adrenal hyperplasia, and
Methylthioninium for amnesia or dementia. R248's lower-rank control loses all
validation support, providing an informative ablation in favor of
disease-relative top-rank coverage.

Two additional coverage hypotheses failed before cataloging:

- Requiring five inhibited genes within the top ten returned zero pairs.
- Requiring the covered top-ranked genes also to directly interact returned
  zero pairs after correcting an initial invalid list-indexing Cypher attempt.

The latter does not show that the modules lack biological interaction; it shows
that this graph's direct Gene-to-Gene interaction layer does not connect these
particular drug-covered modules under the tested predicate.

### Shared Process and Cellular Component Ablations

R251 requires two top-three genes to share a BiologicalProcess with measured
gene-member degree at most 100. R252 adds the adverse gate. R253/R254 apply the
same design to a CellularComponent with measured gene-member degree at most 54:

```cypher
MATCH (gene)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(context:BiologicalProcess)
WHERE context._miner_gene_member_degree <= 100
WITH subject, object, context, count(DISTINCT gene) AS targeted_genes
WHERE targeted_genes >= 2
```

The component form substitutes:

```cypher
MATCH (gene)-[:PART_OF]->(context:CellularComponent)
WHERE context._miner_gene_member_degree <= 54
```

Corrected run artifact:
`results/runs/20260812T142550Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Context | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R251 | compact process | 23 | 3 | 1 | 1 | 17.39% |
| R252 | compact process + adverse gate | 19 | 3 | 1 | 0 | 21.05% |
| R253 | compact component | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% |
| R254 | compact component + adverse gate | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.00% |

The process rule's DRH hit is again Eslicarbazepine-epilepsy. Examples reveal
mixed context quality:

- SCN8A and SCN2A share `cardiac muscle cell action potential involved in
  contraction`, which is real GOA evidence but is not the desired neuronal
  mechanism for epilepsy.
- KCNJ11 and ABCC8 share `negative regulation of insulin secretion`, supporting
  the neonatal-diabetes mechanism.
- MAPT and APP share astrocyte activation, amyloid fibril formation, microglial
  activation, and related processes for the Methylthioninium-dementia
  prediction.

Despite some strong examples, process coherence removes nine training hits
while retaining many unresolved pairs and therefore worsens both precision and
recall relative to the ranked-module parent. R251-R252 are rejected.

An important implementation failure was found and preserved. The first R253
run used:

```cypher
coalesce(context._miner_gene_member_degree, 0) <= 54
```

This incorrectly treated missing degree features as compact components and
produced 28 pairs, 12 training hits, one DRH hit, and 46.43% visible precision.
Those paths were biologically coherent, including voltage-gated sodium channel
complex for SCN8A/SCN2A, inward-rectifying potassium channel for KCNJ11/ABCC8,
and receptor complex for KIT/PDGFRA. However, all inspected component nodes had
null degree. Fail-fast measured-degree semantics reduce R253-R254 to zero.
They are rejected as currently executable. This is a graph-feature coverage
gap: the relevant GO cellular components exist, but the feature materialization
does not cover them.

After the corrected rerun, Pareto surfaces remain:

- Global visible: 32.
- Global validation: 8.
- Treatment-free visible: 14.
- Treatment-free validation: 8.

R249 remains on three surfaces. R246 and R250 remain broader coverage
candidates; R248 and R251-R254 are rejected.

## Hypothesis Batch 30: Signed Process-Level Correction

Hypothesis: a drug-level change to a biological process can be composed with
Gene2Phenotype disease-variant direction. Decreasing a process containing a
gain-of-function driver should be corrective; increasing one containing a
loss-of-function driver should be restorative. Same-direction GOF increase and
LOF decrease are matched worsening controls.

Qualified Drug-to-BiologicalProcess inventory:

| Process effect | Edges | Drugs | Processes | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| increased | 19,087 | 989 | 1,886 | CTD |
| decreased | 10,015 | 921 | 1,296 | CTD |
| unqualified | 17,845 | 1,318 | 1,933 | CTD or SemMedDB |

There are also 4,254 SemMedDB-only Drug `DISRUPTS` BiologicalProcess edges,
covering 1,013 drugs and 336 processes. Gene-to-process GOA coverage is much
larger: 380,302 edges, 64,394 genes, and 11,791 processes; all these edges have
a process gene-member-degree feature.

The direct executable family is:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[process_effect:AFFECTS]->
      (process:BiologicalProcess)
MATCH (driver:Gene)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE process_effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND process_effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND 'infores:ctd' IN
      coalesce(process_effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND process._miner_gene_member_degree <= 100
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

The cross-channel variants additionally require:

```cypher
MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[target_effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)
WHERE target <> driver
  AND target_effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND target_effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND target_effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
```

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T143132Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Variant/process sign | Cross-channel | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R255 | GOF / decrease | no | 1,397 | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| R256 | LOF / increase | no | 7,972 | 3 | 0 | 9 |
| R257 | GOF / increase control | no | 1,897 | 2 | 0 | 1 |
| R258 | LOF / decrease control | no | 5,931 | 4 | 0 | 8 |
| R259 | GOF / decrease | yes | 78 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R260 | LOF / increase | yes | 103 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R261 | GOF / increase control | yes | 32 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R262 | LOF / decrease control | yes | 295 | 1 | 0 | 0 |

The direct motifs are almost completely uncalibrated, with precision below
0.11% and no DRH hits. Cross-channel convergence removes most candidates but
does not recover any visible corrective treatments. The only cross-channel
training hit is the nominally worsening Carvedilol-Duchenne muscular dystrophy
control from clinical-trial evidence.

Partition-safe example paths explain the failure:

- Levodopa decreases DRD2 and CTD says it decreases `regulation of heart
  rate`; SCN5A, the long-QT-syndrome-3 GOF driver, is merely involved in that
  90-gene process.
- Zolpidem increases GABRB3 and `inhibitory postsynaptic potential`; GLRA1, the
  hyperekplexia LOF driver, is also involved in that 29-gene process.
- Carvedilol decreases RYR2 and `regulation of heart rate`; DMD is another
  member of the process, producing the worsening-direction control hit for
  Duchenne muscular dystrophy.

The sources are independent and individually credible at their stated level:
CTD for drug-process direction, targeted text mining for drug-target effect,
GOA for process membership, and Gene2Phenotype for disease variant form. The
composition is nevertheless invalid because `INVOLVED_IN` carries no sign and
a GOF or LOF variant need not increase or decrease every process containing
the gene. The graph needs disease-specific process activity, signed
gene-to-process causal effects, or quantitative pathway-state evidence.
R255-R262 are rejected. No Pareto surface changed: 32 global visible, 8 global
validation, 14 treatment-free visible, and 8 treatment-free validation.

## Hypothesis Batch 31: Negative Evidence on Ranked Modules

Hypothesis: graph-native adverse evidence can improve the causal-module family
without changing its positive thresholds. Three gates were tested:

1. Exact SIDER side-effect overlap with an HPO phenotype of the disease.
2. An opposite-direction increase assertion on any covered gene.
3. Drug `DISRUPTS` a `MINER_DISEASE_PROCESS_SUPPORT` process for the disease.

Aggregate ablations:

| Parent | Pairs | Exact phenotype overlap | Opposite target effect | Disrupted disease process |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R247, top-five/three-target | 4 | 0 | 1 | 0 |
| R246, top-three/two-target | 42 | 12 | 0 | 0 |

The first attempt to list all R249 phenotype overlaps used a disconnected
optional-match plan and exceeded Neo4j's 1.4-GiB transaction-memory limit:

```text
The allocation of an extra 2.0 MiB would use more than the limit 1.4 GiB.
Currently using 1.4 GiB. dbms.memory.transaction.total.max threshold reached
```

The query was replaced by bounded indexed probes over the four already known,
blind-safe R247 pairs. None has an exact side-effect overlap. The sole opposite
assertion is Cannabidiol increasing SCN1A, rank 4, from
`infores:text-mining-provider-targeted`; the positive path also says
Cannabidiol decreases SCN1A. A hard opposite-effect gate would remove the only
R249 novel prediction and leave only the two visible hits. It was not
cataloged: a 100%-calibrated two-pair rule with no novel prediction is not a
useful improvement, and the same-source contradiction should be represented
as uncertainty rather than silently optimized away.

R263 applies direct adverse gating plus exact SIDER phenotype exclusion to the
broader R250 rule:

```cypher
AND NOT EXISTS {
  MATCH (subject)-[:CONTRAINDICATED_IN|EXACERBATES_CONDITION|CAUSES]->(object)
}
AND NOT EXISTS {
  MATCH (subject)-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]->(:PhenotypicFeature)
        <-[:HAS_PHENOTYPE]-(object)
}
```

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T143621Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision | Validation precision |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R250 | 37 | 11 | 2 | 0 | 35.14% | 7.69% |
| R263 | 26 | 4 | 2 | 0 | 23.08% | 9.09% |

R263 enters the treatment-free validation Pareto surface because it preserves
Eslicarbazepine-epilepsy and Sorafenib-thyroid cancer while reducing the
denominator. It nevertheless removes seven established kinase-inhibitor
treatments:

- Sunitinib, Imatinib, Nilotinib, Regorafenib, and Pazopanib for Plexosarcoma.
- Dasatinib and Nilotinib for BCR-ABL1-positive chronic myelogenous leukemia.

The exact shared phenotypes are generic disease symptoms or treatment
toxicities: fatigue, fever, poor appetite, dysphagia, leukocytosis, and
gastrointestinal hemorrhage. Their disease-member degrees range from 54 to
587. A treated disease can share symptoms with a drug's adverse effects
without the drug reproducing the disease mechanism. R263 is rejected despite
its empirical Pareto flag. No phenotype ontology threshold was tuned after
this result; the failure is semantic, not a cutoff-selection problem.

## Hypothesis Batch 32: Provenance-Supported Causal Modules

Hypothesis: target count should be combined with evidence independence rather
than treated as sufficient. Three provenance dimensions were tested:

1. Repeated directional-effect evidence items.
2. At least two distinct publications supporting each directional effect.
3. At least two independent primary binding databases for each target.

A curated SIGNOR-only holdout tests dependence on the dominant text-mining
direction channel.

Direct Drug-to-Gene binding sources are diverse:

| Source | Edges |
|---|---:|
| BindingDB | 6,798 |
| DGIdb | 5,920 |
| Guide to PHARMACOLOGY | 2,892 |
| ChEMBL | 1,590 |
| TTD | 1,249 |
| SIGNOR | 991 |
| DrugCentral | 878 |
| WOMBAT-PK | 750 |

Directional Drug-to-Gene decrease is not diverse: 184,403 edges come from
`infores:text-mining-provider-targeted` and only 135 from SIGNOR. All 184,538
qualified decrease edges have publication lists. Their extracted evidence
count distribution is minimum 1, median 2, p75 4, p90 10, p99 75, maximum
21,504.

The repeated-evidence executable condition is:

```cypher
AND coalesce(
      effect.evidence_count,
      size(coalesce(effect.publications, [])),
      1
    ) >= 2
```

True publication independence is computed after unwinding:

```cypher
UNWIND coalesce(effect.publications, []) AS publication
WITH subject, object, gene,
     collect(DISTINCT publication) AS provenance_values
WHERE size(provenance_values) >= 2
```

Binding-source independence uses:

```cypher
UNWIND coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, []) AS binding_source
WITH subject, object, gene,
     collect(DISTINCT binding_source) AS provenance_values
WHERE size(provenance_values) >= 2
```

Run artifacts:

- `results/runs/20260812T144339Z/summary.json`
- `results/runs/20260812T144608Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Provenance requirement | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision | Validation precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R264 | repeated evidence, top 3 / 2 | 27 | 8 | 2 | 0 | 37.04% | 10.53% |
| R265 | repeated evidence, top 5 / 3 | 3 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 66.67% | 50.00% |
| R266 | two publications, top 3 / 2 | 29 | 9 | 1 | 0 | 34.48% | 5.00% |
| R267 | two publications, top 5 / 3 | 2 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 50.00% | 0.00% |
| R268 | two binding sources, top 3 / 2 | 22 | 9 | 1 | 0 | 45.45% | 7.69% |
| R269 | SIGNOR-only, top 3 / 2 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 100.00% | 0.00% |
| R270 | repeated evidence + two binding sources | 16 | 7 | 1 | 0 | 50.00% | 11.11% |

R265 and R249 have exactly identical candidate sets: Phenytoin-epilepsy,
Eslicarbazepine-epilepsy, and the partition-safe Cannabidiol-epilepsy
prediction. R265 reaches the set through positive evidence support rather than
an explicit adverse-edge veto. Lidocaine fails because its SCN8A decrease has
only one evidence item; all three R265 drugs have at least two extracted items
for SCN8A, SCN2A, and SCN1A. This is the cleaner formulation, but its caveat is
that Eslicarbazepine's two extractions per channel gene come from the same
publication.

The cannabidiol SCN1A contradiction is evidence-resolved rather than merely a
pair of opaque edges:

- The increase edge has one extraction from one 2021 paper stating that CBD
  restores Nav1.1/Nav1.6 balance.
- The decrease edge has two publications describing inhibition or blockade of
  Nav1.1 through Nav1.7.

The positive rule therefore has more direct inhibitory evidence, but both
edges remain represented and the contradiction is reported rather than
discarded.

R268's DRH hit is Sorafenib-thyroid cancer. Example binding consensus:

- Sorafenib-BRAF and Sorafenib-RET each appear in SIGNOR, GtoPdb, DGIdb,
  ChEMBL, and BindingDB.
- Sunitinib-KIT and Sunitinib-PDGFRA have five independent binding sources.
- Tolbutamide-KCNJ11 has GtoPdb, DrugCentral, and DGIdb; Tolbutamide-ABCC8 has
  TTD, DrugCentral, and DGIdb.
