as one Simvastatin-KRAS RASopathy mechanism with two ontology-distinct disease
predictions, not as two independent successes.

Graph limitations:

- The prenylation edge does not specify residue, prenyltransferase, cell type,
  dose, or mutant allele.
- Both predictions depend on one publication and one source.
- The graph does not represent whether the observed prenylation reduction is
  sufficient to change disease-relevant KRAS signaling in vivo.

## Batch 56: Molecular-Input Cofactor Rescue Probe

Hypothesis: a drug or nutrient that is an explicit input to a molecular
activity enabled by a LOF driver may act as a cofactor or substrate
supplement.

```cypher
MATCH (driver:Gene)-[:ENABLES]->(activity:MolecularActivity)
      -[input:HAS_INPUT]->(subject:Drug)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'loss_of_function_variant_form'
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

The graph has 29 MolecularActivity-to-Drug `HAS_INPUT` edges, all from
SemMedDB and all attached to one molecular activity. None intersects an LOF
Gene2Phenotype driver, so the rule has zero coverage. No candidate was
cataloged.

Graph limitation: GO molecular activities generally do not encode chemical
inputs in this graph, while the broad `HAS_INPUT` relation is dominated by
heterogeneous SemMedDB procedures. A cofactor rule needs a curated
enzyme-cofactor or reaction-participant source connected to the enabled
activity and stoichiometric chemical.

## Batch 57: GO-CAM Molecular Input Flow

Hypothesis: GO-CAM `IS_INPUT_OF` edges may support a longer causal rule in
which changing an upstream gene alters molecular flow into an explicit GOF or
LOF disease driver.

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->
      (upstream:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(upstream)
MATCH (upstream)-[flow:IS_INPUT_OF]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE upstream <> driver
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND 'infores:go-cam' IN coalesce(flow.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

The graph contains 860 GO-CAM gene-to-gene `IS_INPUT_OF` edges. None carries
an object-direction qualifier. Run artifact:
`results/runs/20260812T170136Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Variant / drug direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R354 | GOF / decreased | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R355 | GOF / increased control | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R356 | LOF / increased | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R357 | LOF / decreased control | 13 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

R354 predicts Brigatinib-Okur-Chung neurodevelopmental syndrome through
PLK1 `IS_INPUT_OF` CSNK2A1 and Sunitinib-early-onset epileptic encephalopathy
through PRKAA1/PRKAA2 `IS_INPUT_OF` YWHAG. These are GO-CAM activity-model
connections, but neither establishes that inhibiting the input reduces the
GOF driver's pathogenic activity in the relevant cell.

R356 predicts Lithium citrate for an FBXO11 developmental disorder through
GSK3B and Tivozanib for Hao-Fountain syndrome through MAPK3 and USP7. Both
drug-upstream pairs also have decreased-effect assertions and therefore occur
in the nominal LOF worsening branch. The Tivozanib and Lithium effects are
targeted-text-mining assertions.

R357 includes mechanistically adverse examples such as Warfarin and related
vitamin-K antagonists for vitamin-K-dependent coagulation-factor deficiency,
but no visible contraindication label. Its larger 13-pair coverage does not
validate the flow sign; it illustrates why `IS_INPUT_OF` should not be treated
as monotonic activation.

R354 and R356 are rejected with their controls. `IS_INPUT_OF` connects
activities in a causal model, but a perturbation cannot be algebraically
propagated through it without the activity nodes, causal relation type, and
sign.

Graph limitation: the flattened gene-to-gene edge loses the molecular
activity instances and causal semantics that make a GO-CAM path interpretable.
Preserving the original GO-CAM activity graph would allow input/output
composition instead of guessing sign from adjacency.

## Batch 58: R349 Binding-Provenance Ablation

R349 was refined with one bounded provenance comparison rather than a
threshold sweep. Aggregate audit of its 34 pairs found:

- zero pairs with an opposite increased-activity edge;
- 23 pairs with at least two distinct direct-binding primary sources;
- 22 pairs with at least two decreased-activity primary sources.

Because the binding and effect provenance subsets were nearly identical, only
the binding-source variant was promoted:

```cypher
WITH subject, driver, object, collect(DISTINCT binding) AS bindings
UNWIND bindings AS binding
UNWIND coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, []) AS binding_source
WITH subject, driver, object,
     count(DISTINCT binding_source) AS binding_source_count
WHERE binding_source_count >= 2
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T170434Z/summary.json`.

R358 returns 23 target-free pairs, no visible hits, and no adverse pair. It
preserves the multi-database FGFR-inhibitor cohort, including Infigratinib,
Erdafitinib, Pemigatinib, Futibatinib, and Ponatinib for achondroplasia,
hypochondroplasia, or Crouzon syndrome. It also preserves Momelotinib-FOP.

The 11 R349-only pairs include both weak and distinctive mechanisms:
Rabeprazole-FGFR syndromes are removed, but so are Lorlatinib-FOP and
Lonafarnib-Costello syndrome. Binding-source count therefore separates
database consensus, not biological novelty or mechanism quality.

R349 and R358 remain complementary uncalibrated candidates:

- R349 is the broader discovery variant and retains high-specificity
  single-source mechanisms.
- R358 is the conservative multi-database binding variant dominated by
  kinase inhibitors.

No second effect-provenance threshold was promoted.

### Pareto checkpoint after Batches 50-58

After synchronizing all catalog statuses, the accepted surfaces remain:

| Surface | Rules |
|---|---:|
| Global visible | 32 |
| Global validation | 9 |
| Treatment-free visible | 17 |
| Treatment-free validation | 9 |

None of R341-R358 enters a Pareto surface because the retained new
high-mechanism rules are uncalibrated or have lower visible precision than
existing rules. This is expected for rare-disease prediction families and is
why mechanism score is tracked separately from empirical Pareto membership.

## Batch 59: Signed Regulatory Cascade in a Shared Cellular Component

Hypothesis: a drug perturbing a signed regulator of an explicit GOF or LOF
disease driver is more credible when regulator and driver are both active in
the same relatively specific cellular component. This combines a drug-target
edge, a signed drug effect, a signed SIGNOR gene-gene edge, two independent
subcellular-localization edges, and a variant-qualified disease edge.

The first rank-filtered formulation had zero coverage. The evaluated
formulation removed the disease-rank constraint but retained the independently
chosen p90 component-degree bound of 185:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->
      (regulator:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[drug_effect:AFFECTS]->(regulator)
MATCH (regulator)-[gene_effect:REGULATES]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (regulator)-[:ACTIVE_IN]->(component:CellularComponent)
      <-[:ACTIVE_IN]-(driver)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE regulator <> driver
  AND drug_effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND drug_effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND gene_effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND 'infores:signor' IN
      coalesce(gene_effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND component._miner_component_active_gene_degree <= 185
  AND (
    (drug_effect.object_direction_qualifier = $drug_direction_1
     AND gene_effect.object_direction_qualifier = $gene_direction_1)
    OR
    (drug_effect.object_direction_qualifier = $drug_direction_2
     AND gene_effect.object_direction_qualifier = $gene_direction_2)
  )
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T170750Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Variant / composed direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Unresolved |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R359 | GOF corrective | 22 | 7 | 0 | 0 | 15 |
| R360 | GOF worsening control | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| R361 | LOF corrective | 5 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 5 |
| R362 | LOF worsening control | 14 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 14 |

All seven R359 training hits are drugs for lung cancer. Exact path inspection
shows that Erlotinib, Gefitinib, Afatinib, Crizotinib, Osimertinib,
Lazertinib, and BB-10901 are recovered through decreased ERBB2 followed by
SIGNOR upregulation of GOF EGFR. ERBB2 and EGFR are both active in the basal
plasma membrane, whose active-gene degree is 20. The SIGNOR path is supported
by PMID 23027125 or PMID 8816440, but the drug-effect edge is generally from
the targeted text-mining provider. Thus, the seven hits are one coherent
mechanistic cohort rather than seven independent validations.

The 15 target-free predictions are mostly additional kinase inhibitors for
lung cancer, including Dacomitinib, Dasatinib, Lapatinib, Vandetanib,
Neratinib, Tucatinib, and Mobocertinib. Dacomitinib uses the same
ERBB2-to-EGFR basal-membrane path and is therefore a direct within-cohort
prediction. A distinct prediction is Ibrutinib-CBL-related disorder through
decreased FYN, SIGNOR upregulation of GOF CBL, and shared activity in the
membrane raft (degree 73; PMID 9890970).

R359 is retained as a high-mechanism candidate. Its 31.82% visible precision
is useful, but it is empirically dominated by broader direct-target rules and
its apparent support is not independent across drugs. It should be reported as
two regulatory motifs, not as 22 unrelated paths.

R361 is rejected. Its five target-free predictions have no visible
calibration and include Doxorubicin-primary microcephaly 2,
Sunitinib-JAK3-deficient severe combined immunodeficiency, and
Ibrutinib-exudative vitreoretinopathy. The matched LOF worsening branch is
larger, so shared component localization does not establish monotonic rescue
for LOF cascades.

Graph limitations:

- `ACTIVE_IN` supplies a cellular-component context but not disease tissue,
  cell type, complex membership, or simultaneous activity.
- The shared component can be true for both proteins without the SIGNOR
  regulation occurring there.
- Targeted text-mining drug-effect edges are often the weakest link in an
  otherwise curated path.
- The validation concentration in one disease and one ERBB2-EGFR motif limits
  evidence independence.

## Batch 60: Post-Translational Turnover Consensus Probe

Hypothesis: a coherent increase in both ubiquitination and degradation could
remove a GOF driver, while coherent decreases in both aspects could preserve a
LOF driver. Direct binding was required so the mechanism would not reduce to
uncurated association.

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[ubiquitination:AFFECTS]->(driver)
MATCH (subject)-[degradation:AFFECTS]->(driver)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE ubiquitination <> degradation
  AND ubiquitination.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND ubiquitination.object_aspect_qualifier = 'ubiquitination'
  AND ubiquitination.object_direction_qualifier = $ubiquitination_direction
  AND degradation.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND degradation.object_aspect_qualifier = 'degradation'
  AND degradation.object_direction_qualifier = $degradation_direction
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

All four predeclared directions had zero coverage:

- GOF with increased ubiquitination and increased degradation.
- GOF preservation control with both directions decreased.
- LOF with decreased ubiquitination and decreased degradation.
- LOF removal control with both directions increased.

Direct-target ubiquitination alone also had zero coverage. The family was not
cataloged. Relaxing direct binding would leave isolated CTD aspect assertions
without evidence that ubiquitination controls the disease driver, so no
association-only fallback was attempted.

Graph limitation: the graph contains sparse ubiquitination assertions but
does not connect them to concordant degradation evidence, ubiquitin ligases,
proteasome routing, modification sites, or mutant-protein selectivity.

## Batch 61: Signed GO-Process Restoration

Hypothesis: explicit positive- and negative-effect GO predicates can support a
longer process-restoration rule. Drug direction is composed with the target's
signed effect on a process; GOF or LOF direction is composed with the disease
driver's signed effect on the same process. A therapeutic path must make the
drug-induced process change oppose the inferred disease process change.

This differs from the rejected unsigned process-membership rules because both
gene-to-process edges carry explicit causal signs.

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[target_process]->(process:BiologicalProcess)
      <-[driver_process]-(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE target <> driver
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier IN ['increased', 'decreased']
  AND type(target_process) IN
      ['ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_POSITIVE_EFFECT',
       'ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_NEGATIVE_EFFECT',
       'ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_OR_WITHIN_POSITIVE_EFFECT',
       'ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_OR_WITHIN_NEGATIVE_EFFECT']
  AND type(driver_process) IN
      ['ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_POSITIVE_EFFECT',
       'ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_NEGATIVE_EFFECT',
       'ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_OR_WITHIN_POSITIVE_EFFECT',
       'ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_OR_WITHIN_NEGATIVE_EFFECT']
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'loss_of_function_variant_form'
  AND process._miner_gene_member_degree <= 100
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
WITH subject, object,
     collect(DISTINCT
       CASE WHEN
         CASE effect.object_direction_qualifier
           WHEN 'increased' THEN 1 ELSE -1
         END *
         CASE WHEN type(target_process) CONTAINS 'POSITIVE'
           THEN 1 ELSE -1
         END
         <>
         -1 *
         CASE WHEN type(driver_process) CONTAINS 'POSITIVE'
           THEN 1 ELSE -1
         END
       THEN 'corrective' ELSE 'worsening' END
     ) AS path_modes
WHERE 'corrective' IN path_modes
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

The GOF branches had zero coverage. Run artifact:
`results/runs/20260812T171952Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Branch | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Unresolved |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R363 | LOF corrective | 8 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 8 |
| R364 | LOF worsening control | 7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 7 |
| R365 | LOF corrective, no worsening path | 7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 7 |

One pair, Somatotropin-growth hormone insensitivity with immune dysregulation
1, occurs in both directions through different processes and is removed by
R365. Its nominal corrective path increases GHR, while GHR and LOF STAT5B both
positively affect taurine metabolism. This is biologically weak because a
downstream STAT5B defect can remain insensitive to increased receptor
stimulation.

Six R365 pairs are three gliptins crossed with two MAP3K20 disorders:
Vildagliptin, Saxagliptin, and Alogliptin decrease DPP9; DPP9 negatively
affects pyroptosis; and MAP3K20 positively affects pyroptosis. The three drugs
and two diseases therefore represent one DPP9-pyroptosis-MAP3K20 hypothesis,
not six independent mechanisms.

The seventh prediction is Sunitinib-polyhydramnios, megalencephaly, and
symptomatic epilepsy. Sunitinib increases STK11; STK11 and LOF STRADA both
negatively affect the compact `G1 to G0 transition` process. This is a
coherent STK11-STRADA complex-level idea, but the drug-effect assertion is
from targeted text mining.

R363 is rejected because it admits explicit process-sign contradiction. R365
is retained as an uncalibrated high-mechanism candidate. Its predictions must
be reported as two molecular motifs, and neither has a visible target label.

Graph limitations:

- GO causal process annotations are gene-level, not allele-, tissue-, or
  disease-specific.
- Opposing process paths can exist for one pair, requiring pair-level
  contradiction handling.
- A process may be correctly signed yet unrelated to the disease phenotype
  produced by the driver.
- All retained drug-effect edges in the inspected paths come from targeted
  text mining.

## Batch 62: Interacting Molecular-Activity Co-Enablers

Hypothesis: increasing a distinct protein that both physically interacts with
a LOF driver and enables the same compact molecular activity may compensate
for deficient complex function. Conversely, decreasing such a co-enabler may
reduce an excessive GOF function. Matched sign-reversed controls were
evaluated.

An initial role inventory considered `ENABLES` and `CONTRIBUTES_TO`. Adding
direct target-driver physical interaction and top-three disease rank removed
all paths involving `CONTRIBUTES_TO`; only shared `ENABLES` annotations
remained.

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[:ENABLES]->(activity:MolecularActivity)
      <-[:ENABLES]-(driver:Gene)
MATCH (target)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]-(driver)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (driver)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
WHERE target <> driver
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND activity._miner_gene_enable_degree <= 58
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T172212Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Variant / direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R366 | LOF / increased | 20 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R367 | LOF / decreased control | 44 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R368 | GOF / decreased | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R369 | GOF / increased control | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

All branches are uncalibrated, and the LOF worsening branch is more than twice
as large as the nominal compensation branch.

Exact paths demonstrate that shared molecular function is not functional
redundancy:

- Cortisone-androgen insensitivity syndrome links increased NR3C1 to LOF AR
  because both enable estrogen response element binding and SIGNOR reports an
  interaction. Activating the glucocorticoid receptor does not replace
  androgen-receptor function.
- ATP-Werner syndrome links increased ABL1 to LOF WRN through bubble-DNA and
  four-way-junction DNA binding. Shared DNA binding does not restore WRN
  helicase activity.
- Dexamethasone-incontinentia pigmenti links IKBKB and IKBKG through
  transferrin-receptor binding, which is peripheral to the causal IKK-complex
  defect.
- Lithium-campomelic dysplasia links GSK3B and SOX9 through protein-kinase-A
  catalytic-subunit binding, again without substitution.
- Quercetin, Idelalisib, and Alpelisib-McCune-Albright syndrome all collapse
  to decreased PIK3R1, GOF GNAS, and shared insulin-like-growth-factor
  receptor binding. This is one pathway-proximity motif, not evidence that
  PIK3R1 is a co-enabler of the pathogenic GNAS function.

R366 and R368 are rejected with their controls. The direct interaction and
shared activity constraints make the paths coherent but do not establish
which catalytic or structural role is deficient.

Graph limitation: a useful version of this rule needs explicit protein-complex
membership with subunit roles, functional redundancy or genetic rescue,
stoichiometry, and an activity annotation specific to the disease mechanism.
The absence of any direct-interaction path using `CONTRIBUTES_TO` is a
specific coverage gap.

## Batch 63: GO-CAM Negative-Upstream Driver Edges

Hypothesis: a drug can restore a LOF driver by decreasing an upstream negative
regulator, or suppress a GOF driver by increasing an upstream negative
regulator. GO-CAM provides 29 explicit gene-to-gene
`ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_NEGATIVE_EFFECT` edges.

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[negative:ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_NEGATIVE_EFFECT]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (driver)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
WHERE target <> driver
  AND 'infores:go-cam' IN coalesce(negative.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

All four sign branches have zero coverage. A staged aggregate audit found:

| Stage | Paths |
|---|---:|
| GO-CAM negative gene-gene edges | 29 |
| Edges whose driver has a GOF or LOF Gene2Phenotype disease | 4 |
| Plus a drug directly binding the upstream gene | 0 |

The family was not cataloged. The blocker is not disease genetics but
druggability of the four represented negative regulators. Relaxing direct
binding would replace a mechanistic intervention with a generic text-mined
effect and was not pursued.

Graph limitation: signed GO-CAM gene-gene coverage is extremely sparse and
does not intersect the graph's drug-target space for variant-qualified
drivers.

## Batch 64: Opposite-Direction Gates on Causal Modules

Hypothesis: the strongest repeated-evidence multi-target rules should not count
a causal gene if the same drug also has an increased
activity-or-abundance assertion for that gene.

Only two variants were tested to bound within-family tuning:

```cypher
AND NOT EXISTS {
  MATCH (subject)-[opposite:AFFECTS]->(gene)
  WHERE opposite.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
    AND opposite.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
    AND opposite.object_direction_qualifier = 'increased'
}
```

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T172734Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Parent | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R370 | R264 | 27 | 8 | 2 | 0 | 37.04% |
| R371 | R270 | 16 | 7 | 1 | 0 | 50.00% |

Blind-safe set comparison shows exact equality with both parents. The gate
changes which causal genes can satisfy the cardinality but not which
drug-disease pairs pass.

For the partition-safe Cannabidiol-epilepsy prediction, R370 now counts SCN8A
(rank 1, 13 decrease evidence items) and SCN2A (rank 2, six items), neither of
which has an increased activity-or-abundance assertion. Contradictory SCN1A is
not counted. Thus the same prediction has a cleaner executable explanation.

R370 and R371 are retained as mechanistic replacement variants, not new
Pareto points. No additional provenance, rank, count, or opposite-evidence
threshold was tested.

## Batch 65: Variant Direction Crossed With Molecular-Activity Ontology

Hypothesis: direct correction of a variant-qualified disease driver becomes
more specific when the driver has an ontology-native activity class. Three
classes were tested with matched sign-reversed controls: GOF kinase
suppression, GOF GTPase suppression, and LOF transmembrane-receptor
activation. Multi-source effect support was then tested once per corrective
branch. The GTPase branch received one ontology-child ablation because its
errors separated cleanly into RAS G proteins and tubulin-family GTPases.

Executable query template:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(driver)
MATCH (driver)-[:ENABLES]->(:MolecularActivity)
      -[:SUBCLASS_OF*0..5]->
      (:MolecularActivity {id: $activity_root})
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

The multi-source variants insert:

```cypher
UNWIND coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, []) AS effect_source
WITH subject, driver, object,
     count(DISTINCT effect_source) AS effect_source_count
WHERE effect_source_count >= 2
```

Parameters and runs:

- kinase: `GO:0016301`, GOF, decreased corrective / increased control;
- GTPase: `GO:0003924`, GOF, decreased corrective / increased control;
- G protein: `GO:0003925`, GOF, decreased corrective / increased control;
- receptor: `GO:0004888`, LOF, increased corrective / decreased control;
- broad classes:
  `results/runs/20260812T173141Z/summary.json`;
- multi-source corrective branches:
  `results/runs/20260812T173516Z/summary.json`;
- G-protein child and control:
  `results/runs/20260812T173807Z/summary.json`;
- multi-source receptor control:
  `results/runs/20260812T174047Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Branch | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R372 | GOF kinase decrease | 427 | 9 | 0 | 0 | 2.11% |
| R373 | GOF kinase increase control | 32 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R374 | GOF GTPase decrease | 45 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R375 | GOF GTPase increase control | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R376 | LOF receptor increase | 120 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R377 | LOF receptor decrease control | 219 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R378 | multi-source GOF kinase decrease | 267 | 8 | 0 | 0 | 3.00% |
| R379 | multi-source GOF GTPase decrease | 13 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R380 | multi-source LOF receptor increase | 84 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R381 | multi-source GOF G-protein decrease | 6 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R382 | multi-source GOF G-protein increase control | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R383 | multi-source LOF receptor decrease control | 102 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |

The kinase branch is broad and calibration is confined to one lung-cancer
cohort: Erlotinib, Brigatinib, Gefitinib, Afatinib, Ceritinib, Osimertinib,
Amivantamab, Necitumumab, and Lazertinib. Multi-source support removes only
one of those nine labels while retaining 259 unlabeled pairs. Examples such
as Dacomitinib-lung cancer are plausible, but the rule also extrapolates
kinase inhibitors across many rare GOF disorders. R372 and R378 are rejected:
the 3.00% lower-bound precision and zero DRH recovery do not justify the
large unresolved space.

The generic GTPase branch separates into two mechanisms. Sotorasib and
Adagrasib suppress KRAS for Noonan syndrome 3 and cardiofaciocutaneous
syndrome 2, and Lonafarnib reaches the same disorders through RAS-family
targets. In contrast, vincristine, docetaxel, vinorelbine, cabazitaxel,
ixabepilone, Adcetris, and BB-10901 reach TUBB4A hypomyelinating
leukodystrophy through generic tubulin GTPase activity. Single-source
protease-inhibitor-to-KRAS paths are another false branch. Restricting the
activity ontology to `GO:0003925` removes the tubulin and protease-inhibitor
branches and leaves exactly six predictions:

- Sotorasib-Noonan syndrome 3;
- Sotorasib-cardiofaciocutaneous syndrome 2;
- Adagrasib-Noonan syndrome 3;
- Adagrasib-cardiofaciocutaneous syndrome 2;
- Lonafarnib-Noonan syndrome 3;
- Lonafarnib-cardiofaciocutaneous syndrome 2.

An exact provenance audit of these already partition-safe predictions found
that Sotorasib and Adagrasib each have KRAS decreased-activity assertions
from ChEMBL, DGIdb, DrugCentral, and GtoPdb, plus a TTD assertion not used to
reveal target status. Lonafarnib has independent KRAS assertions from ChEMBL
and GtoPdb; GtoPdb also asserts HRAS and NRAS effects. R381 is retained as a
compact, high-mechanism, uncalibrated candidate. R374 and R379 are rejected
as noisy parents. R382 is an empty matched worsening control.

The LOF receptor branch has biologically coherent examples:
Teriparatide-PTH1R for primary failure of tooth eruption and Blomstrand
chondrodysplasia, Glycine-GLRA1 for hyperekplexia, and Zolpidem-GABRG2 for
familial febrile seizures. It also produces broad sets of GABAergic sedatives
for rare epilepsy syndromes. The multi-source worsening control is larger
than the corrective branch, 102 versus 84 pairs, but the sets are completely
disjoint. Worsening-only examples include endothelin antagonists for EDNRB
LOF disease, neuromuscular blockers for CHRNG disease, and kinase inhibitors
for receptor LOF disorders. R376 is rejected as the broad parent. R380 is
retained only as an exploratory, uncalibrated high-mechanism family; it is not
treated as Pareto evidence. R377 and R383 remain rejected controls.

Graph limitations:

- visible treatment labels scarcely cover rare variant-defined disorders, so
  mechanistically precise GOF/LOF rules can have no calibration signal;
- `AFFECTS` direction does not distinguish full agonism, partial agonism,
  positive allosteric modulation, inverse agonism, and context-dependent
  pathway activation;
- source counts can include databases that imported overlapping assertions
  and therefore do not guarantee independent experiments;
- broad GO activity classes conflate pharmacologically distinct families;
- the graph lacks mutation-specific drug sensitivity, dosage, tissue
  exposure, developmental timing, and whether restoring partial receptor
  activity is sufficient for a congenital LOF disease.

## Batch 66: Genetic Rescue, Process Signatures, Complexes, and Dual-Arm PPI

This batch deliberately sampled four different mechanisms rather than tuning
the preceding activity-class rules.

### Genetic-interaction rescue blocker

Hypothesis: inhibiting a genetic suppressor or synthetic-rescue partner of a
disease driver could provide a more meaningful gene-interaction rule than
generic physical proximity.

The relationship-type inventory contains no genetic-interaction, suppressor,
rescue, or epistasis predicate. The only interaction predicates are
`INTERACTS_WITH`, `PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH`, and
`DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH`. The fallback audit was:

```cypher
MATCH (:Gene)-[r:INTERACTS_WITH]-(:Gene)
RETURN count(r), collect(DISTINCT r.predicate),
       collect(DISTINCT keys(r))
```

It found 4,042 generic gene-gene edges: 4,040 from SemMedDB and two from
DGIdb. Every edge has exact predicate `biolink:interacts_with`; all aspect and
direction qualifiers are null. The family was blocked without candidate
generation. Treating literature-derived generic interaction as genetic rescue
would be a semantic error.

Graph limitation: this build lacks signed genetic interaction, synthetic
lethality, suppressor screens, allele context, cell line, and phenotype-level
rescue evidence.

### Signed process-correlation reversal

Hypothesis: a CTD negative drug-process correlation could act as a
signature-reversal signal when the process is compact, supported by multiple
disease genes, and contains a top-three causal driver. Positive correlation
was the matched control.

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[correlation:NEGATIVELY_CORRELATED_WITH]->
      (process:BiologicalProcess)
WHERE 'infores:ctd' IN
      coalesce(correlation.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
MATCH (object:Disease)-[support:MINER_DISEASE_PROCESS_SUPPORT]->(process)
MATCH (driver:Gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
MATCH (driver)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)
WHERE evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND support.gene_count >= 2
  AND support.gene_count <= 20
  AND process._miner_gene_member_degree <= 100
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

The negative-correlation branch has zero paths and zero pairs. Replacing the
relationship with `POSITIVELY_CORRELATED_WITH` produces four paths and four
pairs. The family was not cataloged. Weakening the disease-process anchor
would turn the rule into a one-edge CTD literature association and was not
pursued.

The broader predicate audit also found 26,057 heterogeneous `DISRUPTS` edges,
all unqualified SemMedDB assertions, and 17,120
`PREDISPOSES_TO_CONDITION` edges, also all SemMedDB and spread across drugs,
genes, diseases, phenotypes, procedures, and behaviors. Neither predicate is
safe as a mechanistic intervention or hard adverse gate in this build.

### Protein-complex membership blocker

Hypothesis: explicit protein-complex membership could support component-level
intervention rules more faithfully than shared GO cellular-component
annotation.

`MEMBER_OF` contains 58,612 Panther gene-family edges and no protein-complex
membership. Gene-related `HAS_PART` contains 9,044 SemMedDB rows and only a
bounded GO-CAM residue. Inspection of every GO-CAM row found mostly
normalization-induced self-loops, including Ddx1-Ddx1, Atf4-Atf4,
Bmpr1a-Bmpr1a, TP53-TP53, and CHEBI:33695-CHEBI:33695. The only useful
non-self structural row was the V-type ATPase complex having the V1 domain.
No rule was cataloged.

Graph limitation: explicit complexes, subunit membership, stoichiometry,
obligate versus transient assembly, and disease-specific complex activity are
missing.

### Dual-target dual-driver physical bridge

Hypothesis: two distinct inhibited drug targets that independently contact two
different top-three causal drivers avoid the one-hub failure of the prior
single-connector family.

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target1:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect1:AFFECTS]->(target1)
MATCH (target1)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]-(driver1:Gene)
MATCH (driver1)-[evidence1:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target2:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect2:AFFECTS]->(target2)
MATCH (target2)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]-(driver2:Gene)
MATCH (driver2)-[evidence2:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
WHERE target1.id < target2.id
  AND driver1.id < driver2.id
  AND target1 <> driver1
  AND target2 <> driver2
  AND effect1.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect1.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect1.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND effect2.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect2.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect2.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND evidence1._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND evidence2._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

R384 uses decreased target effects, R385 is the increased-effect control, and
R386 adds:

```cypher
MATCH (driver1)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process:BiologicalProcess)
      <-[:INVOLVED_IN]-(driver2)
WHERE process._miner_gene_member_degree <= 100
```

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T175143Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Variant | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R384 | two inhibited arms | 187 | 6 | 1 | 0 | 3.74% |
| R385 | two activated arms control | 9 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R386 | R384 plus compact shared process | 84 | 4 | 0 | 0 | 4.76% |

R384's visible examples are Donepezil-dementia/Alzheimer disease,
Abemaciclib-breast cancer, Sunitinib-Plexosarcoma, and
Sorafenib/Regorafenib-liver carcinoma. Its sole DRH-exclusive hit is
Sorafenib-thyroid cancer. The novel space is dominated by Quercetin across
neurodegeneration, cancer, cardiovascular disease, immune disease, and
aneuploidy. R386 retains the same broad Quercetin and Trilaciclib patterns.

Exact path audits show why the apparent two-arm independence is insufficient:

- Sorafenib-thyroid cancer uses RET and BRAF drivers reached through
  combinations of MAPK1, MAPK3, MAPK14, MAP2K1, and ABL1. The target
  interaction degrees are 34-262.
- Donepezil-familial Alzheimer disease uses BACE1-APP plus GSK3B-PSEN1;
  BACE1 has degree seven but GSK3B has degree 269.
- Quercetin-Alzheimer disease uses BACE1-APP plus PRKCA-PSEN1 or
  PRKCB-PSEN1; PRKCA and PRKCB have degrees 196 and 63.
- Every inspected target-direction assertion is from
  `infores:text-mining-provider-targeted`.

One predeclared degree ablation attempted to require both targets to have at
most the graph p99 of 50 direct gene-interaction partners. The base and
compact-process queries both ran for more than 90 seconds because Neo4j
evaluated correlated degree counts inside the large join; both were
terminated. An early-filtered serialized rewrite also exceeded 60 seconds and
was terminated. No alternate threshold was tried and no degree feature was
materialized for this rejected family.

Two catalog smoke checks also failed during implementation: the first passed a
string where `load_rules` requires a `Path`; the second attempted to read a
source-only `family` attribute from the compiled `Rule` object. The actual
compiler test, Ruff check, and evaluator all succeeded.

R384-R386 are rejected. The family is directionally separated from its
control, but the only validation hit is the same hub-driven
Sorafenib-thyroid-cancer motif seen in earlier connector rules, and the shared
process does not repair target promiscuity or single-channel effect evidence.

### Pareto checkpoint after Batch 66

After rejection-status synchronization:

| Surface | Accepted | Raw |
|---|---:|---:|
| global visible | 4 | 32 |
| global validation | 4 | 9 |
| treatment-free visible | 17 | 18 |
| treatment-free validation | 8 | 10 |

Two initial `jq` commands failed because they referenced `.rules` instead of
the library's `.results` key. A second count expression used comma and pipe
precedence incorrectly, emitted a partial number, and then failed. The final
object-valued expressions above completed correctly.

## Batch 67: Holdout-Only Preclinical Edges and Mutant-Form Pharmacology

### Preclinical translation remains blind-blocked

The earlier preclinical family was re-audited because graph coverage had
increased. The build now contains 275 `IN_PRECLINICAL_TRIALS_FOR` edges:
215 from `infores:ttd` and 60 from `infores:drug-repurposing-hub`. There is
still no training-safe source. TTD identities remain blind and DRH is the
validation partition, so the predicate cannot be used as a feature without
label leakage. No identities were inspected and no rule was cataloged.

### Mutant-form pharmacology

Hypothesis: a pharmacologic edge explicitly targeting a mutant protein form
can be joined to an explicit GOF or LOF disease-driver direction, avoiding the
generic gene-level mismatch in ordinary target rules.

Only 14 Drug-to-Gene `AFFECTS` edges and the same 14
`DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH` edges carry
`object_form_or_variant_qualifier = 'mutant_form'`. All are human ChEMBL
assertions with explicit direction and mechanism:

- Adagrasib and Sotorasib decrease mutant KRAS activity.
- Dabrafenib, Encorafenib, and Vemurafenib decrease mutant BRAF activity.
- Lazertinib and Osimertinib decrease mutant EGFR activity.
- Enasidenib and Ivosidenib decrease mutant IDH2 and IDH1 activity.
- Deutivacaftor, Elexacaftor, Tezacaftor, and Vanzacaftor increase mutant CFTR
  activity; Lumacaftor increases mutant CFTR abundance.

Aggregate Gene2Phenotype joins produced:

| Branch | Pairs |
|---|---:|
| GOF driver plus mutant activity decrease | 15 |
| GOF driver plus mutant activity increase control | 0 |
| LOF driver plus mutant activity increase | 0 |
| LOF driver plus mutant abundance increase | 0 |

The CFTR branches are blocked because the represented CFTR disease
associations do not carry `loss_of_function_variant_form`.

R387 and R388 implement:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->
      (driver:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(driver)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE binding.object_form_or_variant_qualifier = 'mutant_form'
  AND effect.object_form_or_variant_qualifier = 'mutant_form'
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'gain_of_function_variant_form'
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T180219Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R387 | mutant GOF activity decreased | 15 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 13.33% |
| R388 | mutant GOF activity increased control | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |

The two training examples are Osimertinib-lung cancer and
Lazertinib-lung cancer. All 13 partition-safe predictions are RASopathy
transfers:

- Sotorasib and Adagrasib to Noonan syndrome 3 and cardiofaciocutaneous
  syndrome 2 through KRAS.
- Vemurafenib, Dabrafenib, and Encorafenib to cardiofaciocutaneous syndrome 1,
  Noonan syndrome 7, and LEOPARD syndrome 3 through BRAF.

This initially appeared to be one of the campaign's cleanest mechanistic
families. Exact raw-payload audit invalidated that interpretation. For
Sotorasib, Dabrafenib, and Osimertinib, the ChEMBL edge retains only the
canonical UniProt target and generic `mutant_form`; the `raw_json` contains no
allele identifier, residue, mutation class, or variant CURIE. The rule
therefore assumes that a drug directed at one mutant allele acts on every GOF
allele in that gene. In practice, this abstraction cannot distinguish KRAS
G12C-directed pharmacology from Noonan-associated KRAS variants or BRAF
V600-directed pharmacology from RASopathy variants.

R387 is rejected despite its compact size, directional control, and 13.33%
visible lower-bound precision. R388 remains the empty rejected control.

Graph limitation: variant-form qualifiers must preserve allele identity or a
normalized variant class on both drug-target and gene-disease edges. A generic
`mutant_form` flag is insufficient for mutation-specific therapeutic
inference and can make a false rule look exceptionally mechanistic.

## Batch 68: Anatomy-Qualified Pharmacology and Explicit Mechanisms

### Drug-effect anatomical qualifiers

Hypothesis: use anatomical context carried directly on a signed drug-to-gene
effect, rather than inferring exposure from a separate drug-location edge, and
require that context to match an anatomy associated with the disease.

The graph contains 1,169 Drug-to-Gene `AFFECTS` edges with
`anatomical_context_qualifier`; 1,061 are from SIGNOR and 108 from ChEMBL.
The property is a JSON-serialized string rather than a relationship or a
normalized scalar. Its highest-frequency values include:

| Raw value | Edges |
|---|---:|
| `[""]` | 676 |
| `["BTO:0000246"]` | 119 |
| `["BTO:0000007"]` | 48 |
| `["UBERON:0002107"]` | 35 |
| `["BTO:0002524"]` | 31 |
| `["UBERON:0000948"]` | 29 |
| `["BTO:0000150"]` | 17 |
| `["BTO:0000298"]` | 16 |
| `["UBERON:0000955"]` | 12 |

Only 108 qualified effects use UBERON identifiers, covering 16 contexts.
The largest are liver with 35 effects, heart with 29, brain with 12, blood
with eight, and kidney with five. The disease side is much broader: 48,234
`LOCATED_IN` or `OCCURS_IN` rows cover 1,215 anatomy nodes and 5,673 diseases.

The exact-match probe was:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(driver)
MATCH (driver)-[association:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (object)-[:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->(anatomy:AnatomicalEntity)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND effect.anatomical_context_qualifier =
      '["' + anatomy.id + '"]'
  AND association._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN count(DISTINCT subject.id + '|' + object.id) AS pairs
```

Both decreased and increased directions returned zero pairs. A bounded
ontology-aware variant also returned zero:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(driver)
MATCH (driver)-[association:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (object)-[:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->(disease_anatomy:AnatomicalEntity)
MATCH (effect_anatomy:AnatomicalEntity)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND effect.anatomical_context_qualifier =
      '["' + effect_anatomy.id + '"]'
  AND (
    effect_anatomy = disease_anatomy
    OR EXISTS {
      MATCH (effect_anatomy)-[:SUBCLASS_OF|PART_OF*1..3]->
            (disease_anatomy)
    }
    OR EXISTS {
      MATCH (disease_anatomy)-[:SUBCLASS_OF|PART_OF*1..3]->
            (effect_anatomy)
    }
  )
  AND association._miner_disease_rank <= 3
RETURN count(DISTINCT subject.id + '|' + object.id) AS pairs
```

No rule was cataloged. The failure is intersection sparsity plus vocabulary
fragmentation: most meaningful drug contexts are BTO identifiers, disease
locations are predominantly UBERON, empty strings account for 58% of the
qualified effects, and contexts are embedded as JSON text rather than graph
entities. A BTO-to-UBERON crosswalk and normalized context relationships are
required before this feature can localize pharmacology.

### Explicit pharmacologic mechanism correction

Hypothesis: explicit mechanism types such as inhibition, agonism, channel
blockage, and activation are a more faithful correction signal than a generic
signed `activity_or_abundance` effect.

This probe also exposed a Neo4j semantic-closure issue. Earlier rules requiring
`object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'` do not match the more
specific `activity` qualifier. Predicate closure was materialized for mining,
but qualifier-enum closure was not. The explicit-mechanism rules therefore use
the stored `activity` value directly.

The graph contains 10,891 inhibition/decreased effects, 2,979
agonism/increased effects, 1,939 antagonism/decreased effects, 1,122 channel
blockage effects, 752 positive-modulation effects, and 705 activation effects.
R389-R392 use:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(driver)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND effect.causal_mechanism_qualifier IN $mechanisms
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run artifacts:
`results/runs/20260812T180832Z/summary.json` and
`results/runs/20260812T181155Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Mechanism branch | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R389 | GOF plus negative pharmacology | 827 | 10 | 0 | 0 | 1.21% |
| R390 | GOF plus positive control | 66 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R391 | LOF plus positive pharmacology | 238 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0.84% |
| R392 | LOF plus negative control | 911 | 2 | 0 | 1 | 0.22% |
| R393 | LOF plus exact activation | 30 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 6.67% |
| R394 | GOF plus exact activation control | 18 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |

The broad GOF branch is dominated by EGFR inhibitors transferred across lung
cancer subclasses and by Fx-1006A-TTR-hereditary amyloidosis. The broad LOF
branch includes Migalastat-Fabry disease and Sapropterin-phenylketonuria as
training hits, but its unlabeled space transfers sedatives and generic GABA
modulators across neurological disorders. R389-R392 are rejected.

R393 restricts the positive branch to
`causal_mechanism_qualifier = 'activation'`. Its two training hits are
Migalastat-Fabry disease and Sapropterin-phenylketonuria. Its 28
partition-safe predictions are:

- Ezogabine for KCNQ2 benign familial neonatal seizures and KCNQ5
  intellectual disability.
- Linaclotide for GUCY2C-deficient neonatal intestinal obstruction.
- Carglumic acid for CPS1 deficiency.
- Vosoritide for NPR2-related acromesomelic dysplasia.
- Lubiprostone for CLCN2 leukoencephalopathy and Riluzole for a KCNN2
  neurodevelopmental disorder.
- Glycine, taurine, ethanol, and beta-alanine for GLRA1 hyperekplexia, plus
  glycine for GLRB hyperekplexia.
- Retinoids for RARB microphthalmia, androgens for androgen insensitivity,
  glucocorticoids for SMO-related hypothalamic hamartoma, Levothyroxine for
  THRA hypothyroidism, and Alverine for HNF4A MODY.

Exact path audit shows that the stronger channel, enzyme, and guanylate-cyclase
examples are mixed with a generic receptor-agonism failure mode. The graph
does not distinguish a hypomorphic receptor that may respond to more ligand
from a null or ligand-binding-defective receptor that cannot. The same
unrepresented allele-responsiveness issue affects RARB, AR, THRA, SMO, and
some channel disorders.

The first exact-path command failed before graph execution because the shell
expanded Cypher `$pairs` to an empty string. A second attempt over-escaped the
parameter and sent a literal backslash to Neo4j. The third constructed the
parameter token inside Python and returned 90 source-distinct paths for all 28
already partition-safe pairs. These command-construction failures produced no
candidate identities beyond the safe inspector output.

R393 remains an exploratory candidate with a medium mechanism score, not a
validated rule. Its directional control is clean and several predictions are
biologically coherent, but application requires an assumption about residual
allele function that the graph cannot test. R394 remains the rejected control.

Graph limitations: qualifier-enum closure is absent; pharmacologic mechanisms
are source-dependent; effect edges do not preserve dose, tissue, allele,
residual activity, or whether activation is possible for a disease variant.

## Batch 69: Disease-Side-Effect Vetoes and Channel Opening

### Disease-valued side effects as negative evidence

Hypothesis: prior adverse gates underused 55,793 SIDER `HAS_SIDE_EFFECT`
edges whose objects are Disease nodes. A candidate should be rejected when the
target disease itself, or a more-specific disease beneath it, is recorded as a
side effect.

All 55,793 edges come from `infores:sider` and cover 2,412 Disease nodes. The
aggregate ablation was:

```cypher
CALL () {
  // The executable Cypher body of R250, R264, R268, or R270.
  <base_rule>
}
WITH DISTINCT subject_id, object_id
MATCH (subject:Entity {id: subject_id})
MATCH (object:Entity {id: object_id})
RETURN count(*) AS pairs,
       count(CASE WHEN EXISTS {
         MATCH (subject)-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]->(object)
       } THEN 1 END) AS exact,
       count(CASE WHEN EXISTS {
         MATCH (subject)-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]->(side_effect:Disease)
               -[:SUBCLASS_OF*1..2]->(object)
       } THEN 1 END) AS side_effect_more_specific,
       count(CASE WHEN EXISTS {
         MATCH (subject)-[:HAS_SIDE_EFFECT]->(side_effect:Disease)
         MATCH (object)-[:SUBCLASS_OF*1..2]->(side_effect)
       } THEN 1 END) AS side_effect_more_general
```

| Parent | Pairs | Exact | Side effect more specific | Side effect more general |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R250 | 37 | 5 | 2 | 28 |
| R264 | 27 | 5 | 2 | 19 |
| R268 | 22 | 3 | 1 | 19 |
| R270 | 16 | 3 | 1 | 15 |

Using a side-effect ancestor as adverse would remove 70-94% of each parent and
was rejected without threshold tuning. Exact and more-specific gates produced
identical candidate sets:

| Parent | Base train/DRH/pairs | Gated train/DRH/pairs |
|---|---:|---:|
| R250 | 11 / 2 / 37 | 8 / 2 / 32 |
| R264 | 8 / 2 / 27 | 4 / 2 / 22 |
| R268 | 9 / 1 / 22 | 6 / 1 / 19 |
| R270 | 7 / 1 / 16 | 4 / 1 / 13 |

For R264 the gate removes four established treatments and one unlabeled pair:

- Imatinib-Plexosarcoma.
- Imatinib-BCR-ABL1-positive chronic myelogenous leukemia.
- Dasatinib-BCR-ABL1-positive chronic myelogenous leukemia.
- Phenytoin-epilepsy.
- Propafenone-arrhythmia, the sole unlabeled pair.

The exact gate lowers R264 visible precision from 37.0% to 27.3%; R268 and
R270 show the same loss of positive calibration. SIDER has no publications on
these edges and records treatment toxicities under labels that can equal the
indication. The gate is rejected and no rule was cataloged. Disease-valued
side effects are useful annotations, but exact disease identity is not a hard
mechanistic contradiction.

### Explicit protein stabilization

Hypothesis: explicit stabilization and increased abundance of a directly bound
LOF driver should represent pharmacological-chaperone rescue more specifically
than generic positive activity.

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(driver:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(driver)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'increased'
  AND effect.causal_mechanism_qualifier = 'stabilization'
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'loss_of_function_variant_form'
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

This returns two pairs, both training treatments and no prediction:
Migalastat-GLA-Fabry disease and Miglustat-GAA-Pompe disease. The GOF
stabilization control returns six pairs, one training hit and five unresolved
or blind pairs. The corrective mechanism is precise but has no prediction
utility, so it was documented but not cataloged.

### Explicit channel opening

Hypothesis: opening a directly bound ion channel whose loss of function causes
disease is a high-specificity restoration mechanism.

R395 and its GOF worsening control R396 use the Batch 68 pharmacologic query
with `causal_mechanism_qualifier = 'opening'`. The separate
`molecular_channel_opening` value had zero LOF coverage.

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T182222Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Variant direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Novel or blind |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R395 | LOF corrective | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 2 |
| R396 | GOF worsening control | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 2 |

Blind-safe inspection confirmed that all four are target-free:

- R395 predicts Diazoxide for KCNJ11 familial hyperinsulinemic hypoglycemia.
- R395 predicts Lubiprostone for CLCN2 leukoencephalopathy with mild
  cerebellar ataxia and white-matter edema.
- R396 predicts Diazoxide for KCNJ8 Cantu syndrome.
- R396 predicts Diazoxide for KCNJ11 transient neonatal diabetes 3.

Every binding and opening edge in these paths is from ChEMBL. R395 is retained
as a high-mechanism exploratory candidate despite zero visible calibration:
the exact sign composition is strong and the control identifies the expected
adverse direction. R396 is rejected as the matched worsening control.

Graph limitation: channel opening is represented at canonical gene level and
does not state channel complex composition, tissue, allele sensitivity, dose,
or whether the disease variant leaves a pharmacologically responsive channel.

### Pareto checkpoint after Batch 69

The evaluator refreshed the complete library after R395-R396. Counting only
candidate rules that lie on each eligible surface gives:

| Surface | Candidate entries | All eligible entries |
|---|---:|---:|
| global visible | 4 | 32 |
| global validation | 4 | 9 |
| treatment-free visible | 17 | 18 |
| treatment-free validation | 8 | 10 |

R395 does not enter a calibrated Pareto surface because it has no visible hit.
It is retained for prediction quality and mechanism diversity, not empirical
dominance.

## Batch 70: Disease-Relative Network Centrality for Common Diseases

### SIGNOR direct-interaction module

Hypothesis: for a genetically heterogeneous disease, one inhibited causal
target can remain relevant if it is embedded in the disease-specific gene
module, avoiding the requirement that the drug hit several genes directly.

R397 selects a top-three causal target that directly physically interacts with
at least two other top-ten disease genes:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->
      (target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[target_evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->
      (object:Disease)
MATCH (target)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]-(neighbor:Gene)
      -[neighbor_evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
WHERE target <> neighbor
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND target_evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND neighbor_evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 10
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
WITH subject, target, object,
     collect(DISTINCT neighbor) AS neighbors,
     collect(DISTINCT binding) AS bindings
WHERE size(neighbors) >= 2
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

A predeclared neighbor-count check returned:

| Minimum disease-module neighbors | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 57 | 3 | 1 | 0 |
| 2 | 27 | 1 | 1 | 0 |
| 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

The two-neighbor examples are coherent but concentrated:

- Lapatinib and DRH-only Neratinib for breast cancer through ERBB2 connected
  to EGFR and EGF.
- A group of additional ERBB2 binders predicted for breast cancer.
- Sunitinib or Ruxolitinib for Parkinson disease through LRRK2 connected to
  SNCA and MAPT.
- Curcumin for Parkinson disease through SNCA connected to LRRK2 and PRKN.

R398 adds one evidence-independence ablation:

```cypher
AND coalesce(
      effect.evidence_count,
      size(coalesce(effect.publications, [])),
      1
    ) >= 2
...
WITH subject, target, object, neighbors,
     reduce(
       sources = [],
       relation IN bindings |
       reduce(
         accumulated = sources,
         source IN coalesce(relation.primary_knowledge_sources, []) |
         CASE
           WHEN source IN accumulated THEN accumulated
           ELSE accumulated + source
         END
       )
     ) AS binding_sources
WHERE size(binding_sources) >= 2
```

R399 uses the same evidence and network architecture with target ranks four
through ten as a relevance control.

Run artifact: `results/runs/20260812T182853Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Variant | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R397 | top-three target, two neighbors | 27 | 1 | 1 | 7.41% |
| R398 | R397 plus evidence consensus | 11 | 1 | 1 | 18.18% |
| R399 | rank 4-10 control plus consensus | 18 | 1 | 2 | 16.67% |

All 11 R398 safe pairs are one ERBB2-breast-cancer cohort. The visible pairs
are Lapatinib and DRH-exclusive Neratinib. Predictions include Tucatinib,
Margetuximab, Afatinib, Dacomitinib, Gefitinib, Ibrutinib, Acalabrutinib,
BB-10901, and Masoprocol. The multi-database binding requirement removes the
Parkinson cohort but does not make the rule disease-general.

R397 is rejected. R398 remains an exploratory common-disease candidate because
it operationalizes disease-relative network relevance and contains plausible
targeted predictions, but it does not beat the lower-ranked R399 control on
validation recall or precision. R399 is rejected as the control.

Graph limitation: the disease-specific module is derived from ranked
gene-disease evidence, but interaction direction, disease-state activity, and
whether a drug truly modulates the represented target remain incomplete. One
ERBB2 motif can appear as many predictions without providing independent
mechanistic validation.

## Batch 71: IntAct Physical Modules

Hypothesis: the much larger experimentally derived IntAct interaction network
can provide disease-module embedding missed by SIGNOR direct interactions.
The graph contains 1,038,373 traversals of IntAct
`PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH`, versus 32,709 SIGNOR direct-interaction
traversals.

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (target)-[target_evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->
      (object:Disease)
MATCH (target)-[interaction:PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]-(neighbor:Gene)
      -[neighbor_evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
WHERE target <> neighbor
  AND 'infores:intact' IN coalesce(interaction.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND target_evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND neighbor_evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 10
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
WITH subject, target, object, count(DISTINCT neighbor) AS module_neighbors
WHERE module_neighbors >= $minimum
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

| Minimum neighbors | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 2 | 53 | 6 | 1 | 0 |
| 3 | 9 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| 4 | 9 | 1 | 0 | 0 |

The two-neighbor branch recovers Neratinib-breast cancer, BRAF inhibitors for
melanoma or glioma, Tacrine-Alzheimer disease, and many related predictions.
However, inspected targets are global IntAct hubs: ERBB2 degree 789, APP 517,
BRAF 228, and SNCA 230. Requiring both neighbors to be top-three disease genes
returns 19 pairs, one training hit, and no DRH hit.

No degree threshold was tuned. A global cutoff would mainly remove meaningful
but highly studied proteins while optimizing against the same sparse labels.
The IntAct family is rejected because its apparent gain is hub connectivity,
not independent disease-module specificity.

## Batch 72: Confidence, Inheritance, Effect Provenance, and Colocalization

### Numeric confidence fields

The graph confidence properties are not globally comparable:

- All 991 SIGNOR binding edges with `has_confidence_score` equal 0.8.
- All 367 scored ChEMBL binding edges equal 9.
- ChEMBL effect scores are only 8 or 9 across 8,238 edges.
- All 1,061 scored SIGNOR effects equal 0.8.
- Materialized gene-disease evidence has no `has_confidence_score` or
  `diseases_confidence_score`.

No numeric confidence rule was tested. These values encode source-specific
scales or constants, so cross-source threshold optimization would be invalid.

### Allelic requirement as a residual-function proxy

Hypothesis: monoallelic LOF may leave wild-type protein that an activator can
potentiate, while biallelic LOF requires residual mutant function.

Applying Gene2Phenotype `allelic_requirement` to R393 and R395 produced:

| Mechanism | Allelic requirement | Pairs | Training | DRH |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| activation | HP:0000006 | 5 | 0 | 0 |
| activation | HP:0000007 | 18 | 1 | 0 |
| activation | HP:0001419 | 7 | 1 | 0 |
| opening | HP:0000007 | 2 | 0 | 0 |

The monoallelic activation subset has no calibration, the R393 training hits
occur in recessive and X-linked subsets, and both R395 predictions are
recessive. Inheritance does not state residual protein or pharmacologic
responsiveness, so no gate was promoted.

### Cross-agent directional-effect consensus

Hypothesis: a targeted-text-mined drug effect is more reliable when a manual
or assay-derived effect independently agrees on direction for the same
top-ranked disease gene. TTD and DRH effect sources were excluded.

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[text_effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (subject)-[independent_effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE text_effect <> independent_effect
  AND text_effect.agent_type = 'text_mining_agent'
  AND text_effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND text_effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND text_effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND independent_effect.agent_type IN ['manual_agent', 'automated_agent']
  AND independent_effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND independent_effect.object_aspect_qualifier IN
      ['activity', 'activity_or_abundance']
  AND independent_effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(independent_effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

The agreeing decrease branch returns 834 pairs, 69 training hits, four DRH
hits, and 34 contraindications. The independent-increase contradiction branch
returns 33 pairs, four training hits, two DRH hits, and two contraindications.
Its visible precision is higher, proving that agreement without disease
direction is not sufficient.

Adding explicit Gene2Phenotype direction gives:

| Branch | Pairs | Training | DRH |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| GOF corrective decrease | 422 | 8 | 0 |
| GOF worsening increase | 24 | 0 | 0 |
| LOF corrective increase | 45 | 0 | 0 |
| LOF worsening decrease | 280 | 0 | 0 |

The directional corrective branches remain uncalibrated or very low precision.
No rule was cataloged. Agent type distinguishes acquisition method, not assay
context, target engagement, dose, or disease-specific therapeutic direction.

### Colocalized signed cascade

Hypothesis: GOA `COLOCALIZES_WITH` can provide a more specific subcellular
constraint than generic component membership or `ACTIVE_IN`.

Replacing the R359 shared `ACTIVE_IN` component with a shared
`COLOCALIZES_WITH` component yields zero GOF pairs, three LOF-corrective pairs,
and one LOF-worsening pair. All four are Bloom syndrome through CHEK2
downregulation of BLM in the chromosome telomeric region:

- Brigatinib, Gefitinib, and Sunitinib occur in the corrective branch through
  decreased CHEK2.
- Sunitinib also occurs in the worsening branch through increased CHEK2.

The drug effects are targeted text-mining assertions and the regulation is one
SIGNOR publication. The same drug in both directions exposes unresolved
condition-specific contradiction. No rule was cataloged.

### Query-performance failures

Two redundant schema inventories were terminated after producing no rows:

- A GO-CAM gene-gene property aggregate ran for more than 70 seconds.
- An unbounded outgoing-drug relation/source/label aggregate ran for more than
  70 seconds.

Both were replaced by indexed relationship-type lookup and narrow
predicate-specific counts. No biological candidate query was abandoned.

### Pareto checkpoint after Batches 70-72

| Surface | Candidate entries | All eligible entries |
|---|---:|---:|
| global visible | 4 | 32 |
| global validation | 4 | 9 |
| treatment-free visible | 17 | 18 |
| treatment-free validation | 8 | 10 |

R398, R395, and R393 are retained for mechanism or prediction diversity but
do not enter a calibrated surface.

## Batch 73: Correlation, Predisposition, Disruption, and Signed Metabolites

### Exact drug-disease correlation

Hypothesis: an explicit negative drug-disease correlation may provide
orthogonal epidemiologic support for a mechanistic target rule, while positive
correlation is a matched adverse or indication-confounding control.

The graph contains 79 distinct negative-correlation drug-disease pairs and 512
positive-correlation pairs from ICEES, CTD, or COHD. The staged executable
probe was:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[correlation:CORRELATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE correlation.correlation_direction = $direction
  AND any(
    source IN coalesce(correlation.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:icees-kg', 'infores:ctd', 'infores:cohd']
  )
WITH DISTINCT subject, object
OPTIONAL MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
OPTIONAL MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
WITH subject, object, collect(DISTINCT gene) AS inhibited_genes
OPTIONAL MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
WHERE gene IN inhibited_genes
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
RETURN count(DISTINCT subject.id + '|' + object.id) AS pairs
```

Negative correlation retained 41 pairs after the inhibited-target stage and
zero after intersecting a top-three disease gene. Positive correlation retained
253 and then zero. The exact correlation predicates therefore do not intersect
the causal-target feature space in this build. No rule was cataloged.

Graph limitation: correlation edges are sparse, source-specific, and lack a
uniform representation of covariate adjustment, exposure direction, and
indication confounding.

### Predisposition and disruption as negative evidence

Hypothesis: explicit `PREDISPOSES` or `DISRUPTS` assertions can veto otherwise
promising causal-module predictions.

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:PREDISPOSES|DISRUPTS]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

`PREDISPOSES` overlapped none of R249, R264, R268, or R270. `DISRUPTS`
overlapped one of three R249 pairs, three of 27 R264 pairs, one of 22 R268
pairs, and one of 16 R270 pairs. Blind-safe inspected overlaps were:

- Cannabidiol-epilepsy, a novel R249 prediction.
- Imatinib-chronic myeloid leukemia, a training pair.
- Propafenone-arrhythmia, a novel pair.

All inspected disruption assertions were SemMedDB-derived. Since
`DISRUPTS` can describe desired therapeutic disruption as well as harm, it is
not a valid negative-evidence veto without a disrupted biological object,
direction, and clinical context. No rule was changed.

### Signed active-metabolite correction

Hypothesis: a parent drug can correct a directional genetic disease mechanism
through a distinct active metabolite, even when the parent itself lacks the
relevant target assertion. To keep holdout-derived feature paths out of this
test, selected binding and effect edges from TTD and DRH were excluded.
`HAS_METABOLITE` itself has 928 edges, all sourced from ChEMBL.

The first source-independent probe allowed `activity` and
`activity_or_abundance`:

| Branch | Exact activity | Activity or abundance |
|---|---:|---:|
| GOF corrective decrease | 6 | 2 |
| GOF worsening increase | 1 | 0 |
| LOF corrective increase | 2 | 1 |
| LOF worsening decrease | 7 | 4 |

The broader aspect added only targeted-text-mining paths, so R400-R403 retain
exact `activity` only:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:HAS_METABOLITE]->(metabolite:ChemicalEntity)
MATCH (metabolite)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->
      (driver:Gene)
MATCH (metabolite)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(driver)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE subject <> metabolite
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $drug_direction
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run artifact:
`results/runs/20260812T185251Z/summary.json`.

| Rule | Branch | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Unresolved/blind |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R400 | GOF corrective | 6 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 6 |
| R401 | GOF worsening control | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| R402 | LOF corrective | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 2 |
| R403 | LOF worsening control | 7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 7 |

All six R400 pairs are blind-safe novel predictions:

- Oxcarbazepine through eslicarbazepine and SCN9A for primary
  erythermalgia.
- Oxcarbazepine through eslicarbazepine and SCN4A for hyperkalemic periodic
  paralysis.
- Oxcarbazepine through eslicarbazepine and SCN5A for long QT syndrome 3.
- Oxcarbazepine through eslicarbazepine and SCN11A for hereditary sensory and
  autonomic neuropathy 7 and familial episodic pain syndrome.
- Thioridazine through mesoridazine and KCNH2 for short QT syndrome 1.

The oxcarbazepine paths have independent DGIdb binding and effect assertions.
The thioridazine path uses BindingDB binding and a CTD effect supported by
PMID:17042915. Some target pairs also have parallel TTD edges, but no selected
rule path uses those edges.

R401 predicts Carisoprodol through meprobamate and GABRD for complex
neurodevelopmental disorder. R402 predicts Carisoprodol through meprobamate
and GABRG2 for familial febrile seizures and through GABRA1 for generalized
epilepsy susceptibility. The seven R403 worsening controls include
Oxcarbazepine-Brugada syndrome, Thioridazine-long QT syndrome 2, and
Amitriptyline-EAST syndrome, all pharmacologically credible liabilities.

R400 and R402 remain high-mechanism exploratory candidates because the graph
composition is explicit and uses no treatment edges. Neither is empirically
calibrated, and R402 is outnumbered by its worsening control. R401 and R403 are
rejected controls.

Graph limitations: `HAS_METABOLITE` does not encode active concentration,
parent-to-metabolite conversion fraction, tissue exposure, dose, channel-state
dependence, or therapeutic index. Drug-target direction can be correct while
the parent drug remains clinically unsafe.

## Batch 74: Independent Disease-Pathway Corroboration

### Schema exploration and query failures

Four initial `UNION ALL` schema inventories failed immediately because aliases
were omitted from later branches. Corrected queries established:

- PathBank supplies 198,943 Pathway-to-Gene `HAS_PARTICIPANT` edges over
  30,192 `SMPDB:*` pathways.
- GeneticsKP supplies disease associations for 1,235 `REACT:*` and 234 `GO:*`
  pathway nodes.
- GOA supplies 26,222 `INVOLVED_IN` edges to 392 GO nodes also typed
  `Pathway`, in addition to 354,080 edges to other GO biological processes.
- There are zero graph-native `CLOSE_MATCH` links between PathBank pathways and
  GeneticsKP pathways.

The direct PathBank/GeneticsKP join, distinct-driver join, and multi-target join
all returned zero rows because the pathway identifier systems are disjoint. An
all-pairs exact-label crosswalk query was stopped after 60 seconds. This family
was not rescued with external mappings because applying a rule must require no
external information.

The graph-native GeneticsKP-GO/GOA join did work:

```cypher
MATCH (process:Pathway)-[path_evidence:GENETIC_ASSOCIATION]->
      (object:Disease)
MATCH (target:Gene)-[membership:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
WHERE process.id STARTS WITH 'GO:'
  AND 'infores:geneticskp' IN
      coalesce(path_evidence.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:goa' IN coalesce(membership.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
RETURN effect.object_direction_qualifier AS direction,
       count(DISTINCT subject.id + '|' + object.id) AS pairs
```
