```cypher
WHERE node.information_content IS NOT NULL
RETURN min(node.information_content),
       percentileDisc(node.information_content, 0.25),
       percentileDisc(node.information_content, 0.5),
       percentileDisc(node.information_content, 0.75),
       percentileDisc(node.information_content, 0.9),
       max(node.information_content),
       count(*)
```

The same query was run for `Gene` and `Drug`.

| Category | N | Min | p25 | Median | p75 | p90 | Max |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Disease | 43,468 | 32.9 | 95.4 | 100.0 | 100.0 | 100.0 | 100.0 |
| Gene | 95,098 | 59.8 | 92.8 | 95.4 | 100.0 | 100.0 | 100.0 |
| Drug | 1,998 | 13.4 | 92.8 | 95.4 | 100.0 | 100.0 | 100.0 |

No rule was cataloged. The score is heavily saturated and does not supply a
natural mechanistic boundary. Selecting a cutoff would be threshold tuning
against sparse labels rather than hypothesis-driven biology.

Graph limitation: the information-content calculation is not accompanied by
its ontology, corpus, frequency definition, or uncertainty. Values at the
upper ceiling cannot rank most diseases and many genes or drugs.

## Batch 94: Antibody target-access localization ablation

Hypothesis: inhibitory-antibody rules should require the GOF target to be
physically accessible at the plasma membrane or extracellular region.

The first aggregate query placed the base antibody match outside a correlated
subquery and therefore repeated each aggregate row once per base pair. The
reported values were internally identical, but the query was rewritten so
each relation/root combination is evaluated once.

Corrected executable coverage query:

```cypher
UNWIND ['PART_OF', 'ACTIVE_IN', 'COLOCALIZES_WITH'] AS location_relation
UNWIND ['GO:0005886', 'GO:0005576', 'GO:0016020'] AS root
CALL (location_relation, root) {
  MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->
        (driver:Gene)
  MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(driver)
  MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
  MATCH (driver)-[location]->(component:CellularComponent)
        -[:SUBCLASS_OF*0..5]->(:CellularComponent {id: root})
  WHERE type(location) = location_relation
    AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
    AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity'
    AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
    AND effect.causal_mechanism_qualifier = 'antibody_inhibition'
    AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
        coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
        'gain_of_function_variant_form'
    AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
    AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
    AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
  RETURN count(DISTINCT [subject.id, object.id]) AS pairs,
         count(DISTINCT driver) AS drivers,
         count(DISTINCT component) AS components
}
RETURN location_relation, root, pairs, drivers, components
ORDER BY location_relation, root
```

Results:

| Location relation | Ontology root | Pairs | Drivers |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| `ACTIVE_IN` | plasma membrane (`GO:0005886`) | 6 | 3 |
| `ACTIVE_IN` | membrane (`GO:0016020`) | 6 | 3 |
| `ACTIVE_IN` | extracellular region (`GO:0005576`) | 0 | 0 |
| `PART_OF` | any tested root | 0 | 0 |
| `COLOCALIZES_WITH` | any tested root | 0 | 0 |

The plasma-membrane constraint preserves all six R441 pairs and therefore
changes no predictions or metrics. No duplicate catalog rule was added.

Interpretation: GOA `ACTIVE_IN` independently supports accessibility for EGFR,
MET, and PDGFRA, strengthening R441 qualitatively. The alternate component
predicates are too sparse to serve as required access evidence.

Graph limitation: `ACTIVE_IN` at plasma membrane does not state extracellular
epitope availability, receptor conformation, tumor-specific expression, or
antibody penetration. Absence of a `PART_OF` annotation is missing data rather
than evidence of intracellular localization.

## Batch 95: Disease-module target-selectivity ratio

Hypothesis: for common diseases, a drug should be more credible when most of
its directly bound and decreased targets belong to the disease's top-ten gene
module. This normalizes disease coverage by drug polypharmacology rather than
using an absolute target-degree cutoff.

One predeclared categorical split was used:

- all inhibited targets are top-ten disease genes;
- at least half are top-ten disease genes;
- fewer than half are top-ten disease genes.

Executable blind-safe candidate query:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 10
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
WITH subject, object,
     count(DISTINCT gene) AS disease_targets,
     subject._miner_direct_decreased_bound_target_degree AS total_targets
WHERE disease_targets >= 2
  AND total_targets IS NOT NULL
  AND total_targets >= disease_targets
RETURN subject, object, disease_targets, total_targets,
       CASE
         WHEN disease_targets = total_targets THEN 'all'
         WHEN disease_targets * 2 >= total_targets THEN 'majority'
         ELSE 'minority'
       END AS selectivity
```

The returned pairs were then partitioned with the standard collapsed treatment
source logic.

| Selectivity | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| all targets in disease module | 4 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| majority in disease module | 23 | 4 | 1 | 0 | 21.74% |
| minority in disease module | 69 | 12 | 2 | 1 | 20.29% |

The majority criterion barely changes precision, loses most positives, and
does not improve validation precision materially. Fully selective drugs have
no visible treatment support. No rule was cataloged and no ratio threshold
was tuned.

Graph limitation: the denominator counts only graph-represented direct
binding plus decreased effects, not the complete pharmacologic target
profile. Disease modules are also incomplete and rank-dependent, so the ratio
combines two coverage biases rather than measuring true selectivity.

## Batch 96: Cross-driver directional conflict blocker

Hypothesis: an otherwise plausible drug-disease mechanism should be vetoed
when the same drug has an adverse signed effect on a distinct
variant-qualified driver for that disease:

- decreasing a Gene2Phenotype LOF driver;
- increasing a Gene2Phenotype GOF driver.

The biological parent was fixed as R404. The first conflict branch added:

```cypher
EXISTS {
  MATCH (subject)-[:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->
        (conflict_driver:Gene)
  MATCH (subject)-[conflict_effect:AFFECTS]->(conflict_driver)
  MATCH (conflict_driver)-[conflict_association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object)
  WHERE conflict_effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
    AND conflict_effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
    AND conflict_effect.object_direction_qualifier = 'decreased'
    AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
        coalesce(conflict_association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    AND conflict_association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
        'loss_of_function_variant_form'
}
```

The symmetric branch changed direction to `increased` and variant form to
`gain_of_function_variant_form`.

Both branches returned zero conflicts: all 45 R404 pairs remain in the
conflict-false cell, with 13 training hits, zero DRH hits, and two
contraindications. No rule was cataloged.

Graph limitation: common diseases in R404 rarely have a second
Gene2Phenotype edge with explicit GOF/LOF direction that is also directly
targeted by the same drug. The negative mechanism is biologically meaningful
but cannot be applied from this graph build.

## Batch 97: Preserved Drug raw-JSON property inventory

Hypothesis: the preserved `raw_json` field may contain source properties not
exported as first-class Neo4j properties, such as maximum clinical phase,
molecule type, approval year, or therapeutic flag.

All 5,326 Drug nodes were read locally and parsed as JSON. No parse failures
occurred. Top-level key coverage:

| Key | Drug nodes |
|---|---:|
| category | 5,326 |
| equivalent identifiers | 5,326 |
| id | 5,326 |
| name | 5,326 |
| information content | 1,998 |
| synonym | 1,814 |
| xref | 1,736 |
| description | 1,533 |
| ChEMBL availability type | 1,440 |
| ChEMBL natural product | 874 |
| ChEMBL black-box warning | 467 |
| ChEMBL prodrug | 142 |

No hidden approval-phase, molecule-type, first-approval, route, or therapeutic
flag property exists in the preserved node JSON. The useful ChEMBL fields are
already exported as first-class properties. No derived feature or rule was
added.

The explicit prodrug property is already used by R138-R140 and therefore was
not retested as a new mechanism.

Graph limitation: the KGX node payload has already discarded most source-level
drug-development metadata. `raw_json` preserves the normalized node, not the
complete upstream ChEMBL record.

## Batch 98: Disease-causal process and tissue-action endpoint probe

Hypothesis: direct signed drug effects on a biological process explicitly
caused by a disease could support a process-level corrective rule without
requiring a gene intermediary. A parallel anatomy branch asked whether signed
drug effects on a disease location could provide tissue-level positive or
negative evidence.

The first exploratory query combined endpoint inventory, drug-process
inventory, process joins, drug-anatomy inventory, and disease-anatomy joins in
one Python invocation. The unconstrained join branch:

```cypher
MATCH (d:Drug)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(p:BiologicalProcess)
MATCH (x)-[cause:CAUSES]->(y)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND ((x:Disease AND y = p) OR (x = p AND y:Disease))
RETURN CASE WHEN x:Disease
       THEN 'disease_to_process' ELSE 'process_to_disease' END AS orientation,
       effect.object_aspect_qualifier AS aspect,
       effect.object_direction_qualifier AS direction,
       count(DISTINCT [d, CASE WHEN x:Disease THEN x ELSE y END]) AS pairs
```

did not return within 60 seconds and was interrupted. The disjunctive,
unanchored `CAUSES` pattern prevented a useful indexed expansion; this query
shape will not be reused.

Partial aggregate results completed before the interruption:

- Disease-to-BiologicalProcess `CAUSES`: 1,178 edges from 828 diseases to only
  61 processes.
- Signed CTD Drug-to-BiologicalProcess `AFFECTS`: 19,087 increased edges from
  989 drugs to 1,886 processes.
- Signed CTD Drug-to-BiologicalProcess `AFFECTS`: 10,015 decreased edges from
  921 drugs to 1,296 processes.

The process overlap is sufficiently large to continue, but subsequent queries
must start from the 1,178 disease-causal process edges and use separate
orientation-specific branches. No candidate identities or blind-partition
labels were inspected.

The corrected staged joins returned:

| Causal orientation | Drug effect | Pairs | Drugs | Diseases | Processes |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| disease `CAUSES` process | decreased | 98 | 34 | 35 | 5 |
| disease `CAUSES` process | increased | 42 | 36 | 5 | 3 |
| process `CAUSES` disease | decreased | 2 | 2 | 1 | 1 |
| process `CAUSES` disease | increased | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |

All disease-process causal edges are from SemMedDB; all signed drug-process
effects are from CTD.

Two richer corroboration attempts were then tested. The first required a mined
disease gene to participate in the same disease-caused process:

```cypher
MATCH (object:Disease)-[:CAUSES]->(process:BiologicalProcess)
MATCH (driver:Gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
MATCH (driver)-[:INVOLVED_IN|
                  ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF|
                  ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_OR_WITHIN|
                  ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_POSITIVE_EFFECT|
                  ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_NEGATIVE_EFFECT|
                  ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_OR_WITHIN_POSITIVE_EFFECT|
                  ACTS_UPSTREAM_OF_OR_WITHIN_NEGATIVE_EFFECT]->(process)
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(process)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id
```

It returned zero pairs. Replacing the mined evidence edge with an explicit
Gene2Phenotype GOF/LOF association also returned zero.

The second corroborator required a process-to-phenotype edge to a phenotype of
the same disease. Its broad process/phenotype relationship inventory timed out
at 45 seconds; the fully constrained disease-process-drug-phenotype query
returned zero pairs. Thus the graph cannot currently connect these
SemMedDB disease-process claims to either disease genes or disease phenotypes.

The staged anatomy query found only nine signed pairs sharing a disease
location:

| Drug-anatomy effect | Pairs | Drugs | Diseases | Anatomies |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| increased | 6 | 3 | 2 | 1 |
| decreased | 3 | 2 | 3 | 2 |

The corresponding edges have no object-aspect qualifier, so “increased
anatomy” and “decreased anatomy” are not biologically interpretable. This
branch is not promoted to a rule. The 585,205 unsigned shared-location pairs
are dominated by SemMedDB/CTD co-mention structure and are unsuitable as a
mechanistic substitute.

Four executable variants were cataloged as R443-R446 and evaluated with the
blind-safe partitioner:

| Rule | Orientation and sign | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R443 | disease causes process; drug decreases | 56 | 3 | 0 | 2 | 5.36% |
| R444 | disease causes process; drug increases | 36 | 1 | 0 | 5 | 2.78% |
| R445 | process causes disease; drug decreases | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R446 | process causes disease; drug increases | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |

Run: `results/runs/20260812T204408Z/summary.json`.

R443 training examples were olmesartan-obesity, topiramate-obesity, and
orlistat-obesity. Novel examples included zidovudine-obesity
(contraindicated), hydrocortisone-obesity, dexamethasone-obesity,
amiodarone-obesity, iron-obesity, and curcumin-obesity. R444 retained only
caffeine-obesity as a training hit and produced several obesity
contraindications.

Aggregate path inspection showed that the family is almost entirely generated
by generic process hubs:

| Process | Sign | Pairs | Drugs | Diseases |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| lipogenesis | increased | 34 | 34 | 1 |
| wound healing | decreased | 26 | 2 | 13 |
| lipogenesis | decreased | 26 | 26 | 1 |
| defecation | decreased | 2 | 1 | 2 |
| micturition | increased | 2 | 2 | 1 |
| micturition | decreased | 2 | 2 | 1 |

Exact paths for already partitioned examples confirmed that the three R443
training hits and the sampled obesity predictions all pass through
`GO:0008610` lipogenesis. Zidovudine has both increased and decreased CTD
effects on that same process, demonstrating within-drug sign conflict. The
disease-to-lipogenesis assertion is reused from the same two SemMedDB
publications for every obesity candidate.

This failure is not repaired by a principled threshold: the body composes
literature-level process observations without context, dose, tissue, or
causal-gene/phenotype corroboration. R443-R446 were rejected rather than
split by individual process or sign-conflict filters.

## Batch 99: Etiologic mediator-production interception

Hypothesis: a drug can treat a disease by directly inhibiting a top-ranked
disease gene that produces a chemical mediator which causes the disease. This
adds an explicit producer-mediator mechanism to direct target correction:

```cypher
MATCH (producer:Gene)-[production:PRODUCES]->
      (mediator:ChemicalEntity)-[etiology:CAUSES]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (producer)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->
      (producer)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(producer)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Schema and staged coverage:

- 261 Gene-to-ChemicalEntity `PRODUCES` edges connect 120 genes to 151
  chemicals; every edge is from SemMedDB.
- Joining produced chemicals to ChemicalEntity-to-Disease `CAUSES` gives
  4,602 producer-mediator-disease paths over 100 genes, 103 mediators, and 489
  diseases; both biological edges are from SemMedDB.
- Before gene-disease corroboration, exact-activity decrease gives 3,500 pairs
  and increase gives 551.
- Requiring any mined gene-disease evidence reduces the decreased branch to
  343 pairs and the increased branch to 65.
- Restricting to disease-relative top-three evidence leaves 188 decreased and
  21 increased exact-activity pairs.
- An explicit Gene2Phenotype GOF association for the producer yields zero
  pairs, so variant direction cannot corroborate this family.

The top-three branch is strongly hub-concentrated. TNF production of reactive
oxygen species contributes 84 decreased pairs and TNF production of nitric
oxide contributes 70; VEGFA-to-reactive-oxygen-species contributes 40.
Additional mediators include a duplicate chemical representation of TNF-alpha,
Hioxyl, generic radical, messenger RNA, and ceramide. This indicates severe
SemMedDB abstraction and normalization noise, but the executable family is
retained for one blind-safe evaluation and direct overlap analysis rather
than being tuned by mediator identity.

R447 used exact activity decrease and R448 used exact activity increase as its
matched control.

| Rule | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R447 | 188 | 32 | 0 | 3 | 17.02% |
| R448 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |

Run: `results/runs/20260812T204853Z/summary.json`.

The exact-activity increase control is empty after top-three rank and source
exclusions, so the family has no usable sign calibration. R447 training
examples are dominated by TNF inhibitors across inflammatory diseases and
VEGF inhibitors for diabetic retinopathy; Prednisolone contributes a large
set of indications through TNF. Safe novel examples include
Etanercept-heart failure, Etanercept-septic shock, Etanercept-diabetes,
Sorafenib-proteinuria, Sorafenib-preeclampsia, and
Dapsone-coronary-microvascular-disorder.

A direct blind-safe comparison with R404 showed:

| Relation | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R447 only | 163 | 24 | 0 | 1 |
| intersection | 25 | 8 | 0 | 2 |
| R404 only | 20 | 5 | 0 | 0 |

Thus the mediator chain is not a precise refinement of the independent
GeneticsKP pathway rule. It broadens the same TNF/VEGF cohorts with lower
visible precision and no validation gain.

Exact-path inspection of already partitioned examples confirmed that:

- Etanercept-rheumatoid-arthritis uses TNF production of nitric oxide,
  reactive oxygen species, and a duplicate TNF-alpha chemical node.
- Etanercept-heart-failure uses the same TNF-to-nitric-oxide and
  TNF-to-reactive-oxygen-species chains, with multi-source drug inhibition but
  SemMedDB production and causation edges.
- Sorafenib-preeclampsia and Sorafenib-proteinuria expand through VEGFA
  production of nitric oxide or reactive oxygen species.
- Dapsone-coronary-microvascular-disorder expands through MPO production of
  generic radical and reactive oxygen species.

These paths show why publication counts or target-source filters would not
repair the rule: the failure is the lack of tissue, dose, temporal, and
protective-versus-pathogenic context on mediator production and causation.
R447 and R448 were rejected without mediator blacklists or additional
threshold tuning.

## Batch 100: Chemical derivation transfer and anatomy-produced mediators

### Chemical derivation mechanism transfer

Hypothesis: a Drug that `DERIVES_FROM` a chemical parent may inherit the
parent's signed top-three disease-target mechanism.

Executable aggregate query:

```cypher
MATCH (derived:Drug)-[derivation:DERIVES_FROM]->
      (parent:ChemicalEntity)
MATCH (parent)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(gene:Gene)
MATCH (parent)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE derived <> parent
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier IN
      ['activity', 'activity_or_abundance']
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier IN ['decreased', 'increased']
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN effect.object_aspect_qualifier,
       effect.object_direction_qualifier,
       count(DISTINCT [derived, object]) AS pairs
```

Coverage:

| Parent effect | Decreased | Increased |
|---|---:|---:|
| exact activity | 63 | 43 |
| activity or abundance | 27 | 22 |

All 350 Drug-to-ChemicalEntity derivation edges are SemMedDB-derived. The
relation is not a reliable directed active-moiety relation:

- 70 of 350 edges are reciprocal.
- Caffeine derives from theophylline while theophylline also derives from
  caffeine.
- Dronabinol derives from cannabidiol.
- Graphite derives from dopamine.
- Generic nodes such as `agonists`, `anticoagulants`, and
  `pharmaceutical preparations` are typed as Drugs and enter the candidate
  space.
- Some useful relations do occur, including phenytoin/fosphenytoin,
  phenobarbital/primidone, retinoids/vitamin A, and mycophenolic
  acid/mycophenolate mofetil, but their direction is not represented
  consistently.

Requiring the derived and parent compounds to have the same signed target
effect does not rescue the feature; it expands to 7,479 decreased and 5,417
increased pairs because broad literature effects are shared. No rule was
cataloged. The graph needs curated active-moiety, metabolite, precursor, and
structural-derivative predicates rather than one heterogeneous
`DERIVES_FROM` edge.

### Anatomy-produced mediator suppression blocker

Hypothesis: a drug can treat a disease by decreasing a chemical mediator
produced in an anatomy where the disease occurs.

```cypher
MATCH (object:Disease)-[:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->
      (anatomy:AnatomicalEntity)-[production:PRODUCES]->
      (mediator:ChemicalEntity)
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(mediator)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier IN ['decreased', 'increased']
RETURN effect.object_direction_qualifier,
       count(DISTINCT [subject, object]) AS pairs
```

This produces 679,443 decreased and 497,226 increased pairs. All production
and effect edges are SemMedDB-derived. Requiring the same mediator to
`CAUSES` the disease still leaves 18,030 decreased and 12,202 increased
pairs. The family is dominated by generic anatomy, mediator, and literature
effect hubs and was not cataloged.

## Batch 101: Anatomy-contextualized signed mediator antagonism

Hypothesis: the rejected genetically corroborated mediator-antagonism family
may become indication-specific if the etiologic mediator is produced by an
anatomy where the candidate disease occurs. This creates a seven-edge
nonlinear body combining chemical action, disease causation, top-three genetic
support, anatomy, mediator production, drug binding, and opposing drug action.

The added clause is:

```cypher
MATCH (object)-[:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->
      (anatomy:AnatomicalEntity)-[production:PRODUCES]->(mediator)
WHERE 'infores:semmeddb' IN
      coalesce(production.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
```

Aggregate directional cells before target partitioning:

| Mediator direction | Drug direction | Pairs | Drugs | Diseases | Mediators | Anatomies |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| increased | decreased | 3 | 3 | 1 | 1 | 2 |
| decreased | increased | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| increased | increased | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| decreased | decreased | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 5 |

All four cells are cataloged as R449-R452 so identities are inspected only
after blind-safe partitioning.

Blind-safe evaluation:

| Rule | Mediator | Drug | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Unresolved/blind |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R449 | increased | decreased | 3 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R450 | decreased | increased | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R451 control | increased | increased | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R452 control | decreased | decreased | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 |

Run: `results/runs/20260812T205556Z/summary.json`.

R449's three pairs are cyproterone-prostate-cancer,
enzalutamide-prostate-cancer, and nilutamide-prostate-cancer. The rule is
therefore perfectly precise on visible data but produces no candidate outside
the training partition. R452's sole novel reinforcement-control pair is
curcumin-Parkinson-disease.

A single predeclared ontology generalization allowed the disease anatomy and
mediator-producing anatomy to be identical or directly parent/child by one
`SUBCLASS_OF` edge:

```cypher
WHERE disease_anatomy = production_anatomy
   OR EXISTS {
        MATCH (disease_anatomy)-[:SUBCLASS_OF]->(production_anatomy)
      }
   OR EXISTS {
        MATCH (production_anatomy)-[:SUBCLASS_OF]->(disease_anatomy)
      }
```

It increased the number of supporting anatomy paths but did not add any
drug-disease pair in any directional cell. No arbitrary ancestry depth,
shared-ancestor degree, or anatomy blacklist was tested.

R449-R452 were rejected. R449 is a coherent retrospective explanation of
three known antiandrogen indications, but a 100%-precise rule with zero novel
or blind predictions is not useful for this campaign's prediction objective.
The graph would need additional curated tissue production and mediator-target
coverage to generalize the mechanism.

## Batch 102: Diagnostic evidence and therapeutic-comparator transfer

### Diagnostic relation blocker

Hypothesis: a diagnostic biomarker may provide orthogonal disease context for
a signed drug-target rule.

The 18,343 `DIAGNOSES` edges ending in Disease are almost entirely
non-molecular:

| Marker type | Edges | Markers | Diseases | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Procedure | 17,769 | 664 | 3,394 | SemMedDB |
| Other | 387 | 38 | 310 | SemMedDB |
| PhenotypicFeature | 153 | 29 | 108 | SemMedDB |
| ClinicalAttribute | 28 | 4 | 27 | SemMedDB |
| ChemicalEntity | 5 | 5 | 5 | Drug Repurposing Hub |
| ChemicalEntity | 1 | 1 | 1 | DrugCentral |

There are zero Gene-to-Disease diagnostic edges and therefore zero directly
targetable diagnostic-gene mechanisms. Signed drug effects on the few
chemical diagnostic markers produce four decreased and three increased
pairs, but all usable diagnostic edges come directly from Drug Repurposing
Hub. Using them would leak validation evidence into the rule body. No rule was
cataloged.

Graph limitation: diagnostic observations are represented primarily as
procedures and symptoms, not normalized molecular biomarkers with assay
direction, threshold, tissue, or causal interpretation.

### Therapeutic-comparator mechanism transfer

Hypothesis: a drug compared as a `CLOSE_MATCH` to another chemical may inherit
the comparator's signed top-three disease-target mechanism.

`CLOSE_MATCH` contains 113 edges, all from SemMedDB. Seventy-one are
Drug-to-Drug. Inspection shows that the predicate means literature-level
therapeutic or pharmacologic comparison rather than ontology equivalence:

- acetaminophen-ibuprofen and aspirin-ibuprofen;
- albuterol-formoterol and albuterol-salmeterol;
- alteplase-tenecteplase;
- acyclovir-valaciclovir;
- carbamazepine-oxcarbazepine;
- capecitabine-fluorouracil;
- doxorubicin-epirubicin;
- budesonide-fluticasone;
- enoxaparin-heparin/fondaparinux;
- reciprocal class-member pairs and generic class nodes also occur.

The executable candidate query is:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[similarity:CLOSE_MATCH]-
      (comparator:ChemicalEntity)
MATCH (comparator)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->
      (gene:Gene)
MATCH (comparator)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(gene)
MATCH (gene)-[evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object:Disease)
WHERE subject <> comparator
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND 'infores:semmeddb' IN
      coalesce(similarity.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Before target partitioning, exact activity decrease yields 45 pairs over 16
candidate drugs and 15 comparators; exact activity increase yields 15 pairs
over 12 drugs and 11 comparators. R453-R454 evaluate these two cells without
additional comparator classes or similarity thresholds.

### Batch 102 evaluation and disposition

Run: `results/runs/20260812T210048Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contraindicated | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R453 | comparator decreases target activity | 45 | 3 | 0 | 5 | 6.67% |
| R454 | comparator increases target activity | 15 | 2 | 0 | 1 | 13.33% |

R453 training examples were carbamazepine-epilepsy,
oxcarbazepine-epilepsy, and risperidone-ADHD inattentive type. Its safe novel
examples exposed the failure mode: carbamazepine-arrhythmia was
contraindicated; clarithromycin-long QT syndrome, clarithromycin-torsades de
pointes, and clarithromycin-arrhythmia transferred known macrolide liabilities;
aspirin-hyperglycemia, azithromycin-acute lung injury, and
bumetanide-metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease did not
form a coherent therapeutic set.

R454 training examples were ibuprofen-allergy and risperidone-ADHD. Novel
examples included heparin-hereditary antithrombin deficiency, which was
contraindicated, plus weak ontology or disease-gene transfers such as
ephedrine-Stargardt disease and epoprostenol-abetalipoproteinemia.

Ablation conclusion: reversing the signed molecular effect doubled visible
precision, so the proposed inhibitory direction did not carry the signal.
Both directions had zero DRH validation hits. The `CLOSE_MATCH` relation
therefore behaves as comparison, co-mention, class membership, or adverse
effect transfer rather than graph-native chemical equivalence. R453 and R454
were rejected without further threshold tuning.

## Pareto checkpoint after Batch 102

After rerunning R453-R454 with final rejected status, the executable library
contained 449 evaluated rules. The eligible surfaces were:

| Surface | Rules |
|---|---:|
| Global visible | 32 |
| Global validation | 9 |
| Treatment-free visible | 16 |
| Treatment-free validation | 10 |

The treatment-free visible surface still ended with R439, while the
treatment-free validation surface included R264, R265, and R370. Rejected
R453-R454 were excluded. This checkpoint used only aggregate flags from
`results/rule-library.json`; no blind-partition identities were inspected.

## Batch 103: Direct cellular-context effects

### Cell direction blocker

Hypothesis: a signed drug effect on a disease-localized cell could represent
direct depletion or restoration of a pathogenic cell population.

The graph has 2,280 `Drug-AFFECTS-Cell` edges over 543 drugs and 185 cells,
but none has a qualified predicate, aspect, or direction. Disease context is
available through 1,667 `Disease-LOCATED_IN-Cell` and 243
`Disease-DISRUPTS-Cell` edges, but without drug direction the mechanism cannot
distinguish therapeutic cell depletion, cell expansion, toxicity, or generic
study context. No rule was cataloged.

### Variant-aware cellular-component correction

The graph has 114 signed `Drug-AFFECTS-CellularComponent` edges, all from CTD:
89 increased and 25 decreased. Direct joins to disease `DISRUPTS` edges yield
only three increased and one decreased pair. Disease localization yields six
increased and three decreased pairs. The broader disease-component genetic
association join yields 286 increased and 68 decreased pairs but has no
disease direction.

The stronger hypothesis uses explicit Gene2Phenotype variant direction and
physical GO component membership:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(component:CellularComponent)
      <-[:PART_OF]-(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run: `results/runs/20260812T210844Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Variant/effect | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R455 | LOF / component increased | 11 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R456 | LOF / component decreased | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R457 | GOF / component decreased | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R458 | GOF / component increased | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

All 11 R455 pairs were available through the blind-safe novel partition.
Examples included Tyloxapol-familial apolipoprotein C-II deficiency, ferric
citrate-hereditary hyperferritinemia with congenital cataracts, three
CANDLE-syndrome predictions, and six dexamethasone predictions for unrelated
rare developmental disorders. R458's sole safe novel control was
dexamethasone-Baraitser-Winter syndrome 2.

The first exact-path audit failed before Cypher execution because the shell
expanded the unescaped `$pairs` query parameter to an empty string. No graph
rows were returned and no blind identities were exposed. The query was
corrected by escaping the parameter for the shell.

The corrected exact-path audit showed that every component effect came from
CTD and had a null aspect qualifier:

- Curcumin, Hioxyl, and thioctic acid increased the proteasome core complex
  containing PSMB8 for CANDLE syndrome.
- Tyloxapol increased the very-low-density lipoprotein particle containing
  APOC2 for familial apolipoprotein C-II deficiency.
- Ferric citrate increased the ferritin complex containing FTL for hereditary
  hyperferritinemia with congenital cataracts.
- Dexamethasone increased filamentous actin, transferring to six unrelated
  LOF developmental diseases through six different actin-associated genes.

The last group demonstrates component-hub transfer. The apparently more
specific examples still do not state whether CTD observed component abundance,
assembly, activity, localization, or a downstream marker. Increasing ferritin
complex is not self-evidently corrective for hyperferritinemia, and increasing
an entire proteasome is not equivalent to restoring mutant PSMB8 function.

R455-R458 were rejected. No component-degree threshold was tuned: the missing
effect aspect is a semantic blocker, not a ranking problem. The graph needs
component assembly/activity qualifiers and member-specific causal effects to
support this mechanism.

## Batch 104: Variant-aligned disruption of a disease-driver process

Hypothesis: a drug that directly binds and suppresses a GOF driver is more
credible when SemMedDB independently says the drug disrupts a biological
process containing that same driver. The LOF cells provide both a possible
restoration branch and a coherent adverse control.

The raw `Drug-DISRUPTS-BiologicalProcess` relation has 4,254 relevant edges,
all from SemMedDB. Joining only process membership and G2P genetics produced
997 GOF pairs and 5,889 LOF pairs, so process disruption alone was too broad.
Requiring direct binding and a signed effect on the same driver reduced the
four cells to:

| Cell | Pairs before target partitioning |
|---|---:|
| GOF driver decreased | 26 |
| GOF driver increased | 2 |
| LOF driver increased | 9 |
| LOF driver decreased | 36 |

The executable family is:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[disruption:DISRUPTS]->
      (process:BiologicalProcess)<-[:INVOLVED_IN]-(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (subject)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(driver)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(driver)
WHERE 'infores:semmeddb' IN
      coalesce(disruption.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier IN
      ['activity', 'activity_or_abundance']
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run: `results/runs/20260812T211509Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Cell | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R459 | GOF decreased | 26 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R460 | GOF increased control | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R461 | LOF increased | 9 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| R462 | LOF decreased control | 36 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

Safe R459 examples included several coherent targetopathies: lidocaine and
procaine for SCN9A GOF pain syndromes; bupivacaine, lidocaine, and procaine for
hyperkalemic periodic paralysis; ezogabine for an epileptic encephalopathy;
and sorafenib across multiple FGFR2 GOF developmental syndromes. Other
examples, such as acetylcholine for CHRNA4-associated epilepsy, remained
directionally suspect.

Safe R461 examples were mostly incoherent or adverse: progesterone,
testosterone, danazol, and flutamide for androgen insensitivity; atorvastatin,
lovastatin, and simvastatin for a limb-girdle muscular dystrophy; and
methotrexate for cerebral folate transport deficiency. Ezogabine for a KCNQ2
LOF neonatal seizure disorder was the main plausible exception.

Two comparison commands failed before graph execution because
`miner-compare-rules` does not support `--relation all`; its documented values
are `parent_only`, `child_only`, and `intersection`. The comparisons were
rerun with those explicit set operations.

The corrected set comparisons found:

| Comparison | Corrective only | Intersection | Worsening only |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| R459 vs R460 | 24 | 2 | 0 |
| R461 vs R462 | 3 | 6 | 30 |

The two contradictory R459/R460 pairs were lidocaine-primary erythermalgia
and ezogabine-developmental and epileptic encephalopathy 7. The R461-only set
contained only progesterone, testosterone, and danazol for androgen
insensitivity, while most statin, methotrexate, flutamide, and ezogabine pairs
also appeared in the LOF-worsening control.

Against the broad direct-GOF activity baseline R298, R459 had 19 intersection
pairs, seven `activity_or_abundance`-only pairs, and removed 842 baseline
pairs. All ten R298 training hits were in the removed set. R459 had no overlap
with the specialized voltage-gated-channel rule R302.

Exact safe R459 paths showed that the most coherent channel examples all used
SemMedDB `DISRUPTS` edges to GO `action potential`:

- Bupivacaine-SCN4A-hyperkalemic periodic paralysis used exact activity
  inhibition from ChEMBL, DGIdb, and Wombat-PK.
- Lidocaine-SCN9A-primary erythermalgia used exact activity inhibition from
  ChEMBL/DGIdb plus a targeted-text activity-or-abundance edge.
- Procaine-SCN9A-primary erythermalgia used DGIdb activity inhibition.
- Ezogabine-KCNQ2-DEE7 used targeted-text activity-or-abundance inhibition.

Sorafenib-FGFR2-Apert syndrome instead used broad SemMedDB disruption of
angiogenesis and epithelial-to-mesenchymal transition plus a targeted-text
FGFR2 effect. Acetylcholine-CHRNA4 epilepsy was also directionally suspect.

R459 remains an exploratory high-mechanism candidate because its 24-to-zero
sign-exclusive split and several channelopathy paths are compelling, but it
has no visible calibration and the process assertion removes every known
R298 treatment. R461 and R462 are rejected because the purported LOF
restoration is contradicted by its larger adverse control and by concrete
examples. No numeric threshold was tuned.

## Batch 105: Variant-aligned disruption of a driver-enabled activity

Hypothesis: replace broad biological-process disruption with a more specific
drug disruption of a molecular activity that the variant-defined driver
enables. Direct binding and a signed driver effect remain required.

Before target partitioning, the four cells were:

| Cell | Pairs | Activity concentration |
|---|---:|---|
| GOF driver decreased | 22 | kinase activity; EGFR activity subset |
| GOF driver increased | 4 | kinase activity |
| LOF driver increased | 2 | folic-acid binding; SCF receptor activity |
| LOF driver decreased | 6 | five distinct activities |

Executable family:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[disruption:DISRUPTS]->
      (activity:MolecularActivity)<-[:ENABLES]-(driver:Gene)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
MATCH (subject)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(driver)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(driver)
WHERE 'infores:semmeddb' IN
      coalesce(disruption.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier = $variant_form
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier IN
      ['activity', 'activity_or_abundance']
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run: `results/runs/20260812T212050Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Cell | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R463 | GOF decreased | 22 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 9.09% |
| R464 | GOF increased | 4 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R465 | LOF increased | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R466 | LOF decreased | 6 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |

R463's training pairs are erlotinib-lung cancer and gefitinib-lung cancer,
both through EGFR. Safe novel predictions include everolimus-Proteus syndrome;
quercetin-MTOR-pathway overgrowth; and erlotinib, imatinib, gefitinib, or
sorafenib for multiple GOF kinase developmental syndromes. Some of these may
be mechanistically plausible but developmentally irreversible, so mechanism
does not imply clinical feasibility.

R465's only predictions are methotrexate-cerebral folate transport deficiency
and imatinib-piebaldism. Both conflict with the asserted restoration story and
R465-R466 are rejected.

Compared with the older GOF kinase rule R372, R463 has 20 intersection pairs,
two child-only pairs, and removes 407 pairs. R372 has 9/427 training precision
(2.11%); R463 has 2/22 (9.09%). Both R463 training hits are in the
intersection. The new SemMed activity-disruption condition therefore acts as
a threshold-free precision filter rather than merely adding unsupported
predictions.

R463 remains a high-mechanism candidate as a distinct rule variant. It has no
DRH validation hit, so the precision gain remains training-calibrated rather
than independently validated.

## Batch 106: LOF enzyme-substrate negative evidence

Hypothesis: administering a drug that is a substrate of an explicit LOF
disease driver could be adverse in substrate-handling disorders and might
serve as a veto on otherwise positive LOF rules.

`HAS_SUBSTRATE` has 2,095 edges: 2,091 from ChEMBL and two each from
DrugCentral and Wombat-PK. There are 837 Gene-to-Drug edges over 145 genes and
363 drug-labeled chemicals. G2P variant joining yields 19 LOF pairs and zero
GOF pairs.

Executable diagnostic R467:

```cypher
MATCH (driver:Gene)-[substrate:HAS_SUBSTRATE]->(subject:Drug)
MATCH (driver)-[association:ASSOCIATED_WITH]->(object:Disease)
WHERE 'infores:chembl' IN coalesce(substrate.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:gene2phenotype' IN
      coalesce(association.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND association.subject_form_or_variant_qualifier =
      'loss_of_function_variant_form'
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run: `results/runs/20260812T212439Z/summary.json`

R467 produced 19 pairs with zero training, zero DRH, and zero contraindication
hits. Safe examples show a Biolink category problem rather than a clean drug
safety signal:

- glycine for glycine receptor/transport disorders;
- creatine for creatine transporter deficiency;
- glucose for GLUT1/GLUT2 deficiencies;
- dopamine for dopamine transporter deficiency;
- carnitine-related chemicals and transporter substrates for primary
  carnitine deficiency;
- unrelated transported drugs for UGT1A1 and ABCB11 deficiencies.

R467 overlaps broad direct LOF restoration R299 in only one pair:
dopamine-classic dopamine transporter deficiency. The other 18 substrate
pairs would veto no R299 candidate, while R299 retains both of its training
hits and its one contraindication outside the overlap.

R467 is rejected as a treatment rule and as a practical veto. `Drug` includes
endogenous nutrients, neurotransmitters, and probes, while `HAS_SUBSTRATE`
does not state whether exposure is therapeutic, toxic, compensatory, or
merely transported. The graph needs intervention role, direction of flux,
concentration, and disease-specific substrate accumulation.

Tooling note: a pytest invocation using the nonexistent selector
`rule_catalog_compiles` matched no tests and exited with code 5. The targeted
new-family suite was rerun with valid selectors and passed three tests.

## Pareto checkpoint after Batch 106

The library contained 462 evaluated entries. Surfaces were unchanged:

| Surface | Rules |
|---|---:|
| Global visible | 32 |
| Global validation | 9 |
| Treatment-free visible | 16 |
| Treatment-free validation | 10 |

R459, R463, and rejected R467 were on none of the four surfaces. R463 remains
useful as a high-mechanism parameterized precision variant rather than a
Pareto entry.

## Batch 107: R463 target-effect aspect ablation

This was a bounded semantic ablation, not a numeric threshold search. The
union R463 permits exact `activity` and `activity_or_abundance`; R468 and R469
separate those aspects.

Run: `results/runs/20260812T212736Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Aspect | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R463 | union | 22 | 2 | 0 | 9.09% |
| R468 | exact activity | 20 | 2 | 0 | 10.00% |
| R469 | activity or abundance | 21 | 2 | 0 | 9.52% |

All three variants have identical visible recall because they retain the same
erlotinib/gefitinib lung-cancer hits. R468 therefore dominates both broader
variants on observed precision and recall. R468 remains the candidate;
R463 and R469 are superseded but retained as executable parameterized
variants.

The first Ruff pass after adding the aspect modes reported four `ISC004`
warnings for unparenthesized implicit string concatenation inside the mode
mapping. Rule compilation and evaluation were correct. The strings were
parenthesized and Ruff was rerun.

## Batch 108: Common-disease pathway disruption blocker

Hypothesis: extend R404 by requiring the drug to disrupt the same GO process
that GeneticsKP associates with the disease and GOA links to the inhibited
top-three target.

The exact six-edge query was:

```cypher
MATCH (process:Pathway)-[path_evidence:GENETIC_ASSOCIATION]->
      (object:Disease)
MATCH (target:Gene)-[membership:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)
MATCH (target)-[gene_evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[disruption:DISRUPTS]->(process)
MATCH (subject)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
WHERE process.id STARTS WITH 'GO:'
  AND 'infores:geneticskp' IN
      coalesce(path_evidence.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:goa' IN coalesce(membership.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:semmeddb' IN
      coalesce(disruption.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND gene_evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Both decreased and increased target directions had zero pairs. Two bounded
ontology reconciliations were then tested:

- disrupted process `SUBCLASS_OF*0..2` the GeneticsKP/GOA process;
- GeneticsKP/GOA process `SUBCLASS_OF*0..2` the disrupted process.

Both directions also returned zero rows. No arbitrary depth or threshold was
searched and no rule was cataloged.

Graph limitation: SemMedDB process-disruption nodes and the GO
GeneticsKP/GOA process layer have no exact or two-hop ontology overlap for
R404 candidates. This mechanism requires process normalization or mappings
across source representations.

## Batch 109: Nonlinear independent process and target arms

The source audit found that SemMedDB disruption and GeneticsKP disease
association do share 109 exact biological-process nodes and yield 6,557
drug-disease pairs. The R404 extension failed because it additionally required
the inhibited target to be a member of that same process. R470 instead treats
process disruption and target inhibition as independent arms converging on
the disease.

Executable family:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[disruption:DISRUPTS]->
      (process:BiologicalProcess)-[path_evidence:GENETIC_ASSOCIATION]-
      (object:Disease)
MATCH (target:Gene)-[gene_evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
MATCH (subject)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
WHERE 'infores:semmeddb' IN
      coalesce(disruption.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:geneticskp' IN
      coalesce(path_evidence.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND gene_evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run: `results/runs/20260812T213158Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Contra | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R470 | target decreased | 21 | 9 | 0 | 0 | 42.86% |
| R471 | target increased | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |

All nine training hits are concentrated in two drugs: prednisolone for six
inflammatory/immune indications and infliximab for Crohn disease,
inflammatory bowel disease, and rheumatoid arthritis. The 12 safe novel
predictions include quercetin/curcumin-Alzheimer disease,
curcumin/sunitinib-Parkinson disease, prednisolone-type 1/type 2 diabetes,
infliximab-asthma/type 1 diabetes/multiple sclerosis, and dopamine-Lewy
bodies.

Compared with R404, R470 has 16 intersection pairs containing all nine R470
training hits, five R470-only predictions, and removes 29 R404 pairs. The
removed R404 set contains four training hits and two contraindications.
R470 is therefore a distinct nonlinear precision filter over the shared
target-inhibition core.

### Negative-evidence ablations

Within the fixed 21-pair R470 cohort:

- two pairs had direct contraindication, exacerbation, causation,
  contribution, or predisposition evidence;
- two disjoint pairs shared a drug side-effect phenotype with the disease;
- 17 pairs had neither signal;
- 11 pairs shared only adverse anatomy, which was rejected as too broad.

Run: `results/runs/20260812T213627Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Negative-evidence cell | Pairs | Training | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| R472 | no direct harm | 19 | 9 | 47.37% |
| R473 | direct harm present | 2 | 0 | 0% |
| R474 | no shared adverse phenotype | 19 | 8 | 42.11% |
| R475 | adverse phenotype present | 2 | 1 | 50.00% |
| R476 | neither strong harm channel | 17 | 8 | 47.06% |

R473 has one safe novel identity, dopamine-Lewy bodies, and one unresolved
blind identity that was not inspected. R475 consists of
prednisolone-EXCESS LMW-DNA as a training pair and prednisolone-type 1
diabetes as a safe novel pair. The phenotype veto therefore removes one
known treatment along with one plausible adverse prediction and does not
improve the rule.

An attempted raw exact-path audit over the full R470 cohort was blocked before
execution because the query could not prove that unresolved rows excluded
TTD-only identities. No rows were returned. Subsequent path inspection was
restricted to explicitly enumerated pairs already classified by the training
or novel inspector.

Exact paths for seven explicitly enumerated safe pairs showed:

- prednisolone-asthma used TNF inhibition plus disruption of lymphocyte
  activation;
- prednisolone-Crohn disease used TNF plus phagocytosis and lymphocyte
  activation;
- infliximab-rheumatoid arthritis and infliximab-multiple sclerosis used TNF
  plus immune response;
- prednisolone-type 1 diabetes used the same TNF/lymphocyte-activation motif
  as the known inflammatory indications;
- curcumin-Alzheimer disease used APP plus cell activation;
- dopamine-Lewy bodies used SNCA plus locomotion/insulin secretion and had a
  direct `CONTRIBUTES_TO` edge, so R472 removes it.

All inspected target effects came from the targeted text-mining provider.
Thus the two arms are source-distinct but not fully curated: the target arm is
targeted text mining, while process disruption is SemMedDB and disease-process
association is GeneticsKP.

R472 is retained as the surviving candidate. It dominates R470 at identical
visible recall and higher precision. R474 is rejected because phenotype
overlap removes a known treatment as often as a novel prediction. R476 is
superseded by R472 because it has lower precision and lower recall. The graph
still lacks sufficient negative evidence to reject infliximab-multiple
sclerosis or prednisolone-diabetes.

## Batch 110: Gene-interaction bridges between the target and process arms

Hypothesis: R472 should become more mechanistic and more precise when the
inhibited disease target interacts with a second gene that GOA places in the
independently disrupted, GeneticsKP-associated biological process.

The executable family is:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[disruption:DISRUPTS]->
      (process:BiologicalProcess)-[path_evidence:GENETIC_ASSOCIATION]-
      (object:Disease)
MATCH (partner:Gene)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)
MATCH (target:Gene)-[bridge]-(partner)
MATCH (target)-[gene_evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
MATCH (subject)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
WHERE type(bridge) = $bridge_predicate
  AND partner <> target
  AND 'infores:semmeddb' IN
      coalesce(disruption.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:geneticskp' IN
      coalesce(path_evidence.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND gene_evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(bridge.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND NOT EXISTS {
    MATCH (subject)-[:CONTRAINDICATED_IN|EXACERBATES_CONDITION|
                     CAUSES|CONTRIBUTES_TO|PREDISPOSES_TO_CONDITION]->
          (object)
  }
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run: `results/runs/20260812T214230Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Bridge evidence | Direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R477 | `DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH` / SIGNOR | decreased | 7 | 3 | 0 | 42.86% |
| R478 | same | increased control | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R479 | `INTERACTS_WITH` / SemMedDB | decreased | 9 | 5 | 0 | 55.56% |
| R480 | same | increased control | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R481 | `PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH` / IntAct | decreased | 11 | 3 | 0 | 27.27% |
| R482 | same | increased control | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |

R479 is a new treatment-free visible Pareto entry. The treatment-free visible
surface increased from 16 to 17 rules; the global visible, global validation,
and treatment-free validation surfaces remained 32, 9, and 10 rules.

Compared with R472, R479 retains nine pairs and removes ten. The retained set
contains five of R472's nine training hits and four of its ten unresolved
pairs. All four safely inspectable novel predictions are infliximab for asthma,
type 1 diabetes, multiple sclerosis, and EXCESS LMW-DNA. The five training
pairs are prednisolone for Crohn disease and EXCESS LMW-DNA, and infliximab for
Crohn disease, inflammatory bowel disease, and rheumatoid arthritis.

An exact-path audit was restricted to those nine explicitly enumerated,
already classified safe pairs. Its query was the executable family above with:

```cypher
UNWIND $pairs AS pair
MATCH (subject:Drug {id: pair.subject}) ...
MATCH (object:Disease {id: pair.object}) ...
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id, object.id, target.id, partner.id, process.id,
                bridge.primary_knowledge_sources,
                membership.primary_knowledge_sources,
                disruption.primary_knowledge_sources
```

The first audit command failed before graph execution because shell quoting
removed the Cypher string delimiters around `infores:semmeddb`, producing
`Variable infores not defined`. No rows were returned. The corrected command
escaped `$pairs` and preserved all Cypher literals.

The corrected audit found a coherent but highly concentrated module:

- all nine pairs inhibit TNF;
- every infliximab pair disrupts `GO:0006955` immune response;
- the SemMedDB interaction bridge connects TNF to CD4, CD28, FAS, IL4, STAT3,
  or TLR4, all of which GOA places in immune response;
- both prednisolone pairs disrupt `GO:0006909` phagocytosis and bridge TNF to
  MYD88 or TLR4;
- bridge and disruption evidence are both SemMedDB, while process membership
  is GOA and disease-process association is GeneticsKP.

Thus R479's precision improvement is real in the observed partition but is not
evidence of broad generalization. It is an immune/TNF module with repeated
literature evidence, not a generic interaction rule. R479 remains a
medium-mechanism candidate because it is Pareto-optimal and source-distinct at
the membership and disease-association layers. R477 and R481 remain
high-mechanism exploratory candidates for source-specific reruns; neither is
Pareto-optimal, and their narrow observed support prevents promotion. The
increased-target controls are rejected.

Graph limitation: the generic interaction predicate is unsigned and SemMedDB
supports both the drug-process disruption and target-partner bridge. A
directional regulatory bridge is needed to test whether inhibiting the target
should actually suppress the implicated process rather than merely co-occur
with its members.

## Batch 111: Signed regulatory bridge and downstream interception

Hypothesis: SIGNOR-qualified regulation can distinguish two causal topologies:

1. target `causes` process-member upregulation, so decreasing the target should
   suppress a component of the implicated process;
2. process member `causes` target upregulation, so decreasing the target may
   intercept a downstream process output without necessarily suppressing the
   process itself.

An aggregate-only bridge inventory over the blind-safe R472 cohort returned:

| Predicate | Pairs | Targets | Partners | Principal sources |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `AFFECTS` | 18 | 4 | 228 | targeted text, SemMedDB |
| `PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH` | 11 | 4 | 27 | IntAct |
| `REGULATES` | 10 | 2 | 9 | GO-CAM, SIGNOR |
| `COEXISTS_WITH` | 9 | 1 | 7 | SemMedDB |
| `INTERACTS_WITH` | 9 | 1 | 7 | SemMedDB |
| `PRODUCES` | 9 | 1 | 5 | SemMedDB |
| `DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH` | 7 | 1 | 1 | SIGNOR |
| `ORTHOLOGOUS_TO` | 7 | 1 | 2 | PANTHER |

The `REGULATES` audit found that SIGNOR preserved sign in edge qualifiers:
`qualified_predicate=biolink:causes` and
`object_direction_qualifier=upregulated`. Seven R472 pairs had target-to-member
orientation, while nine had member-to-target orientation. A GO-CAM edge covered
one pair but lacked sign and was not mixed into the signed family.

Executable family:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[disruption:DISRUPTS]->
      (process:BiologicalProcess)-[path_evidence:GENETIC_ASSOCIATION]-
      (object:Disease)
MATCH (partner:Gene)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(process)
MATCH (target:Gene)-[gene_evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
MATCH ($regulator)-[bridge:REGULATES]->($regulated)
MATCH (subject)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
WHERE partner <> target
  AND 'infores:semmeddb' IN
      coalesce(disruption.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:geneticskp' IN
      coalesce(path_evidence.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:signor' IN
      coalesce(bridge.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND bridge.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND bridge.object_direction_qualifier = 'upregulated'
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $drug_direction
  AND gene_evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND NOT EXISTS {
    MATCH (subject)-[:CONTRAINDICATED_IN|EXACERBATES_CONDITION|
                     CAUSES|CONTRIBUTES_TO|PREDISPOSES_TO_CONDITION]->
          (object)
  }
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

For R483/R484, `$regulator=target` and `$regulated=partner`. For R485,
`$regulator=partner` and `$regulated=target`.

Run: `results/runs/20260812T214816Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Topology | Drug direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R483 | target -> process member | decreased | 7 | 3 | 0 | 42.86% |
| R484 | target -> process member | increased | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| R485 | process member -> target | decreased | 9 | 5 | 0 | 55.56% |

The initially preferred R483 topology is directionally clean but contains only
infliximab: Crohn disease, inflammatory bowel disease, and rheumatoid
arthritis as training hits, plus asthma, type 1 diabetes, multiple sclerosis,
and EXCESS LMW-DNA as novel predictions. It remains a high-mechanism
exploratory candidate but is not Pareto-optimal.

R485 was initially entered as a topology control. Evaluation falsified that
interpretation: it is exactly set-equal to R479 and adds independent signed
SIGNOR evidence to every pair. It was therefore reinterpreted, before any
threshold tuning, as a downstream-interception mechanism. Exact paths over the
nine explicitly enumerated safe pairs showed:

- FCGR3A and IL1A cause increased TNF expression, and TLR8 causes increased TNF
  abundance, within the GO immune-response arm for every infliximab pair;
- ANXA1 causes increased TNF expression within the phagocytosis arm for both
  prednisolone pairs;
- the drug decreases TNF activity or abundance, intercepting the signed output
  downstream of those process members.

This does not prove that infliximab or prednisolone suppresses the whole
process. It supports a narrower claim that the drug blocks a positively driven
target downstream of a disease-associated process member. R485 is promoted to
a high-mechanism candidate. Set-identical unsigned R479 is marked superseded,
so the same prediction set is not counted twice as distinct success. R484 is
rejected.

Tooling failure: after compiler tests and Ruff passed, a redundant catalog
smoke command imported nonexistent module `miner2049er.rule_catalog` and
raised `ModuleNotFoundError` before loading the catalog. The authoritative
`miner-evaluate-rules` path then compiled and evaluated all three rules
successfully.

Graph limitation: this family still generalizes only one target, TNF, and two
processes. SIGNOR provides causal sign but not disease-specific context for the
regulatory edge. The observed precision cannot establish cross-target
generalization.

## Batch 112: Curated adverse-manifestation veto

Hypothesis: a positive molecular rule should be vetoed when graph-native
targeted harm evidence says the drug contributes to a curated manifestation of
the candidate disease, even when the graph lacks a direct drug-to-disease harm
edge.

### Provenance audit

R485's binding arm is independently supported. Seven pairs have each of DGIdb,
DrugCentral, and ChEMBL binding paths, and two also have BindingDB. All target
direction edges come from the targeted text-mining provider. Thus the rule is
not a single-source literature loop: binding, target direction, signed
regulation, process membership, process disruption, and disease-process
association draw from distinct graph sources, although the drug-direction edge
remains text-derived.

### Failed broad negative-evidence joins

The first adverse audit inspected the already disclosed safe drug infliximab.
It has many `CAUSES`, `CONTRIBUTES_TO`, `CONTRAINDICATED_IN`, and
`PREDISPOSES_TO_CONDITION` edges, including targeted-text contributions to
optic neuritis, retrobulbar neuritis, and transverse myelitis. The direct R472
harm veto misses these because none is the exact multiple-sclerosis node.

An unrestricted query joining any such adverse endpoint to any relation of the
candidate disease returned thousands of paths dominated by SemMedDB
`COEXISTS_WITH`. It connected nearly every inflammatory candidate to many
adverse nodes and was rejected before cataloging.

A stricter query required targeted `CONTRIBUTES_TO|CAUSES` harm and an exact
shared endpoint on disease `CAUSES|HAS_PHENOTYPE`:

```cypher
MATCH (subject)-[harm:CONTRIBUTES_TO|CAUSES]->(adverse)
WHERE 'infores:text-mining-provider-targeted' IN
      coalesce(harm.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND EXISTS {
    MATCH (object)-[:CAUSES|HAS_PHENOTYPE]->(adverse)
  }
```

This still fired on all nine R485 pairs, including all five known treatments.
Examples included ulcer disease for Crohn disease, pneumonia for asthma,
osteopenia for rheumatoid arthritis and type 1 diabetes, and optic neuritis
for multiple sclerosis. SemMed disease-to-manifestation edges are too broad
for a veto.

### Source-faithful adverse cell

The final predeclared restriction accepted only HPO annotations or explicit
manifestation semantics:

```cypher
EXISTS {
  MATCH (subject)-[harm:CONTRIBUTES_TO|CAUSES]->(adverse)
  WHERE 'infores:text-mining-provider-targeted' IN
        coalesce(harm.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    AND (
      EXISTS {
        MATCH (object)-[phenotype:HAS_PHENOTYPE]->(adverse)
        WHERE 'infores:hpo-annotations' IN
              coalesce(phenotype.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
      }
      OR EXISTS {
        MATCH (adverse)-[:MANIFESTATION_OF]->(object)
      }
    )
}
```

Across the nine already classified safe R485 pairs, this fired only for:

- infliximab-multiple sclerosis through optic neuritis;
- infliximab-EXCESS LMW-DNA through nine HPO-annotated manifestations;
- prednisolone-EXCESS LMW-DNA through six HPO-annotated manifestations.

R486 negates this clause; R487 requires it.

Run: `results/runs/20260812T215403Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Adverse cell | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R486 | curated manifestation absent | 6 | 4 | 0 | 66.67% |
| R487 | curated manifestation present | 3 | 1 | 0 | 33.33% |

R486 retains prednisolone-Crohn disease and infliximab-Crohn disease,
inflammatory bowel disease, and rheumatoid arthritis. Its two safe novel
predictions are infliximab-asthma and infliximab-type 1 diabetes. R487 contains
the known prednisolone-EXCESS LMW-DNA treatment and the two removed novel
infliximab predictions.

R486 and its parent R485 are both on the treatment-free visible Pareto surface:
R485 preserves more recall at 55.56% precision, while R486 trades one known
treatment and two predictions for 66.67% precision. The treatment-free visible
surface now has 15 entries because these new points dominate several earlier
rules. Global visible remains 32, global validation 9, and treatment-free
validation 10.

R486 is retained as a high-mechanism candidate and R487 is rejected as a
counter-mechanism cell. This is a non-numeric negative-evidence split; no
adverse-count cutoff, ontology depth, or endpoint-degree threshold was tuned.

Tooling failures: four blind-safe inspectors took roughly one minute because
the nested adverse checks are expensive but completed within the configured
limit. Two local Pareto helper commands failed before producing counts because
they first assumed the library root was a rule list and then assumed the
`results` field did not exist. The corrected helper used
`library['results']`.

Graph limitation: targeted harm edges are dense and often reflect indication,
comorbidity, or adverse context without explicit polarity. Only the combination
of targeted harm and curated HPO/manifestation semantics was selective. Even
then, one known treatment is removed, so the veto is useful probabilistically,
not as absolute proof of non-treatment.

## Batch 113: One-edge GO ontology reconciliation

Hypothesis: the exact-process join in R485 is unnecessarily brittle when a
drug disrupts a broader GO process and GeneticsKP associates the disease with a
direct child process. A single asserted Ubergraph `SUBCLASS_OF` edge can
reconcile source granularity without an arbitrary transitive search.

Two directions were tested before cataloging:

```cypher
// Direction A: disrupted child under disease-associated parent
MATCH (subject)-[:DISRUPTS]->(disrupted)
      -[:SUBCLASS_OF]->(disease_process)
      -[:GENETIC_ASSOCIATION]-(object)

// Direction B: disease-associated child under disrupted parent
MATCH (subject)-[:DISRUPTS]->(broader_process)
      <-[:SUBCLASS_OF]-(disease_process)
      -[:GENETIC_ASSOCIATION]-(object)
```

Both retained the signed process-member-to-target interception arm and all
R485 exclusions. Direction A returned zero pairs. Direction B returned 17
pairs over 12 direct GO links and three inhibited targets, so only Direction B
was cataloged.

Executable R488 query:

```cypher
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[disruption:DISRUPTS]->
      (broader_process:BiologicalProcess)
      <-[ontology:SUBCLASS_OF]-(disease_process:BiologicalProcess)
      -[path_evidence:GENETIC_ASSOCIATION]-(object:Disease)
MATCH (partner:Gene)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(disease_process)
MATCH (target:Gene)-[gene_evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->(object)
MATCH (partner)-[bridge:REGULATES]->(target)
MATCH (subject)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
WHERE broader_process <> disease_process
  AND partner <> target
  AND 'infores:semmeddb' IN
      coalesce(disruption.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:geneticskp' IN
      coalesce(path_evidence.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:ubergraph' IN
      coalesce(ontology.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:signor' IN
      coalesce(bridge.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND bridge.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND bridge.object_direction_qualifier = 'upregulated'
  AND effect.qualified_predicate = 'biolink:causes'
  AND effect.object_aspect_qualifier = 'activity_or_abundance'
  AND effect.object_direction_qualifier = $direction
  AND gene_evidence._miner_disease_rank <= 3
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(binding.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND none(
    source IN coalesce(effect.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
    WHERE source IN ['infores:drug-repurposing-hub', 'infores:ttd']
  )
  AND NOT EXISTS {
    MATCH (subject)-[:CONTRAINDICATED_IN|EXACERBATES_CONDITION|
                     CAUSES|CONTRIBUTES_TO|PREDISPOSES_TO_CONDITION]->
          (object)
  }
  AND coalesce(object._miner_disease_descendant_degree, 0) <= 500
  AND object._miner_disease_superclass_degree >= 1
  AND object.id STARTS WITH 'MONDO:'
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

Run: `results/runs/20260812T215826Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Target direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R488 | decreased | 17 | 9 | 0 | 52.94% |
| R489 | increased control | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |

R488 contains nine training pairs:

- prednisolone for asthma, Crohn disease, inflammatory bowel disease, multiple
  sclerosis, EXCESS LMW-DNA, and rheumatoid arthritis;
- infliximab for Crohn disease, inflammatory bowel disease, and rheumatoid
  arthritis.

Its eight safe novel predictions are quercetin-Alzheimer disease,
curcumin-Alzheimer disease, curcumin-Parkinson disease,
prednisolone-type 1/type 2 diabetes, and infliximab-type 1 diabetes,
multiple sclerosis, and EXCESS LMW-DNA.

Compared with R485, R488 has eight intersection pairs, nine R488-only pairs,
and one R485-only pair. The R488-only cell has four training hits and five
novel predictions, so the ontology generalization adds recall at 44.44%
precision rather than merely duplicating the exact-process rule.

Exact-path audit was limited to the 17 explicitly enumerated, already
classified safe pairs. It found three target modules:

- APP, rank 1 for Alzheimer disease: curcumin disrupts cell activation while
  B-cell differentiation or natural-killer-cell activation contains ADAM17 or
  FYN that upregulates APP; quercetin disrupts cell migration while leukocyte
  migration contains FYN;
- SNCA, rank 2 for Parkinson disease: curcumin disrupts phosphorylation while
  protein autophosphorylation contains DAPK1 or DYRK1A that upregulates SNCA;
- TNF: infliximab disrupts immune response while innate immune response
  contains IRF5 or TLR8; prednisolone disrupts lymphocyte activation or
  phagocytosis while direct child processes contain ADAM17, ANXA1, FCGR3A, or
  related regulators that upregulate TNF.

R488 is a new treatment-free visible Pareto entry, increasing that surface
from 15 to 16. Global visible remains 32, global validation 9, and
treatment-free validation 10. R488 is retained as a high-mechanism candidate;
R489 is rejected.

Tooling failure: the first Ruff pass found only an unsorted test import for the
new compiler function. The import was reordered, after which Ruff and the
targeted compiler tests passed.

Graph limitation: one GO parent edge improves identifier granularity but can
also broaden semantics substantially. The APP paths connect Alzheimer disease
to immune-cell subclasses and the SNCA path connects Parkinson disease to
autophosphorylation. These are biologically possible but require tissue or
cell-context corroboration before being considered strongly predictive.

## Batch 114: Tissue localization of ontology-generalized interception

Hypothesis: R488's APP, SNCA, and TNF modules should be more credible when the
inhibited target and its signed regulator are both Bgee-expressed in the exact
anatomy where the disease occurs.

Two aggregate queries were run before cataloging:

- target expressed in exact disease anatomy: five pairs, three targets, 26
  anatomies;
- target and regulator both expressed in that anatomy: the same five pairs,
  three targets, eight regulators, and 26 anatomies.

Because regulator coexpression removed no pair, the stronger shared-expression
form was cataloged:

```cypher
// R488 positive motif above
MATCH (target)-[target_expression:EXPRESSED_IN]->
```
