```cypher
      (anatomy:AnatomicalEntity)
MATCH (partner)-[partner_expression:EXPRESSED_IN]->(anatomy)
MATCH (object)-[:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->(anatomy)
WHERE 'infores:bgee' IN
      coalesce(target_expression.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:bgee' IN
      coalesce(partner_expression.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
```

Run: `results/runs/20260812T220150Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Target direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R490 | decreased | 5 | 2 | 0 | 40.00% |
| R491 | increased control | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |

R490's two training hits are prednisolone-rheumatoid arthritis and
infliximab-rheumatoid arthritis. Its three safe novel predictions are
quercetin-Alzheimer disease, curcumin-Alzheimer disease, and
curcumin-Parkinson disease. It removes seven of R488's nine known treatments
while retaining three speculative predictions, so tissue localization lowers
both precision and recall.

Exact anatomy audit over the five explicitly enumerated safe pairs showed:

- rheumatoid arthritis localizes the TNF-regulator paths to bone marrow only;
- Alzheimer disease matches APP and its regulators in 21 anatomies, including
  several brain regions but also blood, heart, skeletal muscle, optic nerve,
  and the generic `adult organism`;
- Parkinson disease matches SNCA-regulator paths in 7 to 15 anatomies,
  including relevant brain regions but also heart, spinal cord, and
  `adult organism`.

Thus the rheumatoid-arthritis context is coherent, but the neurodegenerative
contexts survive because broad disease-location and ubiquitous-expression
edges create many matches. R490 is rejected despite its high qualitative
mechanism because it is not predictive; R491 is rejected as the direction
control.

Tooling failure: an unrelated `.venv/bin/neo4j-admin --help` probe was
accidentally issued while the inspectors ran. The project environment has no
such binary, so it exited immediately with `no such file or directory` and did
not touch Neo4j or campaign artifacts.

Graph limitation: exact shared anatomy identity is not sufficient when disease
location includes generic organism and organ nodes and Bgee expression is
broad. A useful tissue rule needs context-specific expression, cell type,
quantitative enrichment, or a curated disease-site assertion rather than
presence alone.

## Batch 115: Cell-context blocker

Hypothesis: cell-level context may be more discriminating than the failed
anatomy-presence rule, especially for R488's immune-process subclasses.

Global relation inventory found:

- 1,667 disease-to-cell `LOCATED_IN` edges over 506 diseases and 273 cells,
  all from SemMedDB;
- 217,441 gene-to-cell `EXPRESSED_IN` edges over 39,716 genes and 41 cells,
  all from Bgee;
- 5,476 process-to-cell `LOCATED_IN` and 866 process-to-cell `OCCURS_IN`
  edges, all from SemMedDB.

The independent gene-expression source made an exact-cell test reasonable.
Two predeclared queries were run:

```cypher
// Shared target/regulator expression in a disease-located cell
MATCH (target)-[te:EXPRESSED_IN]->(cell:Cell)
      <-[pe:EXPRESSED_IN]-(partner)
MATCH (object)-[disease_cell:LOCATED_IN]->(cell)
WHERE 'infores:bgee' IN coalesce(te.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:bgee' IN coalesce(pe.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
  AND 'infores:semmeddb' IN
      coalesce(disease_cell.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
```

The stricter form additionally required:

```cypher
MATCH (disease_process)-[process_cell:LOCATED_IN|OCCURS_IN]->(cell)
WHERE 'infores:semmeddb' IN
      coalesce(process_cell.primary_knowledge_sources, [])
```

Both returned zero R488 pairs. No cell-degree threshold, ontology
generalization, or cell blacklist was searched, and no rules were cataloged.

Graph limitation: Bgee uses only 41 cell nodes in this snapshot, while
disease/process cell context is SemMedDB-derived and uses a broader cell
vocabulary. Exact identifiers do not bridge these layers. Cell ontology
normalization or ancestry-aware mapping is needed before cell localization can
corroborate the rule.

## Batch 116: Dual-process convergence paused at wrap-up

Hypothesis: a non-linear R488 rule should be stronger when two distinct
drug-disrupted parent processes independently contain disease-associated child
processes whose members positively regulate the same inhibited disease target.

The first executable form self-joined two complete ontology-interception
branches:

```cypher
MATCH (target:Gene)-[gene_evidence:MINER_GENE_DISEASE_EVIDENCE]->
      (object:Disease)
MATCH (subject:Drug)-[binding:DIRECTLY_PHYSICALLY_INTERACTS_WITH]->(target)
MATCH (subject)-[effect:AFFECTS]->(target)
MATCH (subject)-[d1:DISRUPTS]->(b1:BiologicalProcess)
      <-[o1:SUBCLASS_OF]-(c1:BiologicalProcess)
      -[p1:GENETIC_ASSOCIATION]-(object)
MATCH (r1:Gene)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(c1)
MATCH (r1)-[g1:REGULATES]->(target)
MATCH (subject)-[d2:DISRUPTS]->(b2:BiologicalProcess)
      <-[o2:SUBCLASS_OF]-(c2:BiologicalProcess)
      -[p2:GENETIC_ASSOCIATION]-(object)
MATCH (r2:Gene)-[:INVOLVED_IN]->(c2)
MATCH (r2)-[g2:REGULATES]->(target)
WHERE b1.id < b2.id
  AND r1 <> target
  AND r2 <> target
  // same source, sign, target-direction, rank, harm, and disease constraints
  // as R488 on both branches
RETURN count(DISTINCT subject.id + '|' + object.id) AS pairs,
       count(DISTINCT target.id) AS targets,
       count(DISTINCT b1.id + '|' + b2.id) AS parent_pairs,
       count(DISTINCT c1.id + '|' + c2.id) AS child_pairs
```

The aggregate produced no output after more than 60 seconds. At the user's
wrap-up instruction it was interrupted twice; the second interrupt terminated
the client with `KeyboardInterrupt` and exit code 130. No rows were returned,
no rule was cataloged, and process inspection confirmed no campaign query
process remained.

The hypothesis is unfinished rather than rejected. On resume, replace the
quadratic self-join with one R488 branch followed by:

```cypher
WITH subject, object, target,
     count(DISTINCT broader_process) AS parent_process_count
WHERE parent_process_count >= 2
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

This is equivalent for pair discovery and should avoid branch cross-product
materialization. Add an increased-target control only if the grouped aggregate
is nonzero.

## Pause checkpoint

Exploration paused at the user's explicit time-limit instruction. This is not
the requested final deadline rerun, and the campaign goal remains incomplete.

- R485 signed process-to-target downstream interception: 9 pairs, 5 training,
  55.56%, treatment-free Pareto;
- R486 curated adverse-manifestation-gated interception: 6 pairs, 4 training,
  66.67%, treatment-free Pareto;
- R488 one-edge GO ontology interception: 17 pairs, 9 training, 52.94%,
  treatment-free Pareto.

R490 tissue localization was changed from `candidate` to `rejected` after its
40% result and broad-anatomy audit. The catalog is authoritative. The JSON
library still contains the pre-change R490 `library_status` until the next
evaluator invocation refreshes all catalog metadata.

Last recomputed surfaces before that non-Pareto status change:

- global visible: 32;
- global validation: 9;
- treatment-free visible: 16;
- treatment-free validation: 10.

Resume sequence:

1. run any cheap rule evaluation to refresh catalog statuses in
   `results/rule-library.json`;
2. run the grouped dual-process convergence query above;
3. if nonzero, catalog corrective and increased-target control cells;
4. continue with a genuinely different family rather than additional numeric
   threshold tuning;
5. at the actual final deadline, rerun all surviving candidates, recompute all
   surfaces, and produce the requested family/variant/failure/prediction/gap
   report without inspecting TTD-only identities.

## Batch 117: Resumed dual-process ontology convergence

The checkpointed Batch 116 hypothesis was resumed with a linear grouped query
instead of the interrupted quadratic self-join:

```cypher
// Full R488 branch
WITH subject, object, target,
     count(DISTINCT broader_process) AS parent_process_count
WHERE parent_process_count >= 2
RETURN DISTINCT subject.id AS subject_id, object.id AS object_id
```

The aggregate completed in 2.76 seconds and returned two pairs, one target, and
exactly two distinct parent processes per pair. R492 used decreased target
activity or abundance; R493 was the increased-target control.

Run: `results/runs/20260812T221116Z/summary.json`

| Rule | Direction | Pairs | Training | DRH | Novel | Visible precision |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| R492 | decreased | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 100.00% |
| R493 | increased | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0% |

The two R492 pairs are prednisolone-asthma and prednisolone-Crohn disease.
Exact safe-path audit showed the same TNF convergence in both:

- lymphocyte activation with natural-killer-cell activation, alpha-beta T-cell
  differentiation, B-cell differentiation, and T-cell differentiation in the
  thymus; regulators were FCGR3A, ANXA1, and ADAM17;
- phagocytosis with neutrophil clearance; regulator was ANXA1;
- all signed branches upregulate TNF expression or abundance, which
  prednisolone decreases.

R492 temporarily appeared on both visible Pareto surfaces because it has 100%
observed precision. It has no novel prediction, however, and therefore fails
the explicit campaign utility criterion that perfect precision without
prediction is not useful. R492 is rejected as a diagnostic mechanistic pattern,
not retained as a surviving predictive rule. R493 is rejected as the matched
control.

Implementation note: the grouped query is equivalent for pair discovery and
avoids materializing the branch cross product. Targeted compiler tests passed
and Ruff was clean.

Graph limitation: dual-process convergence is present only in one
prednisolone-TNF inflammatory module. The graph does not currently provide
enough distinct process coverage for this structural requirement to
generalize to an unlabeled pair.
